# Architecture

Code map for orienting quickly in the project. One section per system; keep headings stable and update the section that matches the code you change. Conventions and rules live in [CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md); this file describes what exists.

All C# types are in the global namespace (no `namespace` declarations). Paths below are relative to this file.

## Overview

A Unity 6 (URP 2D) top-down action RPG prototype. The player boots into a main menu, starts a new game, and is placed in a seamless world, a grid of chunk scenes streamed around the players (Testlands, the hand-painted test world, at its `start` entry), where they walk in 8 directions and swing a sword. Weasel mobs idle, patrol around their spawn, spot the player with line-of-sight checks, path around walls on their world's navigation, crowd around the player without overlapping, and melee attack on a cooldown. Damage shows floating numbers and a HUD health bar. Mobs target whichever player of their world they spot (nearest visible). A dead player respawns on its own after a delay at the nearest waystone it discovered in its world, whatever the other players do: nothing restarts when every player is dead. Offline and online play (host and join) run in seamless worlds, each player in its own world and travelling between worlds through exits (see Networking and Worlds and streaming). A dev-only stress world spawns hundreds of mobs on a procedurally generated map to profile AI and pathfinding.

## Packages

Notable entries in [Packages/manifest.json](../Packages/manifest.json):

| Package | Used for |
|---|---|
| `jp.hadashikick.vcontainer` (git, pinned to tag `1.19.0`) | Dependency injection, `LifetimeScope`s, entry points |
| `com.unity.inputsystem` | `InputSystem_Actions.inputactions`, `PlayerInputService`, `InputBindings` (rebinding), UI input module |
| `com.unity.render-pipelines.universal` | URP 2D renderer, `Light2D` |
| `com.unity.2d.tilemap` / `.extras` | Tilemaps for terrain, collision and navigation data; `RuleTile` base of the dual-grid walls tile |
| `com.unity.2d.aseprite`, `com.unity.2d.animation` | Sprite import and animation |
| `com.unity.ugui` + TextMesh Pro | Menu, HUD, damage popups |
| `com.unity.test-framework` | EditMode and PlayMode tests |
| `com.unity.netcode.gameobjects` 2.13.3 | Netcode for GameObjects: `NetworkManager`, host/client sessions, network prefab handlers |
| `com.unity.transport` 2.6.0 | `UnityTransport`. Pinned to the version Netcode declares: 2.7.x throws a Burst `NullReferenceException` from its `AnalyticsLayer` job every network update in this editor version. |
| `com.unity.multiplayer.playmode` 2.0.2 | Multiplayer Play Mode (up to 3 extra virtual players in the editor) |
| `com.unity.multiplayer.tools` 2.2.12 | Network Simulator (latency, jitter, packet loss) and network profiling. Simulation only runs in the editor and development builds. |
| `com.coplaydev.unity-mcp` (git) | UnityMCP editor automation |

## Assemblies

| Assembly | Folder | References | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `TopDownRPG.Core` | `Assets/Scripts/Core` | VContainer, Unity.InputSystem, UnityEngine.UI (for the `EventSystem` pointer-over-UI check), Unity.Netcode.Runtime, Unity.Networking.Transport | Boot, scene flow, input, clock, randomness, network session, camera follow, screen fader. Must not reference Gameplay. |
| `TopDownRPG.Gameplay` | `Assets/Scripts` (everything outside `Core` and `Dev`) | Core, VContainer, Unity.InputSystem, Unity.TextMeshPro, Unity.Netcode.Runtime, Unity.2D.Tilemap.Extras | AI, navigation, combat, player, UI, effects, world (including the dual-grid walls and the blended terrain renderer), composition roots. |
| `TopDownRPG.DevTools` | `Assets/Scripts/Dev` | Gameplay, Core, VContainer, Unity.2D.Tilemap.Extras | `defineConstraints: UNITY_EDITOR \|\| DEVELOPMENT_BUILD`; not auto-referenced. Its editor-only code sits behind `#if UNITY_EDITOR`. |
| `TopDownRPG.Editor` | `Assets/Editor` | Core, Gameplay, VContainer, Unity.2D.Tilemap.Editor, Unity.2D.Tilemap.Extras, Unity.2D.Tilemap.Extras.Editor, Unity.2D.Sprite.Editor (slicing the walls sheet), Unity.RenderPipelines.Universal.2D.Runtime (the stress scene's Global Light 2D) | Editor-only platform. Includes the dual-grid tooling, the terrain brush and material tools, the walls sheet generator, the prop placement tool and the scene generator. |
| `TopDownRPG.Editor.Testing` | `Assets/Editor/Testing` | UnityEditor.TestRunner, UnityEngine.TestRunner | Editor-only, not auto-referenced: `ProjectTestRun`, the test runner for tools that drive the editor (see Tests). |
| `TopDownRPG.EditModeTests` | `Assets/Tests/Editor` | Gameplay, Core, Editor (for the prop snapping rule, the scene generator and the terrain tools), DevTools (for the stress map), VContainer, Unity.InputSystem, Unity.InputSystem.TestFramework, Unity.TextMeshPro, Unity.Netcode.Runtime, Unity.2D.Tilemap.Extras, Unity.2D.Tilemap.Editor (for the grid brushes) | Editor-only test assembly. |
| `TopDownRPG.PlayModeTests` | `Assets/Tests/PlayMode` | Gameplay, Core, DevTools, VContainer, Unity.InputSystem, Unity.TextMeshPro, Unity.Netcode.Runtime | Needs DevTools for the stress-scene test. |

Dependency direction: `Core <- Gameplay <- DevTools`, with `Editor` and both test assemblies on top. Core and Gameplay each have an `AssemblyInfo.cs` ([Core](../Assets/Scripts/Core/AssemblyInfo.cs), [Gameplay](../Assets/Scripts/AssemblyInfo.cs)) granting `InternalsVisibleTo` to both test assemblies; `internal` members are test seams. Gameplay also grants it to `TopDownRPG.Editor`, whose dual-grid tools set `internal` members of the dual-grid types and whose terrain brush calls the terrain renderer's `internal` preview API. Editor's own [AssemblyInfo.cs](../Assets/Editor/AssemblyInfo.cs) grants it to the EditMode tests.

## Scenes and boot flow

### Scenes

| Scene | Role | Key contents |
|---|---|---|
| [Main.unity](../Assets/Scenes/Main.unity) | Persistent root, never unloaded. First scene in the build. | `MainLifetimeScope`, Main Camera (`CameraFollow2D`), `EventSystem`, `TransitionCanvas/Fade` (`ScreenFader`) |
| [MainMenu.unity](../Assets/Scenes/MainMenu.unity) | Title screen, loaded additively. | `MenuLifetimeScope`, `MainMenuCanvas` with `MainMenuController`; `Buttons` holds Continue, New Game, Host Game, the join address field, Join Game, Options, Quit and a status line; an `OptionsMenu` prefab instance draws over them while open |
| [Gameplay.unity](../Assets/Scenes/Gameplay.unity) | Session scene, loaded additively for a play session. | `GameplayLifetimeScope` (references the `Player` prefab, spawned at runtime), `PlayerHudCanvas` prefab, `DamagePopupCanvas` (`DamagePopupLayer`), `GameMenuCanvas` prefab (in-game menu and options screen, sort order 10000: above the popups, below the fader) |
| [Worlds/Sandbox/World_Sandbox.unity](../Assets/Worlds/Sandbox/World_Sandbox.unity) and its four chunks under `Chunks/` | The Sandbox, a seamless test world of 2 x 2 chunks at origin (2048, 0), listed in `GameScenes.worlds` and the build; nothing in the menu leads to it, so it is played by opening one of its scenes and pressing Play (see Worlds and streaming). | Root scene: `WorldLifetimeScope`, `Lighting` (`WorldLighting`) holding a white Global Light 2D at 0.85. Each chunk scene: `ChunkLifetimeScope`, a `Grid` at the world's origin with its `WorldChunk`, a `TerrainBlendRenderer` drawing the chunk's ground layers (each list covering its neighbours' border materials) and a `NavigationGrid2D` its navigation page is built from (blocked by the walls and the `Obstacles` layer, reading the cliff of its raised tilemap; `WorldAuthoring.SetUpNavigation` gave every chunk of both test worlds theirs), the walls' `CollisionTilemap` (copied from the area template), and `MobSpawns` holding three `MobSpawnPoint`s of the Weasel (nothing spawns them yet). Painted by script: grass over all 128 x 128 cells, a stone road across the south (rows 40 to 43), a gravel trail up the east (columns 90 and 91), a pond of water in the north-west with a sandy shore, a patch of dirt over the corner where the four chunks meet, a meadow across the border at row 64 in the west and tall grass across the border at column 64 in the north; walls: a line along row 20 and a block across the border at column 64 in the south, a ring around the corner where the four chunks meet and a 2 x 2 block on that corner; a plateau of raised ground (columns 100 to 118, rows 52 to 76) with a ramp in its southern edge across the border at row 64 (WorldStreaming.md's Phase 2); entry `start` (a waystone) on the road at (30.5, 42), and entry `fromInterior` at (33.5, 42), where the interior's exit leads, two and a half cells west of the world exit `Exit_SandboxInterior` (under `Exits` in chunk (0, 0): cells (36, 41) and (36, 42) of the road, leading to the SandboxInterior's `start`); entry `fromTestlands` at (5.5, 42), two and a half cells east of the world exit `Exit_Testlands` (cells (3, 41) and (3, 42) of the road, leading to Testlands' `fromSandbox`). Every chunk has an `AreaTerrain` (its grass layer and walls) |
| [Worlds/SandboxInterior/World_SandboxInterior.unity](../Assets/Worlds/SandboxInterior/World_SandboxInterior.unity) and its chunk | A one-chunk test world at origin (4096, 0), standing in for an interior; listed and built like the Sandbox. | Set up like the Sandbox: a 24 x 16 room of dirt with a square of plaza in its middle, its grass layer empty (the room covers cells (20, 24) to (43, 39)); entry `start` at (32, 32), and the world exit `Exit_Sandbox` on the room's bottom row (cells (31, 24) and (32, 24)), leading back to the Sandbox's `fromInterior` |
| [Worlds/Testlands/World_Testlands.unity](../Assets/Worlds/Testlands/World_Testlands.unity) and its four chunks under `Chunks/` | Testlands, the hand-painted test world of 2 x 2 chunks at origin (6144, 0), the first free slot of the layout when it was made; listed in `GameScenes.worlds` and the build, and `GameScenes`' starting world: New Game and Host Game start at its `start`, and so does every player the host does not know. The PlayMode tests that need a session boot into it (`SceneBootTestHelper.BootIntoTestlands`). | Made with the World Editor's steps (`WorldAuthoring.CreateWorld` and `CreateChunk`, `ListInGameScenes`) and painted with the Terrain Brush's strokes, each written across the chunk borders it crossed (WorldStreaming.md's Phase 5): grass over all 128 x 128 cells, a stone road across the world (rows 38 to 41) crossing the border at column 64, a gravel trail up the east (columns 88 and 89, rows 42 to 127) crossing the border at row 64, a meadow across row 64 in the west, tall grass across column 64 in the south, a patch of dirt on the corner where the four chunks meet, and a pond of water in a sandy shore across column 64 in the north; walls (`WorldAuthoring.AddWalls` on chunk (0, 0), then the brush): a line along row 24 from column 56 to 72 across the border and a short spur up column 56; a plateau of raised ground (columns 100 to 115, rows 56 to 72) across the border at row 64 with a ramp in its southern edge (columns 106 to 108). Each chunk's `MobSpawns` holds two Weasel `MobSpawnPoint`s with their `PersistentId`, all at least 23 cells from the entry. Entry `start` (a waystone) on the road at (24.5, 40.5): every cell within 6 of it is open ground, with room for the seven-cell wall `PlayerControlsPlayModeTests` builds two cells to its right (Docs/SceneGeneration.md's "What the PlayMode tests rely on"). Entry `fromSandbox` at (117.5, 40), two and a half cells west of the world exit `Exit_Sandbox` (under `Exits` in chunk (1, 0): cells (120, 39) and (120, 40) of the road, leading to the Sandbox's `fromTestlands`), so a hosted game reaches the Sandbox and its interior. Entry `north` (a waystone) on the grass of chunk (0, 1) at (30.5, 90.5), 50 cells from `start` and 19 from the nearest Weasel spawn point, for the respawn tests and the Multiplayer Play Mode steps (WorldStreaming.md's Phase 9). Every chunk has an `AreaTerrain` (its grass layer, and its walls where it has them). No sea, so no coast maps. |
| [Worlds/Overworld/World_Overworld.unity](../Assets/Worlds/Overworld/World_Overworld.unity) and its twenty chunks under `Chunks/` | The Overworld, the first generated world ([WorldGeneration.md](WorldGeneration.md)), 5 x 4 chunks at origin (8192, 0), the first free slot of the layout when it was made; listed in `GameScenes.worlds` and the build, but not yet the starting world (WorldGeneration.md's Phase 5). Six regions, the six area generators' seed 1 layouts, on an island in the sea, joined by stone and gravel roads through the wilds' grass; a waystone per region on a plaza at its spawn cell, `clearing` the default entry, and a gate on each linked port. | Made with the World Editor's steps (`WorldAuthoring.CreateWorld`, `CreateChunk` for every chunk, `ListInGameScenes`) and written through the World Generator's code path (`WorldGeneratorSession`) from `WorldGenerator_Overworld.asset`, then synced, baked, repaired and validated: every chunk 5 to 14 layers in the style's order, all eighteen materials laid, the Coast region's plateau of raised ground with its ramp, 48 spawn points of the Weasel under the chunk grids' `GeneratedMobSpawns` (28 in the regions, 20 in the wilds) with derived ids, 14 baked coast maps beside the chunk scenes, no walls; 19 entries, the six waystones then the 13 gates. Regenerate it from its generator asset only (WorldGeneration.md: Generating a world), locking a chunk there before painting it by hand. |
| [Dev/Worlds/StressTest/World_StressTest.unity](../Assets/Dev/Worlds/StressTest/World_StressTest.unity) and its four chunks under `Chunks/` | The stress world, a dev-only world of 2 x 2 chunks at origin (0, 0), the first free slot of the layout when it was made. Not listed in `GameScenes` and not in the build list: its `SceneDefinition`s lie under `Assets/Dev/`, which `SceneBuildValidator` skips and `WorldAuthoring` keeps out of the build. Played by opening one of its scenes and pressing Play. | Made with the World Editor's steps (`WorldAuthoring.CreateWorld` into `Assets/Dev/Worlds`, `CreateChunk`, `AddWalls`). Root scene: `WorldLifetimeScope`, whose auto-inject objects list `StressTest` (at the world's middle, (64.5, 64.5)) holding `StressTestSpawner` and `StressTestOverlay`, and the lighting. Each chunk's grid has an empty `GrassTilemap` layer, walls and a `StressTestMap`, which paints the chunk's part of the 128 x 128 map at runtime. Entry `start` (a waystone) at the world's middle. No mob spawn points: the spawner makes the mobs. |

Chunks get their walls from [Walls.prefab](../Assets/Prefabs/World/Walls.prefab) (`WorldAuthoring.AddWalls`, the World Editor's Add walls): an empty `CollisionTilemap` on the `Obstacles` layer with its dual-grid walls module (`walls_DualGridRuleTile`) and grid colliders, and its `RenderTilemap` at order 10 on the `Terrain` sorting layer, saved from the area template as the areas went (WorldStreaming.md's Phase 9). The Scene Generator writes into standalone scratch scenes (`WorldAuthoring.CreateScratchScene`, see Editor tools), never into a world's chunk.

Scene identity is data, not strings: [SceneDefinition](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Scenes/SceneDefinition.cs) assets under `Assets/Data/Scenes` (and beside each world's scenes under `Assets/Worlds`) hold a scene GUID + path, and [GameScenes](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Scenes/GameScenes.cs) (`Assets/Data/Scenes/GameScenes.asset`) lists MainMenu, Gameplay, the seamless worlds (`worlds`, `FindWorldOwning(scenePath)`: the listed world whose root or chunk scene that is; `FindWorldById`), and the starting world and entry (`StartingWorld`, `StartingWorldEntry`: Testlands' `start`), where New Game and Host Game start and where a joining player the host does not know arrives.

### Load hierarchy

```
Main (MainLifetimeScope)
├── MainMenu (MenuLifetimeScope, parent = Main)            -- while in the menu
└── Gameplay (GameplayLifetimeScope, parent = Main)        -- while in a session
    └── World_* (WorldLifetimeScope, parent = Gameplay)    -- each world with a player in it
        └── World_*_x_y (ChunkLifetimeScope, parent = its world) -- the chunks streamed around the players
```

Every session runs in seamless worlds (see Worlds and streaming). The area path (one area scene at a time under Gameplay) went in WorldStreaming.md's Phase 9.

### Boot sequence

1. Play (or a build) starts in `Main`. In the editor, [PlayModeBootstrapper](../Assets/Editor/Scenes/PlayModeBootstrapper.cs) forces `playModeStartScene = Main` (toggle: `Tools/TopDownRPG/Boot From Main`) and stores the scenes that were open via [EditorBootRequest](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Scenes/EditorBootRequest.cs) (`SessionState`). Test Runner sessions are detected by their `InitTestScene*` scene and left alone.
2. `MainLifetimeScope` builds; its entry point [BootFlow](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Boot/BootFlow.cs) (`IAsyncStartable`) runs.
3. Editor only: `BootFlow` consumes the boot request. If a world's root or chunk scene was open (a world `GameScenes` lists, else any `WorldDefinition` asset in the project, such as the dev stress world), it starts a new game there (`SessionSave.StartNew`, then `GameFlow.StartWorldGameAsync(world)` at the world's default entry); if only Gameplay was open, a new game in the starting world at its entry; if MainMenu was open, the menu. Any other scene is no world's: it warns and shows the menu.
4. Otherwise `GameFlow.ShowMainMenuAsync()`.
5. Every session from the menu runs in seamless worlds (see Save data). Menu "New Game" -> `MainMenuController.StartNewGame()`: `SessionSave.StartNew()`, then `GameFlow.StartWorldGameAsync` offline in the starting world at its entry (`GameScenes.StartingWorld`, `StartingWorldEntry`: Testlands' `start`). "Continue" loads the save into the `SessionSave` and starts offline with the local player at its saved world and position (`PlayerStarts`). "Host Game" starts hosting first, then continues the save the same way when there is one, or starts a new game. "Join Game" connects to the address in the field and then starts `GameFlow.StartJoinedGameAsync`, which waits for the host to place this player in a world (see Networking).

### GameFlow

[GameFlow](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Scenes/GameFlow.cs) (singleton in Main scope) owns all scene transitions. Uses [SceneLoader](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Scenes/SceneLoader.cs) (additive load/unload, `Resources.UnloadUnusedAssets`) and [ScreenFader](../Assets/Scripts/Core/UI/ScreenFader.cs).

- Every transition: reject if one is running -> `TransitionStarted` -> fade out -> work -> fade in -> `TransitionFinished`. Cancelled with `Application.exitCancellationToken`. A trip between worlds is a transition too (`IsTravelling`), so it never runs beside another one, either way round.
- `ShowMainMenuAsync`: leave any world session, unload gameplay and unused assets, load MainMenu under Main.
- `StartWorldGameAsync(world, position)` (and `StartWorldGameAsync(world, entryName)`, which starts at that entry's position, `WorldDefinition.EntryPosition`): unload menu, leave any world session, unload gameplay and unused assets, load Gameplay under Main (made the active scene), set `CurrentWorld`, then await the Gameplay scope's [IWorldSession](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Worlds/IWorldSession.cs) `EnterAsync(world, position)`, which completes once the ground around the players is loaded, before the fade in. Logs an error and enters nothing when the scope provides no `IWorldSession`. Leaving a world session first raises `WorldSessionEnding` (the session's last save), then (`LeaveAsync`, awaited before anything unloads) unloads its chunks and worlds one at a time.
- `StartJoinedGameAsync()`: a joined client's session. Like `StartWorldGameAsync` up to the Gameplay scene, then awaits the Gameplay scope's `IWorldSession.JoinAsync()`: the host spawns this machine's player in a world, which the client loads around it before the fade in, and that world becomes `CurrentWorld`. When the session ends first it warns and leaves the Gameplay scene without a world; `NetworkSessionLifecycle` then takes the game back to the menu. Leaving a world session also leaves one that is still joining (the world session is active though no world is recorded yet).
- `TravelAsync(world, entryName)`: the local player's trip to another world (WorldStreaming.md's Travelling between worlds), run by `LocalTripTraveller` once the trip's authority has approved it and put the player in that world. A transition flagged `IsTravelling`: fade out, set `CurrentWorld`, await the Gameplay scope's `IWorldSession.ArriveAsync(world, entryName)` (the local player moved to the entry, then the ground around it loaded), fade in. It loads and unloads nothing itself, and `TransitionTimeFreeze` leaves game time running. Logs an error outside a world session.
- `WorldSessionEnding` event: raised as a world session is about to end (the menu, a new session), with its players still in their worlds; `GameSaveRecorder` saves then.
- State: `IsTransitioning`, `IsTravelling` (the transition running is a trip), `IsInMenu`, `IsInGame` (Gameplay loaded and a world recorded), `CurrentWorld` (the local player's world: the one the session started in, then each one it travelled to), `GameplayScene`.

## Composition / DI

VContainer scopes live one per scene. Each Gameplay-assembly scope logs an error and registers nothing if it has no parent (i.e. the scene was opened without booting through Main).

### MainLifetimeScope ([MainLifetimeScope.cs](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Boot/MainLifetimeScope.cs), Core)

| Registration | Kind |
|---|---|
| `GameScenes` | instance (inspector) |
| `Camera` (main camera), `ScreenFader`, `CameraFollow2D` | components (inspector) |
| `IClock` -> `UnityClock.Shared` | instance |
| `IRandom` -> `new SystemRandom()` (time-seeded) | instance |
| `SceneLoader`, `GameFlow` (gets this scope as `LifetimeScope` parameter) | singletons |
| `UISelection` (with the inspector's `EventSystem`) | singleton |
| `RuntimeInputActions` (the inspector's `InputActionAsset` and `EventSystem`) | singleton |
| `PlayerInputService` as `IPlayerInput`, `IMenuInput` + self (gets the `RuntimeInputActions` copy and the scene's `EventSystem`; a missing `EventSystem` logs an error) | singleton |
| `GameplayInputGate` (+ self), `TransitionTimeFreeze` (with `ISessionStatus`, so it freezes offline only) | entry points |
| `TimeFreeze` (owns `Time.timeScale`, back to 1 on dispose) | singleton |
| `MenuStack` (+ self) | entry point |
| `BootFlow` | entry point |
| `NetworkSession` as `IGameAuthority`, `INetworkObjectSpawner`, `IClientReadiness`, `IChunkHandshake`, `IChunkHolds`, `ISessionStatus`, `IPlayerIdSource` + self (gets the inspector's `NetworkManager` prefab and `NetworkSettings`, and `UserSettings` for the player id) | singleton; logs an error and is skipped if either is missing |
| `NetworkSessionLifecycle` | entry point |
| `ISaveStore` -> `FileSaveStore` at `Application.persistentDataPath/save.json` (`save.Player2.json` and so on for a Multiplayer Play Mode virtual player, `InstanceFiles`) | instance |
| `GameSave`, `SessionSave`, `PlayerStarts` | singletons |
| `ISaveStore` -> `FileSaveStore` at `Application.persistentDataPath/settings.json` (a virtual player's own, as above), keyed `UserSettings.FileName` | instance |
| `UserSettings` (takes the keyed store) | singleton |
| `InputBindings` as `IInputBindingLabels` + self (gets the `RuntimeInputActions` copy and the inspector's `RebindableActionList`; a missing list logs an error) | entry point |

### MenuLifetimeScope ([MenuLifetimeScope.cs](../Assets/Scripts/Composition/MenuLifetimeScope.cs))

- `OptionsMenuView` via `RegisterComponentInHierarchy`, and `OptionsMenuController` as an entry point + self.
- `MainMenuController` via `RegisterComponentInHierarchy` (injected with `GameFlow`, `NetworkSession`, `GameSave`, `SessionSave`, `PlayerStarts`, `OptionsMenuController` and `UISelection`).

| Registration | Kind |
|---|---|
| `PlayerRegistry` as `IPlayerRegistry` + self | singleton |
| `PlayerControlSettings` (the player prefab's own asset, so there is one source of truth; a prefab without one logs an error and registers nothing) | instance |
| `LocalPlayerTracker`, `PointerTargetPicker`, `LocalPlayerCommandSource`, `PlayerNavigation`, `PlayerBinder`, `PlayerRespawner` | singletons |
| `PlayerSpawner` (with the inspector's player prefab and the Gameplay scene as parameters; places a joining player on a free entry slot with `GameplaySettings`) | singleton |

| `DamagePopupLayer` | component |
| `PointerFeedbackLayer` (inspector; a missing one logs an error and skips `PointerFeedbackPresenter`) | component |
| `GameplaySettings` | instance |
| `CombatSettings` (inspector; a missing one logs an error and registers nothing, like a missing player prefab) | instance |
| `PlayerHudView`, `AbilityBarView`, `StatusBarView`, `OptionsMenuView`, `GameMenuView` | components in hierarchy |
| `CombatEvents`, `DamageService`, `HitService`, `AbilityService`, `SlashSpawner`, `EffectSpawner` | singletons |
| `StatusVisualPool` (with the Gameplay scene) | singleton (disposed with the scope) |
| `StatusEffectService` (+ self) | entry point (`ITickable`) |
| `DamagePopupPresenter`, `GameplayEntryPoint`, `PlayerDeathHandler` (`ITickable`), `PlayerHudPresenter`, `AbilityBarPresenter`, `StatusBarPresenter`, `PointerFeedbackPresenter`, `OptionsMenuController` (+ self), `GameMenuController` (+ self) | entry points |

| `LocalTripTraveller` (+ self) | entry point |

[GameplayEntryPoint](../Assets/Scripts/Composition/GameplayEntryPoint.cs): points `CameraFollow2D` at the local player and snaps whenever `LocalPlayerTracker` assigns one (on a client that happens when the host's spawn arrives); clears the target on dispose.

### WorldLifetimeScope and ChunkLifetimeScope ([WorldLifetimeScope.cs](../Assets/Scripts/Composition/WorldLifetimeScope.cs), [ChunkLifetimeScope.cs](../Assets/Scripts/Composition/ChunkLifetimeScope.cs))

- A world's root scene holds a `WorldLifetimeScope`, built under the Gameplay scope with the world's `WorldDefinition` enqueued by `WorldStreaming`; each chunk scene holds a `ChunkLifetimeScope`, built under its world's scope with a `ChunkIdentity` (world, chunk coordinates) enqueued. Both log an error without a parent scope and then register nothing; the chunk scope also logs one for every `MobController` placed in the chunk: mobs come from spawn points. Other scene objects needing injection go in a scope's `autoInjectGameObjects` (the stress world's root scene lists its `StressTest` object).
- `WorldLifetimeScope` registers the world's `WorldNavigation` (as itself and `INavigation2D`, with the Gameplay scope's `NavigationPagePool`), disposed with the scope, which frees its pages' slots. `WorldStreaming` resolves it into the world's `LoadedWorld`. It also registers the world's `WorldPlayers` (as itself and `IPlayerRegistry`, built by a factory from the Gameplay scope's registry, `PlayerWorlds` and the world's definition, since a constructor taking `IPlayerRegistry` would resolve itself), disposed with the scope: everything injected in the world's scope and its chunks' scopes, the mobs above all, sees only the players in that world. And it registers the world's `WorldState`, from the Main scope's `SessionSave` (`WorldOf(world id)`), which outlives the scope, so what its chunks record survives the world unloading.
- `ChunkLifetimeScope` registers the `ChunkNavigation` entry point with the chunk's `NavigationGrid2D` (an error when the chunk has none, and then nothing below either): in `Start` it adds the chunk's page to its world's navigation, and when the scope is disposed (the chunk unloading) it takes it out. It also registers the `ChunkMobSpawner` entry point, resolvable as itself (with every `MobSpawnPoint` of the chunk and the chunk's scene; its `WorldState` and `SessionSave` come from the parent scopes), plus a build callback that calls its `RegisterNetworkPrefabs`. When the chunk has an `AreaTerrain` (every chunk of the worlds; `WorldAuthoring.SetUpTerrainEdits` gives one) it registers it as a component and the `ChunkTerrainSync` entry point, resolvable as itself (with the chunk's scene), plus a build callback that calls its `RegisterNetworkPrefab`; these come before the grid check.

### How components get injected

- Plain classes: constructor injection (`GameFlow`, `BootFlow`, presenters, spawners, `WorldStreaming`).
- MonoBehaviours: `[Inject] public void Construct(...)`, reached via `InjectGameObject` build callbacks or `IObjectResolver.Instantiate`.

| Component | `Construct` parameters | Injected by |
|---|---|---|
| `PlayerWeaponController` | `SlashSpawner`, `IClock` | `PlayerSpawner` (`resolver.Instantiate`), or `InjectingNetworkPrefabHandler` on clients |
| `PlayerNetworkSync` | `PlayerBinder` | same as `PlayerWeaponController` |
| `PlayerWorldSync` | `PlayerWorlds`, `GameScenes`, `NetworkTripAuthority`, `IClientReadiness`, `WaystoneNotices` | same as `PlayerWeaponController` |
| `PlayerController` | `IClock` | same as `PlayerWeaponController` |
| `PlayerMotor2D` | `PlayerNavigation` | same as `PlayerWeaponController` |
| `PlayerAbilities` | `IClock`, `AbilityService` | same as `PlayerWeaponController` |
| `MainMenuController` | `GameFlow`, `NetworkSession`, `GameSave`, `SessionSave`, `PlayerStarts`, `OptionsMenuController`, `UISelection` | Menu scope |
| `MobController` | `INavigation2D`, `IPlayerRegistry`, `IRandom` | `ChunkMobSpawner` or `StressTestSpawner` (`resolver.Instantiate` through a chunk's or the stress world's scope), or `InjectingNetworkPrefabHandler` on clients (through `ChunkNetworkPrefabs`). The navigation is its world's `WorldNavigation` and the players its world's `WorldPlayers` |
| `MobMotor2D` | `IClock` | same as `MobController` |
| `MeleeDamageDealer` | `IClock`, `HitService` | same as `MobController` |
| `DestroyMobOnDeath` | `EffectSpawner`, `IGameAuthority` | same as `MobController` |
| `NetworkHealth` | `DamageService` | player: same as `PlayerWeaponController`; mob: same as `MobController` |
| `NetworkStatusEffects` | `CombatSettings`, `StatusEffectService`, `IClientReadiness` | same as `NetworkHealth` |
| `StressTestSpawner` | `IObjectResolver`, `WorldNavigation`, `WorldDefinition`, `LocalPlayerTracker` | The stress world's `WorldLifetimeScope` `autoInjectGameObjects` |
| `AreaTerrain` | `IGameAuthority` | Chunk scope `RegisterComponent` |
| `NetworkAreaTerrain` | `AreaTerrain` | `ChunkTerrainSync` (`resolver.Instantiate`) on the host, or a client's chunk handler through `ChunkNetworkPrefabs` |

`MeleeDamageDealer`, `MobMotor2D`, `PlayerController`, `PlayerAbilities` and `PlayerWeaponController` default their clock to `UnityClock.Shared` when not injected. A `PlayerMotor2D` that was never injected has no navigation and moves in straight lines.

## Networking

Netcode for GameObjects, host-and-play. See [Multiplayer.md](Multiplayer.md) for the plan and [WorldStreaming.md](WorldStreaming.md#networking) for worlds online; this section describes what exists. Players, mobs, health and runtime terrain edits are replicated. The host runs all game logic (mob AI, damage, deaths, respawns, trips between worlds); clients move their own player and mirror the rest. Sessions run in seamless worlds: each player is in one world at a time, every machine knows each player's world, the host streams for every player and a client for itself, and what a client receives follows the chunks it holds.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [NetworkSettings](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/NetworkSettings.cs) | ScriptableObject (`Assets/Data/NetworkSettings.asset`): default join address (`127.0.0.1`), host listen address (`0.0.0.0`), port (7777), client connect timeout (10 s), max players per session including the host (4, range 1–4). |
| [NetworkSession](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/NetworkSession.cs) | Main-scope singleton that owns the `NetworkManager`: instantiates it from `Assets/Prefabs/Network/NetworkManager.prefab` (`NetworkManager` + `UnityTransport`, scene management off, no player prefab) and destroys it on dispose, so it never outlives the Main scope despite Netcode moving it to `DontDestroyOnLoad`. `StartHost()`, `JoinAsync(address, timeout, token)` (connects with this build's version and `LocalPlayerId`, the install's player id from `UserSettings`, waits for the connection, shuts down and returns false on failure/timeout), `Shutdown()`, `RegisterPrefab`/`UnregisterPrefab`, `JoinRefusal` (the host's reason when it refused the last join, else null), `IsActive`/`IsHost`/`IsServer`/`IsConnectedClient`/`LocalClientId`, event `ConnectionLost` (local client stopped without `Shutdown` being called). Makes its `NetworkManager` the Netcode singleton before starting, since Netcode falls back to the singleton when spawning. Implements `IGameAuthority` (`IsAuthoritative` = server, or offline when not following a host: a client whose connection dropped stays non-authoritative until `Shutdown()`, which `NetworkSessionLifecycle` calls once it is back in the menu, so a stranded client never saves or runs the game on its own). Player ids: the host keys every approved client by the id in its join payload (`PlayerIdOf(clientId)`; the host's own is `LocalPlayerId`); a client without one, or with one already in the session (a copied `settings.json`; Multiplayer Play Mode's virtual players have files of their own, `InstanceFiles`), gets a new id for the session, with a warning. Readiness (`ClientChunkReadiness`, below, with this session as its `IClientVisibility`), over named messages on `MessageDelivery` (`ReliableFragmentedSequenced`, the channel Netcode creates and destroys objects on, so an acknowledgement arrives after the hides before it): a client's `NotifyJoined()` (`ClientJoinedMessage`) once its Gameplay scope is up; host `JoinedClients`, `IsClientJoined`, event `ClientJoined`; a client's `ReportChunkReady(trip, chunk)` (`ChunkReadyMessage`) and `ReleaseChunkAsync(chunk)` (`ChunkReleasingMessage`, then waits for `ChunkReleasedMessage` or the session's end); host `IsClientReady(clientId)` (settled in its current trip), `IsClientReady(clientId, chunk)`, `BeginTrip(clientId, trip)`, `IsChunkHeld(chunk)`, all true for the host's own client where they ask about one; `Readiness` (internal, for tests). Spawning, all destroyed with their scene: `SpawnPlayerObject(instance, owner)` and `Spawn(instance)` are visible to every joined client; `Spawn(instance, homeChunk)` to the clients that hold that chunk, and, when the prefab's handler here is a `NetworkPrefabInstanceHandlerWithData<NetworkChunkId>` (the chunks register theirs on the host too), it sets the chunk as the instantiation data. `ShowNowVisible`/`HideNoLongerVisible` walk the spawned objects with a visibility check and `NetworkShow`/`NetworkHide` the ones whose check changed for that client. `Despawn(instance)` (host) despawns and destroys one object, destroying it when it is not spawned. `RegisterPrefab(prefab, handler)` installs a handler of the caller's own (`ChunkNetworkPrefabs`' routers), with an error when the prefab already has one. `HasPlayerObject(clientId)`. Also implements `INetworkObjectSpawner`, `IChunkHandshake`, [ISessionStatus](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/ISessionStatus.cs) (`IsActive`, `IsHost`) for the in-game menu, the trips and the time freeze, and [IPlayerIdSource](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/IPlayerIdSource.cs) (`LocalPlayerId`, `PlayerIdOf`) for `PlayerIds`. A client stopping while the application quits or the editor leaves Play Mode is not reported as `ConnectionLost`. |
| [JoinApproval](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/JoinApproval.cs) | Static admission rule, free of Netcode types. `CreatePayload(version, playerId)` is the connection data a client sends (its `Application.version`, a line break, its player id; line breaks inside either are dropped) and `ReadPayload` reads both back (a payload with only a version has no player id); `Evaluate(payload, hostVersion, playerCount, maxPlayers)` returns null to approve, or a reason: another or missing version first, then a full party. `EncodeRefusal`/`TryDecodeRefusal` tag the reason so a client can tell it from Netcode's own disconnect text. |
| [ClientChunkReadiness](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/ClientChunkReadiness.cs) | Plain C#, the host's per-client readiness and the release handshake (WorldStreaming.md's Networking). `Join(client)` (shows it what it may see: the players), `Leave`, `Clear`, `JoinedClients`, `IsJoined`; `BeginTrip(client, trip)` (a newer trip only: reports tagged with an earlier one are ignored from then on, and the client is unsettled until it reports a chunk for the trip; chunks it holds stay held), `TripOf`; `ReportReady(client, trip, chunk)` (ignored before joining, for another trip or no chunk; else the chunk is held, the client settled and shown what it may now see); `Release(client, chunk)` (forgets the chunk, hides what the client may no longer see, then acknowledges, held or not); `IsReady(client, chunk)`, `IsSettled(client)`, `IsHeld(chunk)` (by any client). Showing, hiding and acknowledging go through an [IClientVisibility](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/IClientVisibility.cs), so `ClientChunkReadinessTests` runs it without Netcode. |
| [ChunkId](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Worlds/ChunkId.cs), [NetworkChunkId](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/NetworkChunkId.cs) | A chunk as every machine names it: its world's `WorldId` and its coordinates (`Of(world, coords)`, `IsValid`, value equality), and its wire form (`INetworkSerializable`, the id a `FixedString64Bytes`), which the chunk messages and chunk objects' instantiation data carry. |
| [IChunkHolds](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/IChunkHolds.cs), [IChunkHandshake](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/IChunkHandshake.cs) | The handshake as the world streaming sees it: `IsChunkHeld(chunk)` (host: some client holds it, so the streamer keeps it), `ReportChunkReady(trip, chunk)` and `ReleaseChunkAsync(chunk)` (client only; nothing offline or on the host). |
| [PlayerWorldState](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/PlayerWorldState.cs) | A player's world as `PlayerWorldSync` replicates it: `WorldId` (empty for none), `Trip` (its trip count) and `Entry` (where its latest trip leads), fixed strings and value equality. |
| [INetworkObjectSpawner](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/INetworkObjectSpawner.cs) | `IsActive`, `IsServer`, `RegisterPrefab` (with a resolver and a target scene, or with a handler of one's own, which `NetworkSession` installs and refuses with an error when the prefab already has one, since Netcode keeps one handler per prefab), `UnregisterPrefab`, `Spawn` (visible to joined clients) and `Spawn(instance, homeChunk)` (visible to the clients holding that chunk), `Despawn` (host: despawns and destroys; destroys an object that is not spawned): the slice of `NetworkSession` chunk code needs, so EditMode tests can fake it. |
| [IGameAuthority](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/IGameAuthority.cs) | `IsAuthoritative`: whether this machine decides game state. Implemented by `NetworkSession`; tests use a fixed fake. |
| [IClientReadiness](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/IClientReadiness.cs) | `IsClientReady(clientId)`: whether a client reported a chunk ready since its player's latest trip began (the host's own client always has); `IsClientReady(clientId, chunk)`: whether it holds that chunk; `BeginTrip(clientId, trip)` (host). Implemented by `NetworkSession`; `NetworkStatusEffects` holds a client's player until it is ready, and `PlayerWorldSync` begins its trips. |
| [InjectingNetworkPrefabHandler](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/InjectingNetworkPrefabHandler.cs) | `INetworkPrefabInstanceHandler` that creates network prefab instances through an `IObjectResolver` so their `[Inject]` methods run, moves them into a target scene when one is given (players into Gameplay, a chunk's objects into their home chunk through `ChunkNetworkPrefabs`), and destroys them on despawn. Netcode only calls it on clients; the host must create its instances with `resolver.Instantiate` itself before spawning. |
| [NetworkSessionLifecycle](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Network/NetworkSessionLifecycle.cs) | Main-scope entry point: shuts the session down whenever a transition ends in the menu, and returns to the menu (with a warning) when the connection is lost anywhere but the idle menu. A loss during a transition (a client still joining, mid-trip, or loading) is remembered and acted on when that transition finishes, since `GameFlow` ignores a second transition. A loss in the idle menu just ends the session. |

- `Assets/DefaultNetworkPrefabs.asset` is Netcode's auto-maintained network prefab list, referenced by the NetworkManager prefab: the player, the Weasel, the stress-test mob and `AreaTerrainSync` (the prefab of `NetworkAreaTerrain`).
- Runtime terrain edits, per chunk: only the host (or an offline game) changes a chunk's editable tilemaps, through its `AreaTerrain.TrySetCells`. Each changed cell is kept in a per-chunk list (last write per cell wins) that the host's `NetworkAreaTerrain` of that chunk mirrors into a `NetworkList<TerrainEdit>`; a client applies the whole list when the object reaches it (once it holds the chunk, so a client arriving there catches up as a late joiner does) and each change after that. Every machine's navigation follows the tile changes on its own. A client moves its own player, so between an edit and its arrival that player can still walk where the host just built a wall.
- Scene management is disabled: each machine loads its own worlds and chunks (`WorldStreaming`), so nothing networked is placed in a scene and nothing networked moves between scenes (players live in Gameplay, a chunk's objects in their home chunk).
- Netcode sets `Application.runInBackground` while a `NetworkManager` exists.
- Connection approval is on in the NetworkManager prefab (`NetworkSession` logs an error if it is off). The host approves itself, and approves a client through `JoinApproval` against `Application.version` and `NetworkSettings.MaxPlayers`, counting connected clients plus clients approved this frame but not yet connected. A refused client gets the reason as its disconnect reason; `JoinAsync` exposes it as `JoinRefusal` and the menu shows it. Approval is part of Netcode's config hash, so every build that should connect must have it on. Bump the Version in Player Settings whenever a change breaks compatibility between builds.
- Join order on a client: connect (version and player id), load Gameplay (its scope registers the player prefab in a build callback) and send joined (`GameplayPlayers.Start`); the host spawns its player where `PlayerStarts` places it (its saved place by player id, else the starting world's entry), once its own streaming has loaded that world and the chunks around the place (`IWorldSession.PrepareAsync`), with its world set, and shows it every player, whatever their world; the client's `PlayerWorldSync` puts its player in that world, `WorldStreaming` loads the world and the chunks around it, registering each chunk's mob and terrain sync prefabs (`ChunkNetworkPrefabs`), and reports each chunk ready once it is up; only then does the host show it that chunk's mobs and terrain edits, and stop holding its player. A late joiner goes the same way.
- Walking and trips: a client releases each chunk before unloading it (`WorldStreaming`), and the host hides that chunk's objects from it before acknowledging; the host never unloads a chunk a client holds (its streamer's `IChunkHolds`), except when the session ends. A client's trip is an RPC naming the exit (`PlayerWorldSync.RequestTripRpc`, with the client's own position); the host checks it (`LocalTripAuthority`), streams the entry, and puts the player in the target world for its next trip, which reaches the client as its player's networked world: the client fades, moves its own player there (a teleport snaps on the other machines), reports the new world's chunks for that trip and fades in. Nobody else fades. No session ever restarts when every player is dead: each player respawns on its own, and a respawn is a trip within the player's own world to its waystone, decided by the host and run by the player's own machine like any trip; the host restores the player's health once it sees it there with the waystone's chunk held by its client (see Player). A discovery of a waystone by a client's player reaches its owner as `PlayerWorldSync.WaystoneDiscoveredRpc`, for its popup. The protocol gained that RPC in WorldStreaming.md's Phase 9, so the build version went from 1.3 to 1.4.
- Unity Transport timeouts on the NetworkManager prefab: heartbeat 500 ms, disconnect 10 s (down from the 30 s default). The disconnect timeout is how long a client waits before giving up on a host that vanished without a clean disconnect (a crash, or it dropping off the virtual LAN), and the same for the host about a client. 10 s still rides out short Wi-Fi or Tailscale hiccups; a heartbeat counts as traffic, so an idle but live connection never times out.
- The NetworkManager prefab carries a `NetworkSimulator` set to the `None` preset. It drives every transport in the process, so only one should be enabled (the in-process test clients remove theirs).

### Transform smoothing

Networked transforms use `LegacyLerp` position interpolation with unreliable deltas and the default buffer (`NetworkTransform.InterpolationBufferTickOffset` 0, tick rate 30). This was chosen by measurement, not by the package docs' suggestion: the explicit benchmark `NetworkSmoothnessPlayModeTests.Benchmark_RemotePlayerSmoothness_UnderStress` moves an in-process client's player for 2 s at a time under 150 ms latency, 20 ms jitter and 4% loss and records, per rendered frame of the host's copy, how far the speed strays from the owner's, the longest freeze and visible backward jumps. Results on this machine (6 runs per setting, run in both orders):

| Setting | Start lag | Mean speed error | Runs with a freeze over 150 ms |
|---|---|---|---|
| `LegacyLerp`, unreliable deltas, offset 0 or 1 | ~390 ms | 22–26% | 0–2 of 6 |
| `Lerp`, unreliable deltas, offset 1 or 2 | ~380 ms | 57–63% | 0–2 of 6 |
| `SmoothDampening`, unreliable deltas | ~350 ms | ~100% | up to 3 of 6 |
| Any type with reliable deltas | 450–550 ms | 60–150%, with backward jumps for `Lerp`/`SmoothDampening` | frequent |

Unreliable deltas are the biggest win (a lost update is replaced by the next one instead of resent behind it). Rerun the benchmark by name after changing tick rate, speeds, movement code or transform settings.

Rerun after click-to-move replaced WASD (the client's player now walks on far-away move orders, with the mobs despawned): `LegacyLerp` unreliable offset 0 gave 385 ms start lag, 21% mean speed error and no stall over 150 ms in 6 runs; offset 1 26%; `Lerp` 59–67%; `SmoothDampening` ~106%; reliable deltas 76% with stalls in 5 of 6 runs. The ranking and the shipped setting are unchanged. The 3-run regression test is noisier than the 6-run benchmark (38% in the same session).

## Save data

One local save file, written by the machine that decides game state (offline or the host; a client never saves), holding the world sessions (WorldStreaming.md's Persistence, Phase 8). See [Multiplayer.md](Multiplayer.md#phase-6-services-and-persistence) for the decisions. Per-machine preferences live in a separate settings file (see Settings below).

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [ISaveStore](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Save/ISaveStore.cs) | `TryRead`/`Write` of the save text, so save logic is tested without the disk. |
| [FileSaveStore](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Save/FileSaveStore.cs) | Writes `<path>.tmp`, then swaps it in (`File.Replace`, or a move for the first save) so a crash never leaves half a save; logs IO failures as errors and returns false. |
| [GameSaveData](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Save/GameSaveData.cs) | Serialized form (`JsonUtility`), version 2 (`CurrentVersion`): `time` (the world clock, game seconds played on the save), `players` (per player id: `worldId`, `position`, `waystones` (the waystones it discovered, world id and entry name each; the world's default entry counts as discovered without one)) and `worlds` (per world id visited: its `chunks`, each with `coords`, `deadMobs` (spawn point id and `diedAt` on the world clock) and `terrainEdits` (layer index, cell, filled)). Nested records `Player`, `Waystone`, `World`, `Chunk`, `MobDeath`, `CellEdit`. `TryParse` returns false on malformed JSON. A version 1 save (an area GUID and spawn id) is not read. |
| [GameSave](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Save/GameSave.cs) | `TryLoad(out data)`, `HasSave`, `Write(data)`. A malformed file or another format version (version 1 included) is ignored with a warning. |
| [SessionSave](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Save/SessionSave.cs) | Main-scope singleton: the running session's saved state in memory, used offline and on the host. `StartNew()` (New Game, an editor boot into a world, and a joining client, which never writes it) or `StartFrom(data)` (Continue, Host Game with a save). The world clock `Time`: the saved time plus the game time (`IClock`) since the session started, so a death time from one session means the same in the next. Places by player id (`TryGetPlace`, `SetPlace`) and, apart from them, the waystones each player id discovered (`Discover(playerId, worldId, entry)`, once each, true when new; `WaystonesOf`), which a new place keeps and `ToData` writes into each player's record (a player with discoveries but no place yet is left out; every player in a world has its place recorded before each save), the `WorldState` of every world visited (`WorldOf(worldId)`, made empty on first visit; `TryGetWorld`), `ToData()`. It outlives the Gameplay scope. |
| [WorldState](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Save/WorldState.cs), [ChunkState](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Save/ChunkState.cs) | What persists of a world: a `ChunkState` per chunk coordinates (`Chunk(coords)`, made on first use; `TryGetChunk`; empty ones are not saved), `ToData`/`FromData`. A chunk's record: killed mobs by spawn point persistent id (`RecordMobDeath(id, time)`, `TryGetMobDeath`, `StaysDead(id, now, respawns, delay)`, which forgets a death whose delay has passed so the mob spawns again) and terrain edits, the last write per layer and cell kept in place (`RecordTerrainEdit`, `TerrainEdits`). |
| [PlayerStarts](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Worlds/PlayerStarts.cs) | Main-scope singleton: where a player starts. `TryGetStart(playerId, out world, out position)`: its saved place in the `SessionSave` when its world is listed (`GameScenes.FindWorldById`) and the position lies on one of that world's chunks (`WorldDefinition.HasChunkAt`), else, with a warning for a place gone, `GameScenes.StartingWorld` at `StartingWorldEntry` (an error and false without a starting world). The menu asks it for the local player, the host for each joining one. |
| [GameSaveRecorder](../Assets/Scripts/World/Persistence/GameSaveRecorder.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`). Writes the `SessionSave` through `GameSave` only while `IGameAuthority.IsAuthoritative` and a world session runs (`GameFlow.CurrentWorld`): once a frame at most after any player enters a world (`PlayerWorlds.Changed`: the session's start, a trip, a player joining), every `GameplaySettings.AutosaveIntervalSeconds` (60) of game time, and at once on `GameFlow.WorldSessionEnding` (the menu, the host leaving, a new game) and `Application.quitting` (in the editor, leaving Play Mode). `Save()` first records each registered player's place under its player id (`PlayerIds`): where it stands, or the entry of its trip while `TripArrivals` holds one it has not reached, since a traveller is put in the target world before it moves (`TryGetPlace`, internal), or, for a dead player on no trip, the waystone it will respawn at (`TryGetRespawnPlace`, internal: `WaystoneRules.TryFindNearest`), so leaving or quitting while dead neither skips the respawn nor brings it back beside its killer; nothing when it stands on none of its world's chunks. A player leaving the registry (a client disconnecting) has its place recorded as it goes, for its next join. |
| [InstanceFiles](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Save/InstanceFiles.cs) | Static: `NameFor(fileName)`, the save and settings file names of this instance. In the editor a Multiplayer Play Mode virtual player (launched with `-name Player2` to `-name Player4`, per the package's FAQ) gets its own (`save.Player2.json`), so it keeps its own player id, key bindings and offline save, as a separate install would; the main editor and builds keep the plain names. |

Worlds are saved by world id (their asset GUID), never by path. Booting the editor straight into a world starts a new game there, so its first save replaces the save file, as New Game does. A killed mob comes back after its config's `RespawnDelaySeconds` of world-clock time, and only as its chunk loads again: chunks load outside every player's view (the interest rectangle is the view plus the load margin), or behind a fade (a session's start, a trip, a join), so a mob never appears in front of a player. Multiplayer Play Mode virtual players share the main editor's data folder; `InstanceFiles` gives each its own files.

### Settings

The local machine's preferences, in `settings.json` next to the save (a Multiplayer Play Mode virtual player's own `settings.Player2.json` and so on, see `InstanceFiles`). Every machine reads and writes its own, clients included; nothing is replicated. Starting a new game never touches it. Specified in [KeyBindings.md](KeyBindings.md#saving).

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [UserSettingsData](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Settings/UserSettingsData.cs) | Serialized form (`JsonUtility`): `version` (`CurrentVersion` 1), `inputBindingOverrides` (the Input System's override JSON as a string) and `playerId` (the install's player id; a file from before it reads with an empty one, so the format version stayed 1). `TryParse` returns false on malformed JSON. |
| [UserSettings](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Settings/UserSettings.cs) | Reads the file once when created, through the `ISaveStore` registered under the `UserSettings.FileName` key (so tests swap it like the save store); a malformed file or an older format version is ignored with a warning and replaced on the next change, while a file from a newer build is ignored with a warning and never overwritten (`IsReadOnly`: changes stay in memory for the session). `InputBindingOverrides`, and `SetInputBindingOverrides`, which writes the file unless it is read-only and returns whether it did. `PlayerId`: this install's player id (WorldStreaming.md's Player identity), a GUID made the first time it is read and written to the file with the other settings (kept for the session only when the file is read-only). |

## Input

Click-to-move, specified in [PlayerControls.md](PlayerControls.md). Mouse only for gameplay; the gamepad still works in menus. Every gameplay control but the pointer can be rebound ([KeyBindings.md](KeyBindings.md)); these are the defaults.

| Input | Action (`Player` map) |
|---|---|
| Right mouse button | `MoveClick`: pressed gives an order at the cursor, held re-evaluates it |
| A | `AttackMove`: an attack move to the cursor's point at once (no confirm click) |
| Pointer position | `Point` (`<Pointer>/position`, pass-through) |
| S | `Stop` |
| Q W E R, 1 2 | `Ability1`…`Ability6`: cast the ability in that slot toward the cursor (quick cast) |

- [IPlayerInput](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Input/IPlayerInput.cs): `GameplayEnabled`, `PointerScreenPosition`, `IsPointerOverUI`, `MovePressedThisFrame`, `MoveHeld`, `AttackMovePressedThisFrame`, `StopPressedThisFrame`, `WasAbilityPressedThisFrame(slot)`. The local device seam; gameplay reaches it only through `LocalPlayerCommandSource` (see Player). [AbilitySlots](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Input/AbilitySlots.cs)`.Count` is 6.
- [PlayerInputService](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Input/PlayerInputService.cs): wraps the `Player` action map of [InputSystem_Actions.inputactions](../Assets/InputSystem_Actions.inputactions) (action names are public constants; `AbilityActionName(slot)`). Returns neutral input while the map is disabled. `IsPointerOverUI` asks the injected Main-scope `EventSystem` (`IsPointerOverGameObject`, so only graphics with `raycastTarget` block clicks: the ability bar's slot frames, and `ScreenFader` while visible; every other HUD graphic has it off). Logs an error if the map or any action is missing. The `Player` map holds only `MoveClick`, `AttackMove`, `Point`, `Stop` and `Ability1`…`Ability6`, one `Keyboard&Mouse` binding each. A `Menus` map holds `Menu` (Escape, gamepad Start) for the in-game menu; `SetMenusEnabled`/`MenusEnabled` (the gate drives it).
- [GameplayInputGate](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Input/GameplayInputGate.cs): Main-scope entry point (+ self) that enables the `Player` map only when `GameFlow.IsInGame && !IsTransitioning` and nothing holds a block (`Block(owner)`/`Unblock(owner)`, `IsBlocked`; the in-game menu blocks it while open), and the `Menus` map whenever in game outside transitions; listens to `TransitionStarted`/`TransitionFinished`.
- [RuntimeInputActions](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Input/RuntimeInputActions.cs): Main-scope singleton holding this session's `Instantiate` copy of the project input asset (ids kept), which `PlayerInputService`, `InputBindings` and the `EventSystem`'s `InputSystemUIInputModule` use (it disables the module, points it at the copy and enables it again, so the module enables the copy's UI actions). The project asset is never changed at runtime; the copy is destroyed on dispose.
- [InputBindings](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Input/InputBindings.cs): Main-scope entry point that owns the binding overrides on the session's copy, for the actions listed in [RebindableActionList](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Input/RebindableActionList.cs) (`Assets/Data/Input/RebindableActions.asset`: `MoveClick`, `AttackMove`, `Stop`, `Ability1`…`Ability6`, each with the name and section the options screen shows; actions are matched by id, so a copy of the asset works too). On `Initialize` it loads the overrides from `UserSettings`, drops any on other actions or on forbidden inputs and both sides of a duplicate (with one warning, then rewrites the file); malformed overrides are ignored with a warning, and two actions sharing a default input are logged as one error. `TryAssign(action, path, out swappedWith)` binds any key but Escape, or a mouse button (left, right, middle, back, forward), swapping with the rebindable action that had the input, removing the override when the path is the default, saving and raising `BindingsChanged` once. `Reset(action, out swappedWith)` assigns the default the same way; `ResetAll`. `GetShortLabel` (the layout's key name, or LMB, RMB, MMB, M4, M5), `GetAbilityKeyLabel(slot)` (the same for an ability slot's action, found once at construction) and `GetLabel` (mouse buttons spelled out); `GetDisplayName` and `GetSection` from the list. A keyboard layout change raises `BindingsChanged`. `StartRebind(action, finished)` listens for the next key or allowed mouse button (`PerformInteractiveRebinding` restricted to `<Keyboard>` and the five mouse buttons, any-key excluded, Escape cancelling) and routes it through `TryAssign`; `finished` gets `RebindOutcome.Assigned` with the swapped action, or `Cancelled` (also for a refused input). One rebind at a time, never on an enabled action (logged as an error); `CancelRebind`, `IsRebinding`. `Dispose` cancels a running rebind. It only ever changes the session's `RuntimeInputActions` copy.
- [IInputBindingLabels](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Input/IInputBindingLabels.cs): `BindingsChanged` and `GetAbilityKeyLabel(slot)`, the seam the HUD uses.
- [IMenuInput](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Input/IMenuInput.cs): `BackPressedThisFrame`, the `UI` map's Cancel (Escape, gamepad East) while that map is enabled, and `MenuPressedThisFrame`, the `Menus` map's Menu (Escape, gamepad Start) while that map is enabled; `PlayerInputService` implements it and logs the missing action with the others.
- UI input uses the `UI` map through the `EventSystem` in Main.

## Clock

- [IClock](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Clock/IClock.cs): `float Time`. Used for cooldowns and gameplay timers.
- [UnityClock](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Clock/UnityClock.cs): `Time.time`; `UnityClock.Shared` is registered in the Main scope.
- Clock consumers: `MeleeDamageDealer`, `PlayerWeaponController`, `PlayerController` (order timing: hold re-evaluation, chase repaths, cast times, the cast buffer window), `PlayerAbilities` (cooldowns), `MobMotor2D` (attack animation window). Mob AI timers are driven by the `dt` passed to `MobController.TickStateMachine`, which tests control. Purely visual, per-client animations (`SwordSlashAttack` lifetime, `MobDeathAnimation`, damage popups) advance with `Time.deltaTime` on purpose. `StatusEffectService` ticks statuses with the clock's delta each frame.
- [TimeFreeze](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Clock/TimeFreeze.cs) (Main-scope singleton): the only writer of `Time.timeScale`: 0 while any owner holds it (`Hold`/`Release`, `IsFrozen`, on a [HoldSet](../Assets/Scripts/Core/HoldSet.cs), the owner set `GameplayInputGate` blocks with too), 1 after the last release and on dispose. Holders: `TransitionTimeFreeze`, and `GameMenuController` in solo play.
- [TransitionTimeFreeze](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Scenes/TransitionTimeFreeze.cs) (Main-scope entry point): offline, holds the `TimeFreeze` from `GameFlow.TransitionStarted` to `TransitionFinished` (and releases it on dispose), except during a trip (`GameFlow.IsTravelling`), which is a local fade only, so nothing on the game clock, `Time.deltaTime`, physics or `Awaitable.WaitForSecondsAsync` advances behind the fade of a new game, Continue or the menu. In a session (`ISessionStatus.IsActive`) it never holds (`Freezes(transitioning, travelling, inSession)`): a machine's own start, join or leave stops nobody's game (WorldStreaming.md's Simulation and presentation). `ScreenFader` uses unscaled time.
- [IRandom](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Random/IRandom.cs): `Range(min, max)`, `InsideUnitCircle()`. [SystemRandom](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Random/SystemRandom.cs) wraps `System.Random`, time-seeded or with an explicit seed (tests). Gameplay code never uses `UnityEngine.Random`.
- Randomness consumers: `MobPatrolAnchor` (roam destinations, idle durations via `MobConfig.NextIdleDuration(IRandom)`), passed down from `MobController`.

## Camera and sprite sorting

- [CameraFollow2D](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Camera/CameraFollow2D.cs) (Core, on Main Camera): `LateUpdate` SmoothDamp follow of the target's Rigidbody2D position, separate smooth times for moving/idle, can snap to the pixel grid (32 PPU; `snapToPixelGrid` is off in `Main`, see Known gaps). `SetTarget`, `SnapToTarget`.
- The Main Camera's Pixel Perfect Camera (32 assets PPU, reference resolution 640 x 360) uses no crop frame: it picks the largest whole zoom that fits the 640 x 360 reference and shows the rest of the screen as more of the world, instead of drawing the reference into a centred box with a black border. At 1280 x 720 and 1920 x 1080 that is exactly the reference view. With a crop frame it renders into an offscreen texture blitted into the box, and Unity draws the Game view's gizmos as if the camera filled the whole window, so the spawn points' gizmos landed up to about 1.6 times farther from the screen centre than the world under them. The Pixel Perfect Camera warns in the Game view when the window has an odd width or height.
- [YPositionSorter](../Assets/Scripts/Camera/YPositionSorter.cs) (Gameplay, `[ExecuteAlways]`): sets `sortingOrder = offset - round(y * multiplier)` on a `SortingGroup` or on all child renderers (keeping their relative offsets). On the player, Weasel and death-animation prefabs.
- Sorting layers, back to front: `Default`, `Terrain` (ground: the blended terrain's chunks at order 0, the walls' render tilemap at 10; the move marker and hover ring also use order 10), `Entities` (units and props, sorted by `YPositionSorter`; slashes one order in front of their owner, or behind when swinging up), `Overlay` (world-space bars that draw over every unit).
- The camera culls the `Obstacles` layer, so the collision tilemap itself is never drawn in the game; in the editor `CollisionTileGizmo` (see Editor tools) shows its cells. It is the data tilemap of a DualGrid walls module (`walls_DualGridRuleTile`): the module's `RenderTilemap` child, on the Default layer and the Terrain sorting layer at order 10 (above the ground), draws the 32 px wall art, while the data tiles carry `Grid` colliders, so physics, navigation and the art all follow the same cells.

## UI

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [MenuStack](../Assets/Scripts/Core/UI/MenuStack.cs) / [IMenuLayer](../Assets/Scripts/Core/UI/IMenuLayer.cs) | Main-scope entry point (`ITickable`) and the only reader of `IMenuInput`: the open menus, newest on top (`Push`, `Remove`, `Top`, `Count`). Each tick it handles at most one press: Back or the Menu key goes to the top layer's `HandleBack`, and the Menu key with nothing open raises `MenuRequested`. Escape is both keys and still counts once, so no menu needs its own frame bookkeeping. |
| [UISelection](../Assets/Scripts/Core/UI/UISelection.cs) | Main-scope singleton over the scene's `EventSystem`: `Select(selectable)` (null clears). The menus move focus only through it, never through `EventSystem.current`. |
| [ScreenFader](../Assets/Scripts/Core/UI/ScreenFader.cs) | `CanvasGroup` fade used by `GameFlow` (unscaled time); blocks raycasts while visible; starts opaque. |
| [MainMenuController](../Assets/Scripts/UI/MainMenuController.cs) | Button handlers `ContinueGame` (`SessionSave.StartFrom` the save, then an offline world session with the local player at `PlayerStarts`' place for this install's player id; the button is only interactable, and selected first, when `GameSave.HasSave`), `StartNewGame` (`SessionSave.StartNew`, then the starting world at its entry, offline), `HostGame` (start hosting, then from the save when there is one, else new, as Continue and New Game do; an error and a status line when `GameScenes` has no starting world), `JoinGame` (join the address in `joinAddressField`, prefilled with the default; once connected it empties the `SessionSave` and starts `GameFlow.StartJoinedGameAsync`, ignores repeat clicks while connecting, and shows the host's reason when it refuses the join) and `QuitGame`; the four start handlers do nothing while a `GameFlow` transition runs (the fader blocks clicks, but keyboard and gamepad Submit still reach the selected button, and a session started then would be shut down when the menu finishes fading in) or while a join is connecting; writes progress and failures to `statusText`; selects Continue, or else `firstSelected`, for gamepad/keyboard navigation; logs an error in `Awake` when `continueButton` is unassigned. `OpenOptions` (the Options button) hides the `Buttons` panel and opens the options screen, unless a transition runs; closing it shows the buttons again with Options selected. Logs an error in `Awake` when the buttons panel or Options button is unassigned. |
| [OptionsMenuView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/OptionsMenuView.cs) / [KeyBindingRowView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/KeyBindingRowView.cs) | Passive options screen on the [OptionsMenu](../Assets/Prefabs/UI/OptionsMenu.prefab) prefab: a full-screen `Panel` (hidden by default) with the "Controls" title, a `Rows` list filled from the `OptionsSectionHeader` and `KeyBindingRow` prefabs (label, a binding button showing the key, a Reset button), a notice line, Reset all and Back, a listening panel that blocks clicks, and a Reset all confirmation (Reset / Cancel). Logs an error in `Awake` for missing references. |
| [OptionsMenuController](../Assets/Scripts/UI/OptionsMenuController.cs) | Entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`) and `IMenuLayer`: in `Start` builds a row per `InputBindings` action under its section header and wires the buttons. `Open(closed)` clears the notice, selects the first binding and pushes it on the `MenuStack`; `Close()` cancels a listening rebind, takes it off the stack and runs `closed` once. Selecting a binding starts `StartRebind` on the next tick (the click is never captured), shows the listening prompt and clears the selection; when it ends the row is reselected and a swap names the action that moved ("Class ability 1 moved to S."). A row's Reset resets that action (same notice on a swap); Reset all asks first. `HandleBack` closes the confirmation, or else the screen, and does nothing while a rebind is pending or listening (the rebind consumes Escape as its cancel). Rows follow `BindingsChanged`. |
| [GameMenuView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/GameMenuView.cs) | Passive in-game menu on the [GameMenuCanvas](../Assets/Prefabs/UI/GameMenuCanvas.prefab) prefab (which also holds an `OptionsMenu` instance): a dimmed full-screen `Panel` (hidden by default) with a box of Resume, Options, Leave game and Quit, and a confirmation (question, confirm button whose label it sets, Cancel). Logs an error in `Awake` for missing references. |
| [GameMenuController](../Assets/Scripts/UI/GameMenuController.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IStartable`) and `IMenuLayer`. `MenuStack.MenuRequested` opens it (`Open()`: Resume selected, pushed on the stack, a `GameplayInputGate` block, and a `TimeFreeze` hold when `ISessionStatus.IsActive` is false, so solo play pauses and online play, a lone host included, never does). `HandleBack` closes the confirmation or else the menu; nothing opens or reacts during a transition or once leaving. `Close()` also closes the options screen, takes it off the stack and gives back input and time; `Dispose` does the same, so leaving or a session restart never strands them. Options opens the options screen on top of it, whose close shows the menu again with Options selected. Leave game runs `GameFlow.ShowMainMenuAsync` (`NetworkSessionLifecycle` then ends the session) and Quit runs `ApplicationExit.Quit`, each behind a confirmation for an online host ("Leaving ends the game for everyone in it."). `IsOpen`, `IsPaused`, `IsLeaving`. |
| [PlayerHudView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/PlayerHudView.cs) | Passive view on `PlayerHudCanvas`: health fill + "HP n" text, weapon icon with tint when missing. |
| [AbilityBarView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/AbilityBarView.cs) / [AbilitySlotView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/AbilitySlotView.cs) | Passive ability bar at the bottom centre of `PlayerHudCanvas`: six slots (four class, a gap, two weapon), each labelled with its bound key (`SetKey`). Each slot shows the ability's icon, or its name when it has none (all blanks today), or an empty state (dim frame); a radial cooldown fill (from the top, counter-clockwise) with whole seconds remaining (cached strings, no per-frame allocation); and a red failure flash. Slot frames block clicks. `SetAbility` resets the cooldown and flash. |
| [StatusBarView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/StatusBarView.cs) / [StatusIconView](../Assets/Scripts/UI/StatusIconView.cs) | Passive row of eight status icons on `PlayerHudCanvas`, centred just above the ability bar. Each icon: a frame in the status colour, its icon sprite (or its two-letter abbreviation when it has none, as every test status does today), a radial fill darkening as it runs out, and a stack count above 1. Nothing in it blocks clicks; unused icons are inactive. |
| [StatusBarPresenter](../Assets/Scripts/UI/StatusBarPresenter.cs) | Entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`): binds to the local player's `StatusEffects` through `LocalPlayerTracker` (rebinding when it changes, clearing the row without one); on `Changed` shows one icon per active status, buffs then debuffs, up to the row's capacity, with its stacks (independent instances counted together); each frame pushes each icon's elapsed fill (from its longest remaining instance) only when it moved. On a client the remaining times come from the mirror's countdown. |
| [WorldStatusIcons](../Assets/Scripts/UI/WorldStatusIcons.cs) | On the Weasel's `HealthBar` child: four small rounded squares above the health bar (`Overlay` layer, unlit, hidden until used) showing the unit's distinct debuffs in landing order, each in its status colour with its icon sprite or a plain square. Refreshed on `StatusEffects.Changed`, so it works on every machine. Players have no world bar, so no world icons. |
| [AbilityBarPresenter](../Assets/Scripts/UI/AbilityBarPresenter.cs) | Entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`): binds to the local player's `PlayerAbilities` through `LocalPlayerTracker` (rebinding when it changes, clearing the bar without one), refreshes slots on `SlotsChanged` (weapon changes), flashes a slot on `CastFailed` (fading over 0.35 s on `IClock`), and pushes cooldown fill and seconds each frame only when they change. Labels the slots from `IInputBindingLabels` at start and on `BindingsChanged`. |
| [PointerFeedbackPresenter](../Assets/Scripts/UI/PointerFeedbackPresenter.cs) | Entry point (`ITickable`), local only: each frame picks under the pointer with `PointerTargetPicker` and, over a living enemy, shows the hover ring under it and the attack cursor; a move press that is not on an enemy drops a move marker at the cursor, and an attack-move press drops a red one there, over an enemy too. Nothing shows while gameplay input is off, the pointer is over UI, or the local player is missing or dead. |
| [PointerFeedbackLayer](../Assets/Scripts/UI/PointerFeedbackLayer.cs) | On `PointerFeedback` in the Gameplay scene: an `ObjectPool<MoveMarker>` (prewarmed with 4), the `HoverRing` sprite (red `GroundRing` on the `Terrain` sorting layer, order 10) and the attack cursor (`AttackCursor.png`, hotspot at its centre, set with `Cursor.SetCursor` only when it changes and reset on disable). |
| [WorldHealthBar](../Assets/Scripts/UI/WorldHealthBar.cs) | Thin world-space bar above a unit, like League of Legends minion bars: a dark rounded frame (0.66 x 0.075) and a bright red rounded fill (0.62 x 0.04) that empties from the right. Both are 9-sliced `SpriteRenderer`s using `Assets/Textures/UI/RoundedBar.png` (a smooth 32x16 rounded rectangle, 256 PPU, bilinear, 7 px borders; it lives outside `Assets/Sprites` so the `PixelArtTextureDefaults` postprocessor does not force it to 32 PPU point filtering), on the `Overlay` sorting layer with URP's `Sprite-Unlit-Default` material, because the areas' 2D lights do not light `Overlay` and a lit material would draw black. The fill is resized through `SpriteRenderer.size`, so its corners keep their radius. Polls `Health.CurrentHealth` in `LateUpdate` with one integer compare (network syncs set health silently, so events would miss them) and hides while the unit is dead. On the Weasel prefab (and its stress-test variant), so it shows on every machine. |
| [MoveMarker](../Assets/Scripts/UI/MoveMarker.cs) | Pooled ring (`Assets/Prefabs/UI/MoveMarker.prefab`, `GroundRing` sprite on `Terrain` order 10) that shrinks and fades over 0.45 s, green for a move and red for an attack move (`Show(position, attackMove)`). |
| [PlayerHudPresenter](../Assets/Scripts/UI/PlayerHudPresenter.cs) | Entry point; binds to whichever player `LocalPlayerTracker` holds (rebinding when it changes) and listens to its `Health.Damaged`/`Healed`/`Died`/`Restored` and `PlayerWeaponController.EquippedWeaponChanged`, pushing into the view; clears the health display while there is no local player. |
| [WaystonePopupPresenter](../Assets/Scripts/UI/WaystonePopupPresenter.cs) | Entry point: on `WaystoneNotices.Discovered` for the local player, spawns `GameplaySettings.WaystoneDiscoveredText` ("Waystone discovered") in its colour over the player on the `DamagePopupLayer`, which `DamagePopupPresenter` ticks; waystones have no art yet, so this is how a player learns it found one. On this machine only. |

UI text uses the static `LiberationSans SDF` font asset, which is also TMP's default for new text. Its dynamic `LiberationSans SDF - Fallback` only gains glyphs (and a git diff) when text uses characters outside the static atlas; nothing should reference the fallback directly.

Damage popups are under Combat.

## Player

Prefab: [Player.prefab](../Assets/Prefabs/Player/Player.prefab) (`PlayerController` with `PlayerControlSettings`, `PlayerMotor2D`, `PlayerWeaponController`, `PlayerAbilities` with `Class_Placeholder`, `Health` (100 HP), `DamageReceiver` (`Profile_Player`), `DisableOnDeath`, `StatusEffects`, `StatusVisuals`, `NetworkObject`, `NetworkTransform` with owner authority syncing x/y position only (`LegacyLerp`, unreliable deltas, see Transform smoothing), `PlayerNetworkSync`, `NetworkHealth`, `NetworkStatusEffects`, `PlayerWorldSync`, `YPositionSorter` on a child), referenced by `GameplayLifetimeScope`, spawned at runtime by `PlayerSpawner`, and listed in `Assets/DefaultNetworkPrefabs.asset`. Offline the networking components stay unspawned and do nothing.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [PlayerController](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerController.cs) | Wires the command source, `PlayerOrders`, `PlayerMotor2D` and the weapon. Each `Update` (internal `Tick`): read a `PlayerCommand`, tick the orders (which start casts on its `PlayerAbilities`) with the motor's state, the clock and the equipped weapon's `AttackRange`, then apply the output (`MoveTo`/`Stop` on the motor, `SetAim`, `TryAttack(aim)` on swing requests). Before ticking it passes its `StatusEffects` controls into the order context and their `MovementScale` (slow; 0 while stunned or rooted) to the motor's `SetSpeedScale`; on a client that is the owner's mirror of the host's statuses. Remote copies (`SetSimulatesMovement(false)`) do nothing in `Tick` and only show replicated movement. Death (`OnDisable`, through `DisableOnDeath`) and `Teleport` clear the orders, so a respawn starts Idle. No source on a simulated player = idle, with an error logged in `Start`. Keeps `SetCommandSource`, `CurrentMove`, `FacingDirection`, `SimulatesMovement`, `Face`, `Teleport`; adds `ShowRemoteMovement`, `ClearOrders`, `CurrentOrder`, `ControlSettings`. Gizmos (Debug toggle) for the ability side of its orders: a line and box to the target a CastWhenInRange is approaching, labelled with the slot number and ability, and a "buffered" label under the player. |
| [PlayerOrders](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerOrders.cs) | Plain class: the order state machine (Idle, Move, Attack, AttackMove, CastWhenInRange, Casting, plus a paused order and a buffered cast), see Player orders below. [PlayerOrderTypes.cs](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerOrderTypes.cs) holds `PlayerOrderKind`, `PlayerMotorRequest`, the `PlayerOrderContext` input and the `PlayerOrderOutput` (motor request + destination, aim, swing). |
