| [PlayerMotor2D](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerMotor2D.cs) | Owns a `PathFollower2D` (configured from `PlayerControlSettings`, straight-line shortcut on). `MoveTo(point)` paths on its own navigation, `PlayerNavigation.Of(transform)` (its world's `WorldNavigation`; the follower is reconfigured when that changes), with partial paths allowed (an unwalkable point ends at the nearest walkable cell; a point beyond the loaded chunks ends at the loaded edge; on a standalone grid, as in tests, a point with no walkable cell within the grid's own nearest-cell search radius is walked back along the line toward the player until a walkable cell); without navigation (a player in no world) it goes in a straight line. `FixedUpdate` sets `Rigidbody2D.linearVelocity` from the follower (`Time.fixedDeltaTime`) at the settings' move speed times a speed scale (`SetSpeedScale`; the follower gets the scaled speed, so at 0 the body holds still, stall time stays 0 and the path is kept), facing follows movement. `Update` drives the animator (`IsMoving`, `MoveX/Y`, `LastMoveX/Y`, walk speed). `Stop`, `Face`, `SetAim(aiming, direction)` (while aiming the player faces the aim and movement does not turn it), `Teleport`, `ReachedDestination`, `StalledTime`, `CurrentMove`, `FacingDirection`; `ShowRemoteMovement(move, facing)` for remote copies, whose body it never moves. Sets up the body (no gravity, frozen rotation, interpolation) in `Awake`. |
| [PlayerControlSettings](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerControlSettings.cs) | ScriptableObject (`Assets/Data/PlayerControlSettings.asset`): move speed 5, waypoint reach and arrival 0.1, terrain profile `TerrainMovement_Default`, stuck timeout 0.75 s, hold re-evaluation 0.15 s, chase repath 0.5 s or when the target moved 0.5, cast buffer window 0.4 s, attack-move acquire range 3, pick radius 0.35, enemy layers `Enemy`, ally layers `Player`. `FollowerSettings` builds the path follower settings. |
| [PlayerCommand](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerCommand.cs) / [IPlayerCommandSource](../Assets/Scripts/Player/IPlayerCommandSource.cs) | One frame of intent in world space (`PointerWorld`, `Target` under the pointer, `MovePressed`, `MoveHeld`, `StopPressed`, `HasAbility`/`AbilitySlot` (the lowest slot pressed this frame), `AttackMovePressed`; `default` is "nothing") and where the local player gets it from. Only the local player reads commands. |
| [LocalPlayerCommandSource](../Assets/Scripts/Player/LocalPlayerCommandSource.cs) | Builds commands from `IPlayerInput` with the Main-scope `Camera` (screen to world) and `PointerTargetPicker` (only while the move button is pressed or held, or an ability key is pressed). A press over UI is dropped along with the hold that follows it; a hold that started on the world keeps steering over UI. The attack-move key, like the ability keys, is passed on wherever the pointer is. Nothing while gameplay input is disabled. `PlayerBinder` gives it to the local player only. |
| [PointerTargetPicker](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PointerTargetPicker.cs) | `Pick(worldPoint)`: non-allocating `Physics2D.OverlapCircle` within the pick radius on the enemy + ally layers (no triggers), returning the living unit whose collider centre is closest. Enemies need a `DamageReceiver` (alive `Health`); allies must be registered, living players. No line-of-sight test. The collider to `Health` lookup for enemies is cached by an [EnemyHealthLookup](../Assets/Scripts/Player/EnemyHealthLookup.cs) (up to 64 colliders, cleared when full; destroyed entries are looked up again), so the hover pick every frame does no component lookups once warm. |
| [UnitTarget](../Assets/Scripts/Player/UnitTarget.cs) | A picked or ordered unit: `Health` (identity), `Collider`, `UnitTeam` (`None`, `Enemy`, `Ally`). |
| [IUnitQueries](../Assets/Scripts/Player/IUnitQueries.cs) / [PlayerUnitQueries](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerUnitQueries.cs) | What orders ask about a target: alive, position, and collider-to-collider distance from the player (`Collider2D.Distance`, zero when overlapping or when the target is the player's own collider, as for an ally cast on oneself, which Unity refuses to measure; centre distance when a collider is missing or disabled); and `NearestEnemyInRange(point, range)` for attack moves: a non-allocating `Physics2D.OverlapCircle` around the player's collider on the settings' enemy layers (no triggers), keeping living enemies (through its own `EnemyHealthLookup`) within `range` by collider distance, and returning the one whose position is closest to `point`. |
| [PlayerWeaponController](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerWeaponController.cs) | Holds the equipped `PlayerWeapon`; `TryAttack(direction)` checks the `IClock` cooldown, spawns a slash toward the direction (the facing direction when it is zero) via `SlashSpawner` and raises `Attacked(direction)`. `PlayRemoteAttack(direction)` replays another machine's swing without a cooldown or event. Event `EquippedWeaponChanged`. |
| [PlayerHandle](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerHandle.cs) | One player as other systems see it: `Transform`, `Health`, `IsAlive`. |
| [IPlayerRegistry](../Assets/Scripts/Player/IPlayerRegistry.cs) / [PlayerRegistry](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerRegistry.cs) | Every player in the session: `Players`, `Contains`, `AnyAlive`, events `PlayerAdded`/`PlayerRemoved`. `Add`/`Remove` (ignore null and duplicates) are on the concrete class only. Used by mobs and death handling. |
| [PlayerSpawner](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerSpawner.cs) | Instantiates the player prefab through `IObjectResolver` (injecting its components) and moves it into the Gameplay scene so it survives trips. `SpawnLocalPlayer`: offline binds it as the local player directly; when hosting, spawns it as the host's network player object. `SpawnRemotePlayer(clientId, world, position)` (host only): places a player owned by that client on a free entry slot at the position (`EntrySlots`, so players joining at one entry stand apart), gives its `PlayerWorldSync` the world to start in and spawns it, unless the client already has one. `PlayerNetworkPrefab`, `PlayersScene`. |
| [GameplayPlayers](../Assets/Scripts/Player/GameplayPlayers.cs) | Gameplay entry point. Offline: spawns the local player. Online: `RegisterNetworkPrefabs` (build callback) registers the player prefab with the session so client copies are injected from this scope and placed in the Gameplay scene; in `Start` the host spawns its own player, one for every joined client and then each newly joined client, where `PlayerStarts` places it by its player id, once the world session has loaded that world around the place (`IWorldSession.PrepareAsync`, at once when it already is; one wait per client, dropped when the client leaves or the scope goes); a client sends joined (`NetworkSession.NotifyJoined`). Unregisters on dispose. |
| [PlayerBinder](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerBinder.cs) | `BindLocal(controller)`: local command source, movement on, registers, assigns it to `LocalPlayerTracker`. `BindRemote(controller)`: no command source, movement off, registers. `Unbind(handle)`. It places nobody: the world session does (its start, trips, respawns). |
| [LocalPlayerTracker](../Assets/Scripts/Player/LocalPlayerTracker.cs) | The current `LocalPlayer` (null until this machine's player exists) and a `Changed` event. |
| [PlayerNetworkSync](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerNetworkSync.cs) | `NetworkBehaviour` on the player. On spawn the owner binds as local and forwards `Attacked` through `AttackRpc` (owner-invoked, runs on everyone else, replays with `PlayRemoteAttack`); other machines bind it as remote, make its body kinematic without interpolation, and every frame pass the owner-written `move`/`facing` `NetworkVariable`s to `PlayerController.ShowRemoteMovement`, which drives the copy's animation (owner updates them when they change by more than 0.01; `move` is the motor's `CurrentMove`). The owner also forwards `PlayerAbilities.CastStarted` as `CastRpc(slot, point, direction, target, hasTarget)` (owner-invoked, runs on everyone else; the target is a `NetworkObjectReference` resolved with this object's `NetworkManager`, an ally when it has a `PlayerController`, otherwise an enemy). Receivers call `PlayerAbilities.PlayRemoteCast`, which only the host's `AbilityService` applies. When a client's own cast completes, the owner sends `CastCompletedRpc(slot, point, direction, target, hasTarget)` to the server only, with the owner's own position at completion, which calls `PlayerAbilities.ResolveRemoteCast` so the host's `AbilityService` resolves its effect around where the owner was rather than around the host's lagging copy; the host's own player resolves directly. An interrupted cast sends nothing, so it never lands. the copy's facing comes from the replicated `facing` (the owner aims during the cast), as it does for `AttackRpc`. The owner's teleports (`PlayerController.Teleported`: arriving at an entry, a session's start) go through `NetworkTransform.Teleport`, so the other machines snap the copy there instead of interpolating, which across worlds would slide it thousands of units. `Handle` (internal): the player's registry handle once bound. Unbinds on despawn. |
| [PlayerWorldSync](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerWorldSync.cs) | `NetworkBehaviour` on the player, its `ITripLink` and `IWaystoneLink`. In `OnNetworkPostSpawn` (once `PlayerNetworkSync` bound the player): the host puts the player in the world `SetSpawnWorld` gave it (a client's player) and writes a server-only `NetworkVariable<PlayerWorldState>` (world id, trip count, trip entry) from `PlayerWorlds` now and on every change, telling `IClientReadiness.BeginTrip` about a client's player; every client applies it to its own `PlayerWorlds` (`Apply`; an unlisted world id logs an error), and the owner hands a new trip to `NetworkTripAuthority.Deliver` with the entry's position. `SendTripRequest(exitId, position)` (owner) sends `RequestTripRpc` to the host, which checks it through `NetworkTripAuthority.Approve`. `SendWaystoneDiscovered(entry)` (host, for a client's player) sends `WaystoneDiscoveredRpc` to the owner, which raises it on its `WaystoneNotices`. A respawn reaches the owner as a trip like any other. `State` (internal). |
| [LocalPlayer](../Assets/Scripts/Player/LocalPlayer.cs) | The player this machine controls: `Controller`, `Weapon`, `Handle`, `Transform`. Reached through `LocalPlayerTracker` by the camera, HUD and the stress spawner. |
| [PlayerDeathHandler](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerDeathHandler.cs) | Entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`); does nothing on a client (`IGameAuthority`). Listens to `Health.Died` of every registered player (follows `PlayerAdded`/`PlayerRemoved`) and runs each dead player's respawn on its own (WorldStreaming.md's Death and respawn): `GameplaySettings.RespawnDelaySeconds` of game time (`IClock`) after the death, and once a trip the player was on has arrived, `PlayerRespawner` sends it to its waystone; while it travels there it stays dead, and once it stands there it is restored. A player in no world, or in a world without entries, is restored where it stands. No rule looks at the other players: nothing restarts when every player is dead at once. A player leaving the registry, or alive again, is forgotten; `PendingCount` and `IsTravellingToWaystone` (internal) for tests. |
| [PlayerRespawner](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerRespawner.cs) | What a respawn does, where game state is decided. `TryFindWaystone(player)`: the player's world and the waystone it respawns at (`WaystoneRules.TryFindNearest` from where it stands, among the waystones its player id discovered in the `SessionSave`, `PlayerIds`). `TrySendToWaystone(player, out respawn)`: a trip within its world to that waystone through `NetworkTripAuthority.Send` (the arrival held, so this machine streams the waystone's chunks at once in whatever world, the player put in the world for one more trip, `Approved` raised: the host's own player travels through `LocalTripTraveller`, a client's hears of it through its networked world and fades, moves itself, waits for the chunks and reports them with the new trip count). `HasArrived(player, respawn)`: in that world within two cells of the entry (`TripArrivals.HasArrived`), the entry's chunk loaded here and, for a client's player, held by its client (`IClientReadiness.IsClientReady(owner, chunk)`). `TryRestore(player)`: `Health.Restore()` for a dead registered player, which re-enables it through `DisableOnDeath` and reaches every machine through `NetworkHealth`; it moves nobody. `IsTravelling(player)`: a trip under way (`TripArrivals.IsHeld`). |

Config: [GameplaySettings](../Assets/Scripts/Composition/GameplaySettings.cs) (`Assets/Data/GameplaySettings.asset`): respawn delay (3 s of game time), entry slot spacing (0.6, `EntrySlots`), autosave interval (60 s of game time, `GameSaveRecorder`), and under Waystones the discovery reach (2 cells along each axis, `WaystoneDiscovery`) and the discovery popup's text ("Waystone discovered") and colour (`WaystonePopupPresenter`).

### Player orders

[PlayerOrders](../Assets/Scripts/Player/PlayerOrders.cs) runs on the machine that moves the player (the owner). It holds one current order, at most one paused order while a cast runs, and at most one buffered cast. Each tick: Stop; a move press (or a held button every `HoldReevaluateInterval`) issues an order; an attack-move press issues an attack move (after a move press on the same tick, so it wins); an ability press is handled; a buffered cast older than `CastBufferWindow` is dropped; then the current order runs. A held button only keeps steering a move or attack: unlike a press, it never replaces a CastWhenInRange approach or drops a buffered cast (the camera follows the player, so the cursor's world point changes at every re-evaluation). One `fresh` flag marks the order that has not started yet (so a move ignores the motor's stale arrival); it is set for new orders, for the order resumed after a cast, and for the paused order when a buffered cast fires, so a buffered `Continue` cast restarts the paused move instead of reading the stopped motor as arrived. The output's aim is zero whenever there is no aim, so a swing at a target on top of the player falls back to the facing.

| Order | Issued by | Runs | Ends |
|---|---|---|---|
| Idle | Stop, or an order ending | Nothing | Any new order |
| Move(point) | Press (or hold) on the ground, an ally, or a dead enemy | `MoveTo(point)` once; the arrival check is skipped on the tick it is issued, because the motor still reports the previous path | Motor reached its destination, or stalled for `StuckTimeout` |
| Attack(target) | Press (or hold) on a living enemy; re-issuing the same target keeps the chase as it is | In `AttackRange` (collider distance): `Stop` once, aim at the target, request a swing every tick (the weapon cooldown decides). Out of range: `MoveTo(target)` when the chase starts, every `RepathInterval`, or once the target moved `TargetMoveRepathDistance` from the last goal. Walls and distance never cancel it. | The target dies or is destroyed |
| AttackMove(point) | The attack-move key, at the cursor's point | Without a target, every tick it takes the living enemy within `AttackMoveAcquireRange` of the player (collider distance) that is closest to the point (`IUnitQueries.NearestEnemyInRange`); with none, it walks to the point like a Move. With a target it engages it exactly like Attack (chase, stop once in range, aim, swing). Taking a target or losing it restarts the movement (the order is fresh again), so after a kill it walks on at once, ignoring the motor's stale arrival, or takes the next enemy in range. Paused during a `Continue` cast it walks, takes targets and chases, but never swings. | Arrived at the point, or stalled for `StuckTimeout`, with nothing in range |
| CastWhenInRange(slot, target) | A `Unit` cast whose target is beyond the ability's range | Chases the target like Attack | In range: the cast starts (and this order is gone, so the cast resumes into Idle). Target lost: Idle, reported as `NoTarget`. Another order replaces it. |
| Casting(slot, aim) | A cast that started | Faces the aim (unless the ability targets `None`). `Stop`/`Ability` movement: the motor is stopped at the start. `Continue`: the paused order keeps running its movement (a paused attack chases but never swings). | After `CastTime` (instant casts end on the tick they start): a buffered cast fires if it is still valid, keeping the paused order paused; otherwise the paused order resumes as a fresh order (a move repaths, an attack re-chases). |

Ability presses (`PressAbility`):
1. The caster's `CanCast` fails (`EmptySlot`, `OnCooldown`, `Dead`), or crowd control blocks the ability (`Silenced`; `Rooted` for `CastMovement.Ability`): reported, nothing else happens. The same control check runs again whenever a cast is about to start (an approach in range, a buffered cast).
2. The aim is built from the command: `None` ignores it; `Direction` aims at the cursor; `Point` clamps the cursor to the range; `Unit` needs the unit under the cursor to be alive and accepted by the unit filter (`Enemy`, `Ally` (registered players, the caster included) or `Any`), otherwise `NoTarget`.
3. During a cast: a non-bufferable ability fails with `CastInProgress`; a bufferable one becomes the buffered cast with that aim, replacing (and reporting as `Cancelled`) an older one.
4. Otherwise the cast starts at once (`IAbilityCaster.TryCast`), pausing the current order, or turns into CastWhenInRange when a unit is out of range.

Rules: a new order replaces the current one, except during a cast, when it replaces the paused order instead; a click (not a hold) drops the buffered cast (`Cancelled`). Stop drops the buffered cast and, during a cast, the paused order; the started cast itself runs to its end, then the player is Idle. A buffered cast is re-checked when it fires (cooldown, a dead unit target: reported and dropped, and the paused order resumes); a buffered unit cast whose target moved out of range becomes CastWhenInRange, replacing the paused order. An empty command (gameplay input disabled during transitions) makes no new order and lets the current one run on. Crowd control arrives in `PlayerOrderContext.Controls`: when a stun starts the orders `Clear` and ask the motor to stop once, and while it lasts commands are ignored, ability presses report `Stunned` and nothing swings (the player is Idle afterwards); when a silence starts a running cast is cancelled (its cooldown stays spent, its effect never lands), the buffered cast is dropped (`Cancelled`) and the paused order resumes; a root changes nothing in the orders, since the motor holds still without stalling, so a move or chase waits for it to end. A cast whose cast time runs out completes (`IAbilityCaster.CompleteCast`); one that is stopped early is cancelled (`CancelCast`). `Clear` (death, teleports, becoming a remote copy) cancels a running cast, drops the buffered one and every order without a motor request; the controller stops the motor itself.

## Abilities

Abilities that go through the whole pipeline (cooldown, cast time, movement during the cast, buffering, networking). Four have real effects, which land when their cast completes (see below); the two movement abilities are still blanks. Cooldowns, orders and cast state live on the owner only and are never replicated; the loadout is the prefab default on every machine, so a slot index identifies an ability on the wire.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [AbilityDefinition](../Assets/Scripts/Abilities/AbilityDefinition.cs) | ScriptableObject: display name, icon, cooldown (from cast start), cast time (0 = instant), `AbilityTargeting` (`None`, `Direction`, `Point`, `Unit`), `AbilityUnitFilter` (`Enemy`, `Ally`, `Any`; `Accepts(team)`), range, `CastMovement` (`Stop`, `Continue`, `Ability`, which stands still until abilities can move the caster), bufferable, and an effect: a `Hit` (damage, type, statuses), a heal amount (`HasEffect`) and an area radius (`None`: around the caster, 0 = the caster only; `Point`: around the point; `Direction`: half-width of a line `range` long; unused for `Unit`). `Create(...)` for tests. |
| [AbilityTypes.cs](../Assets/Scripts/Abilities/AbilityTypes.cs) | The enums above, `CastOutcome` (`Started`, `EmptySlot`, `OnCooldown`, `CastInProgress`, `NoTarget`, `Dead`, `Expired`, `Cancelled`, `Stunned`, `Silenced`, `Rooted`), `CastAim` (point, direction, optional `UnitTarget`) and `AbilityCast` (slot, ability, aim). |
| [PlayerClass](../Assets/Scripts/Abilities/PlayerClass.cs) | ScriptableObject: display name and exactly four abilities (slots 1–4). [AbilitySlotArrays](../Assets/Scripts/Abilities/AbilitySlotArrays.cs) keeps class and weapon lists at their fixed size. |
| [AbilityCooldowns](../Assets/Scripts/Abilities/AbilityCooldowns.cs) | Plain class: per-slot ready times on the clock, `IsReady`, `Remaining`, `Start`. |
| [IAbilityCaster](../Assets/Scripts/Abilities/IAbilityCaster.cs) | What orders need: `GetAbility`, `CanCast` (empty, cooldown, dead), `TryCast`, `CompleteCast` (the cast time ran out), `CancelCast` (stopped early), `ReportFailure`. Whether a cast is already running is decided by the orders. |
| [PlayerAbilities](../Assets/Scripts/Abilities/PlayerAbilities.cs) | Component (implements `IAbilityCaster`): builds the six slots from its `PlayerClass` and the equipped weapon's two abilities, rebuilding on `EquippedWeaponChanged` and in `OnEnable`. `TryCast(slot, aim)` fails without side effects (raising `CastFailed`) when the slot is empty, on cooldown, dead or another cast runs; otherwise starts the cooldown, raises `CastStarted` and hands the cast to `AbilityService.Apply`. `CompleteCast` raises `CastEnded` and `CastCompleted` (with the cast as started) and has `AbilityService.Resolve` apply its effect; `CancelCast` only raises `CastEnded`. `ResolveRemoteCast(slot, aim, casterPosition)` resolves a cast a client completed, under the `PlayRemoteCast` rules. `RemainingCooldown(slot)` feeds the HUD, `SlotsChanged`. `PlayRemoteCast(slot, aim)` replays another machine's cast into `AbilityService` with no cooldown or events, ignoring it when this machine sees the caster as dead or a `Unit` cast's target as missing (it despawned or did not resolve) or dead. Logs an error in `Start` without an injected service. Gizmos (Debug toggles): the last 8 casts, local and remote, are kept in a fixed ring; each shows its aim while its cast time runs and fades over `castGizmoSeconds` (1 s) after, in a per-slot colour (`None`: a circle round the caster; `Direction`: a 2-unit ray; `Point`: a line to the point; `Unit`: a line to the target), dimmer for remote casts, with a label stack above the head (slot number 1–6, name, cast time left, "(remote)"). When selected, the range of every `Point` and `Unit` ability is a circle labelled with its slot number on its right edge. |
| [AbilityService](../Assets/Scripts/Abilities/AbilityService.cs) | Gameplay-scope singleton mirroring `DamageService`: `Apply(caster, cast)` (cast started) and `Resolve(caster, cast, casterPosition)` (cast completed; the position is where circles around the caster and lines from it start) act only where `IGameAuthority.IsAuthoritative`. `Apply` raises `CastApplied` and nothing else. `Resolve` picks the affected units by targeting: the `Unit` target; the caster (`None` with radius 0) or every unit in a circle around it; every unit in a circle around the aimed `Point`; or every unit in a capsule from the caster along the aim `Direction`, `range` long (none without a direction). Areas use non-allocating `Physics2D` overlaps on `CombatSettings.UnitLayers` (no triggers), each unit counted once. Each unit is healed through `DamageService.ApplyHeal` and hit through `HitService`, with the caster as the source, so damage and debuffs only land on enemies and heals and buffs on allies. Then it raises `CastResolved`. Offline and host-owned casts reach both from `PlayerAbilities`; a client's cast reaches `Apply` through `CastRpc` and `Resolve` through `CastCompletedRpc`, so each cast is applied once and resolved at most once, on the host. |

Blanks (`Assets/Data/Abilities`, used by `Assets/Data/Classes/Class_Placeholder.asset` and the sword):

| Slot | Ability | Targeting | Cooldown | Cast time | Movement | Bufferable |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q | Blank Bolt | Direction | 2 s | 0.25 s | Stop | no |
| W | Blank Leap | Point, range 5 | 4 s | instant | Stop | yes |
| E | Blank Mend | Unit, allies, range 4 | 6 s | 0.5 s | Stop | no |
| R | Blank Smite | Unit, enemies, range 3 | 10 s | 1 s | Continue | no |
| 1 (sword) | Blank Whirl | None | 1.5 s | 0.3 s | Continue | yes |
| 2 (sword) | Blank Lunge | Direction | 5 s | 0.2 s | Ability | no |

Effects (when the cast completes): Blank Bolt deals 12 Frost and applies Chill along a 6-unit line 0.7 wide; Blank Whirl deals 10 Physical to every enemy within 1.5 of the caster; Blank Smite deals 15 Physical and applies Stun; Blank Mend heals 25 and applies Fortify. Blank Leap and Blank Lunge stay blank until abilities can move the caster. The asset and display names still say "Blank".

## Combat

### Health and damage

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [Health](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/Health.cs) | Int HP; `ApplyDamage(amount, source, type, flags)` clamps, returns applied amount; events `Damaged(Health.DamageEvent)` (amount, source, HP before and after, type, flags) and `Died(Health)` (once per death). `Heal(amount)` adds up to max HP, never revives a dead unit, and raises `Healed(Health, added)`. `Restore()` refills to max and raises `Restored(Health)` (no-op at full health); a restored `Health` can die again. `SyncTo(value)` sets HP (clamped) for network sync without `Damaged`, but raises `Died` when it takes a living unit to 0, so death reactions (`DisableOnDeath`, `DestroyMobOnDeath`) also run on machines that only learn the result. Coming back to life always goes through `Restore`. |
| [NetworkHealth](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/NetworkHealth.cs) | `NetworkBehaviour` on players and mobs. Host: mirrors HP into a server-written `NetworkVariable` and sends `HitRpc(amount, type, flags, healthAfter)` on `Health.Damaged` and on `DamageReceiver.ImmuneHit` (amount 0), `HealedRpc(amount, healthAfter)` on `Health.Healed` and `RestoredRpc` on `Restored`. It forwards `Health.Damaged` rather than a service event because that fires before `Died`, so the RPC leaves before a dying mob despawns. Clients: sync HP from the variable on spawn (a unit that arrives dead, e.g. a teammate awaiting respawn when someone joins, dies there through `SyncTo`), replay hits and heals through `DamageService.ApplyReplicatedHit`/`ApplyReplicatedHeal` (so `Damaged`/`Died`, HUD and popups work as on the host) and correct to `healthAfter`, and `Restore` on `RestoredRpc`. |
| [DamageReceiver](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DamageReceiver.cs) | Marks something as hittable: `FindFor(transform)` walks up parents; exposes its `Health`, `PopupWorldPosition`, its `CombatProfile`, `Faction` (`None` without a profile), `StatusImmunities`, `Statuses` (its `StatusEffects`, or null), `GetDefense(type)` (profile resistance and immunity combined with its statuses' resistance deltas, granted immunities and damage-taken multiplier) and `DamageDealtMultiplier` (from its statuses). Event `ImmuneHit(type, flags)`, raised by `DamageService` on the authority for hits it was immune to. Applies nothing itself. |
| [DamageService](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DamageService.cs) | Gameplay-scope singleton (with `CombatSettings`) and the only gameplay path that changes HP. `ApplyDamage(receiver, amount, type, source, flags)` returns a `DamageResult`; it rejects (`default`) null or dead targets, non-positive amounts, every hit on a machine without `IGameAuthority` (a client) and a source of the target's own faction (found with `GetComponentInParent<DamageReceiver>` on the source; no faction on either side means no rule). Otherwise it resolves through `DamageMath` with the source unit's damage-dealt multiplier and the target's defence, raises `ImmuneHit` for immune hits, applies to `Health` and publishes a `DamageReport` (immune hits included, with amount 0). The result's amount is the HP actually lost. `ApplyPeriodicDamage(receiver, amount, type, source, dealtMultiplier)` does the same for a damage-over-time tick, with the multiplier captured when its status landed and the `Periodic` flag. `ApplyHeal(receiver, amount, source)` heals a living target unless the source is of another faction and publishes a `HealReport`. Slashes play on every machine but only the host's (or the offline game's) apply damage. `ApplyReplicatedHit`/`ApplyReplicatedHeal` apply and publish what the host already decided, regardless of authority (used by `NetworkHealth` on clients). Tests still call `Health.ApplyDamage` directly to set up state. |
| [DamageReport](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DamageReport.cs) | Target, amount (HP lost), type, flags, source, popup world position; `IsImmune`. [HealReport](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/HealReport.cs): target, amount added, source, popup world position. |
| [CombatEvents](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/CombatEvents.cs) | Gameplay-scope event bus: `DamageApplied(DamageReport)`, `HealApplied(HealReport)`, `StatusBlocked(StatusReport)` (`PublishBlocked`). |
| [Hit](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/Hit.cs) | Serializable struct: amount, damage type and the statuses it applies (`StatusCount`, `GetStatus`). |
| [HitService](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/HitService.cs) | Gameplay-scope singleton: `ApplyHit(receiver, hit, source)` applies the damage through `DamageService` and then, if the target survived, each status through `StatusEffectService`, each under its own faction rule (so a damaging hit's buffs still land on allies whose damage is refused, and its debuffs only on enemies). Returns whether the hit reached the target: its damage resolved (an immune hit included) or a status landed, stacked, refreshed or was blocked by an immunity. Used by `SwordSlashAttack`, `MeleeDamageDealer` (which record a hit or start the cooldown only when it reached) and `AbilityService`; it keeps the two services independent. |
| [CombatFactions](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/CombatFactions.cs) | Static: `UnitOf(source)` (the `DamageReceiver` on it or a parent), `SameFaction` (blocks hostile effects), `OtherFaction` (blocks helpful ones); a side without a faction is exempt. |
| [DamageTypes.cs](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DamageTypes.cs) | `DamageType` (`Physical`, `Fire`, `Frost`, `Lightning`, `Poison`, `True`; serialized, append-only), `DamageTypeMask` (flags, `Includes(type)`), `DamageFlags` (`Immune`, `Resisted`, `Weakness`, `Periodic`), `Faction` (`None`, `Players`, `Mobs`). |
| [DamageMath](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DamageMath.cs) | Static, pure: `Resolve(raw, type, dealt, DefenseSnapshot, CombatSettings, flags)`. Immune: 0 with `Immune`. Otherwise resistance clamped to the settings' floor and cap (0 for `True`), `raw x dealt x (1 - resistance/100) x taken`, rounded half away from zero, at least the minimum damage; `Resisted`/`Weakness` by the resistance's sign. See [DamageAndStatusEffects.md](DamageAndStatusEffects.md#resolving-a-hit). |
| [DefenseSnapshot](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DefenseSnapshot.cs) / [DamageResult](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DamageResult.cs) | A unit's defence against one type (resistance, immunity, damage-taken multiplier); a resolved hit (type, raw amount, amount, flags, `IsImmune`, `Resolved` = dealt damage or immune; `default` = rejected). |
| [CombatProfile](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/CombatProfile.cs) | ScriptableObject: faction, [DamageResistance](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DamageResistance.cs) entries (type, percent in [-100, 100]; entries for one type add up), damage immunities, status immunity tags. `Create(...)` for tests. Assets `Assets/Data/Combat/Profile_Player.asset` (`Players`) and `Profile_Weasel.asset` (`Mobs`), no resistances yet. |
| [CombatSettings](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/CombatSettings.cs) | ScriptableObject (`Assets/Data/Combat/CombatSettings.asset`): resistance floor -100 and cap 80, minimum damage 1, the unit layers area effects search (`Player` and `Enemy`), the `StatusEffectCatalog`, the periodic popup scale 0.75, a popup colour per damage type, heal and immune colours, the immune text. |
| [MeleeDamageDealer](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/MeleeDamageDealer.cs) | Mob melee: `TryDealDamage(target)` with `IClock` cooldown, through `HitService` (logs an error and deals nothing without one); damage/interval overwritten from `MobConfig`, damage type and statuses (`AttackDamageType`, `AttackStatuses`; none on `Mob_Default`) taken from it (the statuses copied into the component's own array), applied as a `Hit`. A hit that reached the target (an immune one included) starts the cooldown, so an immune target is not reported every frame. |

### Player weapon and slash

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [PlayerWeapon](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/PlayerWeapon.cs) | ScriptableObject: name, damage (20 on the sword), damage type (`Physical` on the sword), on-hit statuses (none on the sword) and `Hit` built from them, cooldown, attack range for auto attacks (collider-to-collider, 0.3 on the sword), exactly two abilities (the A and S slots; the sword has Blank Whirl and Blank Lunge), spawn distance, per-direction offsets (Down, Up, Left, Right), slash prefab, HUD icon. Asset: `Assets/Data/Weapons/Sword.asset`. |
| [SlashSpawner](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/SlashSpawner.cs) | Instantiates the weapon's slash prefab through `IObjectResolver` and initializes it with the `HitService`. |
| [SwordSlashAttack](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/SwordSlashAttack.cs) | Trigger hitbox that follows the owner's sprite, rotates to the attack direction (the clip is drawn facing up), mirrors when facing east, sorts one order behind the owner's sprite when swinging up (so the arc passes behind the head) and one in front otherwise, plays its clip through a `PlayableGraph`, decides which `DamageReceiver`s it hit (each once, never the owner; an immune hit counts as hit) and applies the weapon's `Hit` (damage, type, on-hit statuses) through `HitService`, checks initial overlaps, self-destroys after the clip length. Prefab: `Assets/Prefabs/Combat/SwordSlash.prefab`. |

### Death

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [DisableOnDeath](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DisableOnDeath.cs) | Player: on death disables every other enabled MonoBehaviour except `Health`/`DamageReceiver` and networking components, then `DeathPhysics.Disable`; on `Health.Restored` re-enables exactly the behaviours and colliders it turned off and restores the Rigidbody2D's `simulated` flag. |
| [DestroyMobOnDeath](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DestroyMobOnDeath.cs) | Mobs: disables physics and spawns the `MobDeathAnimation` template via `EffectSpawner` on every machine. With authority it despawns the mob (destroying it everywhere) or, offline, destroys it; on a client it only hides the mob's renderers until the host's despawn arrives. |
| [DeathPhysics](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DeathPhysics.cs) | Static helper: disable colliders, zero and unsimulate the Rigidbody2D. |
| [MobDeathAnimation](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/MobDeathAnimation.cs) | Copies the dead mob's sprite renderer settings/scale, plays `MobDeath.anim` via `PlayableGraph`, self-destroys. Prefab: `Assets/Prefabs/Combat/MobDeathAnimation.prefab`. |

### Damage popups

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [DamagePopupPresenter](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DamagePopupPresenter.cs) | Entry point: `CombatEvents.DamageApplied` -> `DamagePopupLayer.Spawn` with the amount in the type's colour, or the immune text in the immune colour; `HealApplied` -> "+amount" in the heal colour; damage-over-time hits at `PeriodicPopupScale` (0.75) of the size; `StatusBlocked` -> the immune text (colours from `CombatSettings`); `Tick` advances popups, `PostLateTick` re-projects them after the camera moved. |
| [DamagePopupLayer](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/DamagePopupLayer.cs) | On `DamagePopupCanvas`; `ObjectPool<FloatingDamageText>` with prewarm; releases popups past their lifetime. |
| [FloatingDamageText](../Assets/Scripts/Combat/FloatingDamageText.cs) | TMP text (message and colour given by the caller) that rises and fades; world-to-canvas projection. Prefab: `Assets/Prefabs/UI/DamagePopup.prefab`. |

## Status effects

Specified in [DamageAndStatusEffects.md](DamageAndStatusEffects.md). The host applies, ticks, expires and removes statuses; clients mirror them. Nothing applies statuses in play yet (Phase 5 adds a dev tool and the first ability effects). Crowd control reaches players through `PlayerController` (orders and motor speed scale) and mobs through `MobController`.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [StatusTypes.cs](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusTypes.cs) | `StatusTags` (flags: `Stun`, `Root`, `Silence`, `Slow`, `Burn`, `Chill`, `Poison`, `Bleed`; append-only), `StatusControls` (flags: `Stun`, `Root`, `Silence`), `StatusKind` (`Buff`, `Debuff`), `StatusStacking` (`Refresh`, `AddStack`, `Independent`), `StatusPeriodic` (`None`, `Damage`, `Heal`), `StatusApplyOutcome` (`Landed`, `Refreshed`, `Stacked`, `Immune`, `WrongFaction`, `TargetDead`, `NoAuthority`, `Invalid`). |
| [StatusEffectDefinition](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusEffectDefinition.cs) | ScriptableObject: display name, icon, description, colour (HUD frame, world icon and visual tint), optional `StatusVisual` prefab, `Abbreviation` (first two letters of the name, cached), kind, tags, duration, stacking and max stacks, periodic effect (amount per tick and stack, damage type, tick interval), move speed, damage dealt and taken multipliers, controls, resistance deltas, granted damage immunities and status immunities. `Create(...)` for tests. |
| [StatusEffectCatalog](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusEffectCatalog.cs) | ScriptableObject listing every definition; `Get(index)`, `IndexOf(definition)`. The index is a status's id on the wire. Referenced by `CombatSettings`. |
| [StatusEffectSet](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusEffectSet.cs) | Plain class: one unit's instances (definition, id, stacks, remaining, tick timer, source, captured damage-dealt multiplier). `Apply` (blocked by base or granted status immunity; refresh, add a stack, or add an independent instance; at the cap the instance closest to expiring is renewed, keeping its tick phase so spamming cannot stall ticks; a granted status immunity removes the active statuses it now blocks), `Remove`, `Clear`, `Tick(deltaTime, dueTicks)` (first tick one interval after landing, a tick on the expiry instant, refreshing keeps the tick phase), `SyncFrom(snapshots)` and `CountDown(deltaTime)` (runs remaining times down without ticking or expiring) for mirrors, `GetRemaining(definition)` (longest instance), `GetSnapshot`, `GetStacks`, and combined values recomputed only on change: move speed, damage dealt and taken (products, multipliers raised to the stack count), controls (OR), resistance deltas (times stacks), granted immunities. Event `Changed`. No allocation once warm. [StatusTick](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusTick.cs) and [StatusSnapshot](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusSnapshot.cs) are its outputs. |
| [StatusEffects](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusEffects.cs) | Component on players and mobs: owns the set; `Changed`, `Blocked`, `Count`, `GetSnapshot`, `GetStacks`, `GetRemaining` (longest instance), damage and move speed multipliers, `Controls`, `MovementScale` (0 while stunned or rooted, otherwise the move speed multiplier), `IsHeld` (from an [IStatusHold](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/IStatusHold.cs)). No injection. |
| [StatusEffectService](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusEffectService.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`ITickable`), authority only. `Apply(target, definition, source)`: rejects without authority, unknown or unit-less targets, dead targets, and the wrong faction (debuffs only across factions, buffs only within one); captures the source's damage-dealt multiplier; an immune target raises `StatusEffects.Blocked` and publishes `CombatEvents.StatusBlocked`. Tracks every unit a status landed on (subscribing to its `Health.Died` to clear its statuses on death) and drops it once its set is empty or it is destroyed. `Tick` advances every tracked, unheld unit by the `IClock` delta, then turns the due ticks into `DamageService.ApplyPeriodicDamage` or `ApplyHeal`. `Remove`, `ClearAll`, `PublishReplicatedBlock` (clients). |
| [NetworkStatusEffects](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/NetworkStatusEffects.cs) | `NetworkBehaviour` on players and mobs. Host: rewrites a server-written `NetworkList<StatusEntry>` whenever the set changes, sends `BlockedRpc(catalogIndex)` for blocked statuses, and is the unit's `IStatusHold` (held while the owner is a client that `IClientReadiness` says is not ready: joined but with no chunk reported, or travelling until it reports a chunk for its trip). Clients: rebuild their mirror with `SyncFrom` on spawn and once per frame after list changes, subtracting the server time elapsed since each write from its remaining time. Logs an error when spawned without a catalog, and for a status missing from it. [StatusEntry](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusEntry.cs): instance id, catalog index, stacks, remaining, server write time. |
| [StatusVisual](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusVisual.cs) | On a visual prefab: tints its sprite with the status colour and pulses its alpha (`Time.deltaTime`, visual only); `SetLayer(i)` draws the i-th visual on a unit 20% larger per layer so stacked rings stay visible; `Follow`, `Show`, `Hide`. The only prefab is [StatusAura.prefab](../Assets/Prefabs/Effects/StatusAura.prefab): the `GroundRing` sprite under the unit (`Terrain` layer, order 9, just below the hover ring), scale 0.9. |
| [StatusVisualPool](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusVisualPool.cs) | Gameplay-scope pool, one free list per visual prefab; new instances move into the Gameplay scene; all destroyed on dispose. |
| [StatusVisuals](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusVisuals.cs) | Component on players and mobs, injected with the pool (logs an error in `Start` without one): on `Changed` it returns the visuals of ended statuses and shows one for each active status with a visual, re-layering them; keeps them at the unit's feet in `LateUpdate`; returns them all in `OnDisable` (death through `DisableOnDeath`, despawn). Runs on every machine. |
| [StatusReport](../Assets/Scripts/StatusEffects/StatusReport.cs) | A blocked status: target, definition, source, popup position. |

Statuses survive trips between worlds, since players live in the Gameplay scene, and run on during them, since trips never stop time. Test statuses in `Assets/Data/StatusEffects`, all in `StatusEffectCatalog.asset`:

| Status | Kind | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| Burn | Debuff, `Burn` tag | 3 Fire per second per stack for 4 s, `AddStack` up to 5 |
| Poison | Debuff, `Poison` tag | 2 Poison per second for 6 s, `Independent` up to 3 |
| Regeneration | Buff | Heals 4 per second for 5 s |
| Fortify | Buff | +30% resistance to every type but True for 8 s |
| Vulnerable | Debuff | Takes 1.2x damage for 5 s |
| Empower | Buff | Deals 1.25x damage for 6 s |
| Invulnerable | Buff | Immune to all damage for 3 s |
| Chill | Debuff, `Chill` and `Slow` tags | Moves at 0.6x for 3 s |
| Stun | Debuff, `Stun` tag | Stunned for 1.5 s |
| Root | Debuff, `Root` tag | Rooted for 2 s |
| Silence | Debuff, `Silence` tag | Silenced for 3 s |
| Unstoppable | Buff | Immune to `Stun`, `Root` and `Slow` statuses for 4 s, removing active ones |

## Effects

- [EffectSpawner](../Assets/Scripts/Effects/EffectSpawner.cs): `Spawn<T>(template, configure)` instantiates an inactive copy at scene root, runs `configure`, activates it. No pooling. Only user: `DestroyMobOnDeath`.

## Mob AI: components

Prefab: [Weasel.prefab](../Assets/Prefabs/Mobs/Weasel.prefab): `MobController`, `MobMotor2D`, `MobPerception2D`, `MobPathAgent2D`, `MobPatrolAnchor`, `MeleeDamageDealer`, `Health` (100 HP), `DamageReceiver` (`Profile_Weasel`), `StatusEffects`, `StatusVisuals`, `DestroyMobOnDeath`, `NetworkObject`, `NetworkTransform` (server authority, x/y only, `LegacyLerp`, unreliable deltas), `NetworkHealth`, `NetworkStatusEffects`, `MobNetworkSync`, `YPositionSorter` (on `Visuals`), `WorldHealthBar` and `WorldStatusIcons` (on the `HealthBar` child, 1.45 above the feet, clear of the ears); config `Mob_Default`, death template `MobDeathAnimation.prefab`, animator `Weasel.controller`.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [MobController](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobController.cs) | The brain. Initializes all mob components from `MobConfig` and its `INavigation2D` (its world's `WorldNavigation`, or a standalone `NavigationGrid2D` in tests), builds a standalone grid if needed, prewarms regions, creates the six states and `MobSeparation2D`. `Update` -> `TickStateMachine(dt)`; `FixedUpdate` -> `FixedTickStateMachine()`. Exposes components to states, `ChangeState`, attack-range checks (collider distance), `onAttackRangeEntered` UnityEvent, gizmos + state label. `Configure(...)` is a non-DI setup path used by tests. `SetHome(rect)` gives a mob of a world its home chunk's area (`ChunkMobSpawner` calls it; see `MobPatrolAnchor`). Logs an error in `Start` if it was never given its grid, players and random source. Reads its `StatusEffects` (when it has one): while stunned (`IsStunned`) perception still ticks but the state neither ticks nor fixed-ticks and the motor is stopped, so the mob resumes in the same state; every fixed tick it sets the motor's speed scale to the statuses' `MovementScale` (0 while stunned or rooted, so a rooted mob keeps deciding and attacking in range but cannot move). |
| [MobMotor2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobMotor2D.cs) | Rigidbody2D movement: `desiredVelocity` (path) + `steeringVelocity` (separation), clamped to `CurrentMoveSpeed` (move speed times the speed scale set by `SetSpeedScale`), accelerated with `MoveTowards` in `FixedTick`. Drives animator params incl. optional `IsAttacking` (timed on `IClock`); faces intended direction, not drift. `IsMoving`, `FacingDirection`, event `AttackAnimationPlayed(direction)`. `ShowRemoteMovement(moving, facing)` drives the animator from replicated state instead of its own velocity (and keeps doing so after attack animations). |
| [MobNetworkSync](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobNetworkSync.cs) | `NetworkBehaviour` on mobs. Host: writes `IsMoving`/`FacingDirection` into server-written `NetworkVariable`s when they change and forwards `AttackAnimationPlayed` as `AttackRpc` to clients. Clients: disable the `MobController` (no AI), make the body kinematic without interpolation, and feed the replicated state into `MobMotor2D.ShowRemoteMovement` / `PlayAttackAnimation`. |
| [MobPerception2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobPerception2D.cs) | Picks its target from its `IPlayerRegistry`: its world's `WorldPlayers`, so a mob of a world considers only the players in it and drops a target that leaves it. Range hysteresis (`detectionRadius` to acquire, `loseTargetDistance` to keep), throttled `Physics2D.Linecast` against `obstacleLayerMask`. Keeps its current target while it stays detected; while it is not detected (hidden or outside detection range) it switches to the nearest other alive player inside `detectionRadius` with line of sight. Drops the target when it dies, leaves the registry or goes beyond `loseTargetDistance`. Exposes `CurrentTarget`, `CurrentTargetPlayer`, `HasDetectedTarget`, `HasLineOfSight`, `LastKnownTargetPosition`, `IsTargetHiddenInRange`. |
| [MobPathAgent2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobPathAgent2D.cs) | Mob wrapper around `PathFollower2D`: configures it from `MobConfig` (movement profile, `waypointReachDistance`, `arrivalDistance`, `nearestCellSearchRadius`; shortcut off), builds from `MobMotor2D.Position`, ticks the follower at `MobMotor2D.CurrentMoveSpeed` (so a rooted mob does not stall) and feeds the returned velocity to the motor (`Stop` when there is no path). Public API: `BuildPathToWorld`, `CanReachWorldTarget`, `FixedTick`, `ClearPath`, path flags, `StalledTime`, `Navigation`. Keeps the `MobPathAgent2D.BuildPathToWorld` profiler marker and the path gizmos. See Navigation. |
| [MobPatrolAnchor](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobPatrolAnchor.cs) | Remembers spawn position; samples reachable roam destinations within `patrolRoamRadius` and idle durations from the injected `IRandom` (no roaming without one). A mob of a world has a home (`SetHome`, `HasHome`, `HomeArea`: its home chunk's 64 x 64 cells in world units, kept across rebuilds): roam destinations lie inside it (`IsInHome`), and `IsWithinLeash(position)` and `IsPathWithinLeash(waypoints)` tell whether points lie within `MobConfig.LeashCells` of it, always true without a home. `IsLeashed` (`SetLeashed`) records that the mob gave up at its leash and is going home; `Initialize` clears it. An area's mob has no home and no leash. |
| [MobSeparation2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobSeparation2D.cs) | Plain class for crowd steering. See Crowd separation. |
| [IMobState](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/IMobState.cs), [MobStateId](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobStateId.cs) | State contract (`Enter`/`Tick`/`FixedTick`/`Exit`) and ids. |
| [MobConfig](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobConfig.cs) | ScriptableObject with movement, perception, search, combat, crowd, patrol, leash (`LeashCells`, 8: how far beyond its home chunk's edge a mob of a world chases; see Worlds and streaming for the rule it must keep) and navigation tuning, read-only at runtime: private serialized fields behind PascalCase properties (`MoveSpeed`, `DetectionRadius`, `AttackStatuses` as a read-only list, ...), with the design notes as inspector tooltips. Tests build theirs with `MobConfig.Create(...)`, whose named values left out keep the defaults. Assets: `Assets/Settings/AI/Mob_Default.asset`, `Assets/Dev/StressTest/Mob_StressTest.asset`. |

Per-frame order: `Update`: perception tick, then state `Tick` (decisions, repath; skipped while stunned). `FixedUpdate`: motor speed scale from statuses, separation sense, state `FixedTick` (path following sets desired velocity; skipped and the motor stopped while stunned), motor applies desired + steering.

## Mob AI: state machine

States are plain classes deriving from [MobStateBase](../Assets/Scripts/AI/StateMachine/MobStateBase.cs), which exposes the brain's components and config. "Spots target" below (`SpotsTarget`) means the mob is not leashed, `HasDetectedTarget`, the target and the mob both lie within the leash (`IsWithinLeash`), and `PathAgent.CanReachWorldTarget(target)`. "Gives up at the leash" (`GiveUpAtLeash`) marks the mob leashed and goes to Return; while leashed it spots no target, until Idle's timer runs out. Only a mob of a world (with a home chunk) has a leash; for an area's mob every leash check passes.

| State | Enter | Tick transitions | FixedTick |
|---|---|---|---|
| [IdleState](../Assets/Scripts/AI/StateMachine/IdleState.cs) | Clear path, stop, roll idle duration | Spots target -> Chase; timer done -> no longer leashed, Patrol | Stop |
| [PatrolRoamState](../Assets/Scripts/AI/StateMachine/PatrolRoamState.cs) | Pick reachable roam point (inside the home chunk for a mob of a world), build path; fail -> Idle | Spots target -> Chase; arrived or stalled `stuckTimeout` -> Idle | Follow path |
| [ChaseState](../Assets/Scripts/AI/StateMachine/ChaseState.cs) | Clear path | Lost target -> Search if hidden within lose range, else Return; target or mob beyond the leash -> gives up at the leash; in attack range -> AttackRange; every `repathInterval` repath, and unreachable / partial / goal-adjusted -> Return, a waypoint beyond the leash -> gives up at the leash | Wait (stop, face target) if an ally is directly ahead near the target (with release hysteresis), else follow path |
| [AttackRangeState](../Assets/Scripts/AI/StateMachine/AttackRangeState.cs) | Clear path, stop, fire `onAttackRangeEntered`, attack | Lost target -> Search/Return; target or mob beyond the leash -> gives up at the leash; beyond `attackStopDistance + attackExitBuffer` -> Chase; else face + `TryDealDamage` + attack animation | Stop |
| [SearchState](../Assets/Scripts/AI/StateMachine/SearchState.cs) | Last known position beyond the leash -> gives up at the leash; strict path to it; fail -> Return | Spots target -> Chase; once arrived or stalled, count down `searchDuration` -> Return | Follow path |
| [ReturnToSpawnState](../Assets/Scripts/AI/StateMachine/ReturnToSpawnState.cs) | Resolve spawn cell (or nearest walkable), build path; fail -> Idle | Spots target -> Chase; in spawn cell, arrived or stalled -> Idle | Follow path |

```mermaid
stateDiagram-v2
    Idle --> Patrol: idle timer
    Idle --> Chase: spots target
    Patrol --> Idle: arrived / stuck
    Patrol --> Chase: spots target
    Chase --> AttackRange: in range
    Chase --> Search: lost, hidden in range
    Chase --> Return: lost / unreachable / leash
    AttackRange --> Chase: out of range
    AttackRange --> Search: lost, hidden in range
    AttackRange --> Return: lost / leash
    Search --> Chase: spots target
    Search --> Return: search time over / no path / leash
    Return --> Chase: spots target
    Return --> Idle: home / stuck
```

## Crowd separation

[MobSeparation2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Core/MobSeparation2D.cs), owned by each `MobController`:

- `Sense(position)` each fixed tick: `Physics2D.OverlapCircle` (non-allocating, `ContactFilter2D` on the mob's own layer, no triggers) within `separationRadius * SenseRadiusFactor`; push away from neighbours closer than `separationRadius`, weighted by overlap; exactly coincident mobs get opposite deterministic directions from instance ids.
- Velocity = push * `separationStrength`, clamped per axis so it never points into an unwalkable cell or across the cliff of raised ground (look-ahead on the mob's navigation, `CrossesCliff`).
- `HasNeighborToward(target, maxDistance)`: used by `ChaseState` to wait behind allies within `crowdWaitDistance` of the target instead of pushing into them.
- The motor adds this steering velocity to the path velocity.

Config: `MobConfig` Crowd header (`separationRadius`, `separationStrength`, `crowdWaitDistance`).

## Navigation and pathfinding

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [NavigationGrid2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/NavigationGrid2D.cs) | MonoBehaviour on each chunk's grid, and on a standalone grid (a Scene Generator scratch scene, tests): what a navigation page reads, and a standalone grid's navigation (`INavigation2D`). On a standalone grid, `BuildGrid` (in `Awake`) collects cells from each terrain source's tilemap (row z = 0), tagged with the source's `TerrainType2D`, then removes every cell that has a tile on one of its `blockingTilemaps` (the collision tilemap), so a wall blocks all terrain layers, and every cell that a collider on its `obstacleLayers` overlaps (`Obstacles` on every grid: the footprints of blocking props). Those come from one `Physics2D.OverlapArea` over the collected cells at build time, each collider's bounds shrunk by 0.01 so one ending on a cell edge leaves the next cell free; triggers and colliders on tilemaps are skipped, since blocking tilemaps already cover theirs. The obstacle cells are kept for the cell refreshes below, so a tile edit never unblocks a prop's cell; a prop moved, added or removed at runtime needs a `BuildGrid` (`ConfigureObstacleLayers` sets the mask in code). Sources on a dual-grid render tilemap are skipped with an error, and so are blocking tilemaps that are dual-grid render tilemaps. The cells are then baked into a `NavigationPage` over `WalkableBounds` (the smallest rectangle holding every walkable cell; `GetCell`), in a `NavigationMap2D` of that one page and a pool of one slot of its own, made again on every `BuildGrid`; the grid passes every query to that map. On a chunk's grid (one with a `WorldChunk`) `Awake` builds nothing: `WorldNavigation.AddChunk` builds its page over the chunk's 64 x 64 cells into the world's map (`PlaceIn`), `RemoveChunk` takes it out (`Unbuild`), and outside Play Mode `BuildGrid` builds that page on its own for the gizmos. Each `TerrainMovementProfile2D` (and the null profile) gets a cost field on first use (`NavigationCostField`, see `NavigationPagePool`): per-cell traversal cost (0 = blocked; where terrain layers overlap, the highest `NavigationTerrainSource2D.Priority` decides and equal priorities combine as most restrictive walkability, highest cost; plus the profile's `EdgeCost` on cells touching a blocked one), diagonal cost, and 4-connected region, rebaked lazily, page by page, when a page changes or the profile's `Version` does. The grid's `Version` changes on every build and every change to its page (its own cells, or a neighbouring chunk's page loading or unloading). Live updates: while enabled the grid listens to `Tilemap.tilemapPositionsChanged` and, for its source and blocking tilemaps, re-reads just the changed cells (`RefreshCells`, also public) and bumps `Version`; a changed cell outside a standalone grid's `WalkableBounds` that gained terrain triggers a full `BuildGrid` (a chunk ignores cells outside its chunk). World/cell conversion, octile heuristic, Bresenham line cost, nearest-walkable search, O(1) `AreCellsConnected` and the `GridAStarPathfinder2D` are its map's. With a raised tilemap (`ConfigureRaisedTilemap`, `RaisedTilemap`; the scene writer and Convert Ground set the grid's) it also bakes the cliff of raised ground, a line along the sides between raised cells (ramps included) and level ones that blocks no cell (`RaisedGround.CanCross`; TerrainTypes.md's Phase 13): a byte per cell with a bit per side (`LeftSide`, `RightSide`, `DownSide`, `UpSide`; `GetCliffSides`), kept in the pool, which A* (a straight step across a cliff side, a diagonal one when either way round its corner crosses one), `TryGetLineCost`, `MovementCost` (a blocked step's cost), the region flood fill and `CrossesCliff(from, to)` (public, along the Bresenham line, for movement off the paths) respect, so units cross between raised and level ground only at a ramp's foot. The profile's edge cost counts a cell with a cliff side, on either side of the cliff. A changed cell of that tilemap re-reads the sides of the cell and its 4 neighbours. The cliff face rises the style's cliff height over `TerrainStyle.PixelsPerCell` (read at each build, before blocked cells are removed, from the `TerrainBlendRenderer` on the grid's object that draws the same raised tilemap; none without one) from the bottom of the lowest raised cell that is no ramp with level ground below, through the raised cells above it. The rows it covers whole (`RaisedGround.RowsCovered`: two at the project's 64 px, none for a face under a cell) are removed like walls (`RaisedGround.IsOnFace`, over the raised ground of every loaded page through `RaisedGround.IKinds`), except above a ramp, where the ramp's slope rises instead (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 14), and a changed cell of the raised tilemap also re-reads the cells above it up to that many rows (`RefreshFaceColumn`). `MoveOffCliffFace(point)` moves a point on the face, a covered cell included, straight up to its top (`RaisedGround.OffTheFace`); `PathFollower2D.BuildPath` applies it to every goal. `GetBlocker(cell, profile)` (internal, for `MovementBlockerGizmo`) tells what keeps a profile off a cell (`Blocker`: `None`, `NoGround` (no terrain, also outside the bounds), `Terrain` the profile cannot walk, `Wall` (a blocking tilemap), `Obstacle`, `CliffFace` (a cell the cliff face covers whole); the cliff's line blocks no cell). Outside Play Mode its gizmos build it on demand (`BuildGizmoGridIfStale`: when stale, or when unbuilt unless the last such build failed, so a broken setup logs its error once rather than on every repaint). |
| [NavigationTerrainSource2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/NavigationTerrainSource2D.cs) | Sits on a tilemap (`[RequireComponent(typeof(Tilemap))]`) and marks its cells as one `TerrainType2D`, with a `Priority` for layers stacked over others (a road or bridge). On dual-grid terrain it goes on the data tilemap; `IsDualGridRenderTilemap` flags a misplaced one. On a ground layer of a `TerrainBlendRenderer` (`TerrainBlendRenderer.FindLayerDrawing`) the priority is the layer's index there, its draw order (see World): `Reset` gives a source added there that priority, its inspector (`NavigationTerrainSource2DEditor`) shows it read-only, and in the editor `OnValidate` tells that renderer, which rebuilds and checks it, so sources added after the renderer built are followed too. |
| [INavigation2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/INavigation2D.cs) | What paths, players and mobs use: `IsBuilt`, `Version`, `Pathfinder`, world/cell conversion, `IsCellWalkable`, `TryGetNearestWalkableCell`, `MovementCost`, `HeuristicCost`, `AreCellsConnected`, `TryGetLineCost`, `CrossesCliff`, `MoveOffCliffFace`, `PrewarmRegions`. A world's `WorldNavigation` and a standalone `NavigationGrid2D` both provide it. |
| [NavigationPage](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/NavigationPage.cs) | Internal. One rectangle of cells read from a `NavigationGrid2D`'s configuration (its terrain layers, at most 64, usable blocking tilemaps, raised tilemap, obstacle cells and cliff face height): per cell, in flat arrays allocated once, the terrain layer bits of the highest priority there (0 = blocked or no ground; the cost bake reads a layer's `TerrainType2D` by its bit) and the kind of raised ground. `ReadTerrainBlock` reads each layer and blocking tilemap in one `GetTilesBlockNonAlloc`. Its `Slot`, `Base` (its first index in the pool), `Around` (the 3 x 3 pages around it in its map, itself in the middle), `IndexAt(x, y)` (a local cell, or one a step beyond its edge in a neighbouring page), and a `Stamp` renewed on every change, its own or a neighbour's. |
| [NavigationPagePool](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/NavigationPagePool.cs) | Slots for pages, shared by every map placed in it: a cell's index is `slot * SlotSize + local index`. Holds the cliff sides, each profile's `NavigationCostField` and the A* `SearchState`, all flat arrays sized to the pool and reused. The Gameplay scope's pool (24 slots of 4096 cells: the host holds at most 16 chunks in interest, plus those waiting out their hysteresis) is shared by every loaded world; a standalone grid has one of a single slot. A page placed when every slot is taken grows it (doubling, with a warning). `GetCostField(profile)` bakes again only the pages whose stamp changed (traversal cost from the layer bits, the edge cost, diagonal costs, and the page's own 4-connected regions, labelled from its base), then, after any change, joins the regions meeting along the right and upper borders of loaded neighbouring pages where no cliff runs between them (a union-find over the labels, flattened so `AreCellsConnected` is one read). A cell beside one where no page is loaded counts as beside a blocked cell for the edge cost. `UsedSlots`, `SlotCount`. |
| [NavigationCostField](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/NavigationCostField.cs) | Internal. One profile's view of a pool: `Costs` (0 = blocked), `DiagonalCosts`, `Regions` (-1 = blocked), `Roots` (each region's joined root), the stamp each slot was baked at and its region count. |
| [NavigationMap2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/NavigationMap2D.cs) | `INavigation2D` over pages of one size on a page grid, in a pool: the queries above, reading across the borders of loaded neighbouring pages, a cell where no page is loaded blocked. `AddPage` places a page, links it with its loaded neighbours, reads its cells (the cliff face's rows reading the page below) and then reads again what its neighbours read across their shared borders (their border cliff sides, and the face rows of the page above), stamping them; `RemovePage` does the same the other way. `RefreshCell` and `RefreshRaisedCell` follow tile edits in whichever pages the cells touch, stamping a neighbour when the cell is on its border. Every change bumps `Version`. `MaxSearchCells` (4096) and `NearestCellSearchRadius` (8) by default; a standalone grid sets its own. A single-grid map (a standalone grid's) also reads its tilemaps outside its page, as a standalone grid always did; a world's map knows nothing outside its pages. |
| [WorldNavigation](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/WorldNavigation.cs) | A world's navigation, in the world's scope: a `NavigationMap2D` with a page per loaded chunk on a 16 x 16 grid of 64-cell pages from the world's cell (0, 0), its pages in the Gameplay scope's pool. `AddChunk(grid, coords)` builds the chunk grid's page over the chunk's cells and joins it (false, with an error, for a chunk outside the world or added twice), `RemoveChunk(coords)`, `HasChunk`; `Dispose` takes every page out. A Sandbox chunk's page builds in 1 to 3 ms in the editor (WorldStreaming.md's Phase 3 notes). |
| [ChunkNavigation](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/ChunkNavigation.cs) | Chunk-scope entry point: adds the chunk's page to its world's `WorldNavigation` in `Start` (once every collider of the scene is in the physics world) and takes it out when the chunk's scope is disposed. |
| [IPathfinder2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/IPathfinder2D.cs) | `FindPath(request[, cellsBuffer])`. |
| [GridAStarPathfinder2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/GridAStarPathfinder2D.cs) | A* over a `NavigationMap2D`'s cost field: 8 neighbours without corner cutting or a step across the cliff of raised ground (not checked on a map without raised ground, `HasCliffSides`), a step off a page's edge reading the loaded neighbouring page directly (`NavigationPage.IndexAt`), binary heap, search state (`SearchState`) in flat arrays indexed like the pool, shared by every map in it and stamped per search (no clearing and no allocations once warm). Expands exactly the cells the earlier dictionary-based version did, about 7x faster in `PathfindingBenchmarkTests`. Strict requests fail fast when the goal is unwalkable or in another region. Partial requests adjust an unwalkable goal to the nearest walkable cell, and toward an unreachable goal return the path to the closest expanded cell, capped by `MaxSearchCells`; on a world's map a partial request toward a goal where no page is loaded, with no walkable cell near it, heads for it the same way, so the path ends at the loaded edge. `ProfilerMarker` `GridAStarPathfinder2D.FindPath`. |
| [PathTypes.cs](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/PathTypes.cs) | `PathRequest` (start, goal, allowPartial, profile), `PathResult` (success, partial, goal adjusted, reached resolved goal, cells). |
| [TerrainType2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/TerrainType2D.cs) | Terrain tag asset (`Assets/Settings/AI/Terrain_Grass.asset`, `Terrain_DryGrass.asset`, `Terrain_Weeds.asset`, `Terrain_Meadow.asset`, `Terrain_TallGrass.asset`, `Terrain_Snow.asset`, `Terrain_Dirt.asset`, `Terrain_Sand.asset`, `Terrain_Mud.asset`, `Terrain_Ice.asset`, `Terrain_Gravel.asset`, `Terrain_Plaza.asset`, `Terrain_Stone.asset`, `Terrain_Shallows.asset`, `Terrain_Water.asset`, `Terrain_Ocean.asset`, `Terrain_Lava.asset`, `Terrain_Planks.asset`); tests build theirs with `Create(id)`. |
| [TerrainMovementProfile2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/TerrainMovementProfile2D.cs) | Per-mob rules: default walkable/cost + per-terrain `TerrainRule`s (read-only properties) + `EdgeCost`, extra cost for entering a walkable cell whose 8 neighbours include one the profile cannot walk (keeps paths off ground edges and wall corners; 0 = off), read-only at runtime; `Version` bumps when the asset is edited (`OnValidate`) so cost fields rebake. Tests build theirs with `Create(defaultWalkable, defaultCost, [edgeCost,] rules)`. Asset: `Assets/Settings/AI/TerrainMovement_Default.asset`. |
| [PathFollower2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/PathFollower2D.cs) | Plain class shared by mobs (through `MobPathAgent2D`) and, from Phase 2 of [PlayerControls.md](PlayerControls.md), players. `Configure(navigation, profile, settings)` (any `INavigation2D`), `BuildPath(start, goal, allowPartial)`, `CanReach(start, goal)`, `Tick(position, moveSpeed, deltaTime)` returning the desired velocity (zero once there is no path), `Clear`, path flags, `StalledTime`, `Waypoints`. No engine lookups, no motor or `MobConfig` dependency, no allocations once its buffers are warm. |
| [PathFollowerSettings2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/PathFollowerSettings2D.cs) | Waypoint reach distance, arrival distance, nearest-cell search radius (-1 = the grid's) and `UseStraightLineShortcut`. |
| [PlayerNavigation](../Assets/Scripts/World/PlayerNavigation.cs) | Gameplay-scope lookup of the navigation a player paths on, since players live in the Gameplay scope and cannot inject a world's. `Of(transform)`: for a registered player in a world (`PlayerWorlds`), that world's `LoadedWorld.Navigation` through `Worlds`; null while the world is not loaded and for a player in no world. Read by `PlayerMotor2D` on every `MoveTo`. It replaced `ActiveNavigationGrid` (WorldStreaming.md's Phase 3); its area half went with the areas in Phase 9. |

Path follower flow ([PathFollower2D](../Assets/Scripts/AI/Navigation/PathFollower2D.cs)): world goal (moved off the cliff face, `MoveOffCliffFace`) -> cells -> `FindPath` into a reused buffer -> smooth (skip intermediate cells only when the straight line is traversable and no more expensive than the route) -> waypoints (last one is the exact goal unless partial; the first is dropped when already within the reach distance) -> `Tick` steers toward the next waypoint, using the arrival distance for the final one, and grows `StalledTime` while the body covers less than 20% of `moveSpeed`. A failed build clears the path and the goal cell. When the navigation's `Version` has moved on since the path was built (a tile edit, a build, or in a world a chunk's page loading or unloading), the next `Tick` checks the remaining segments once (`TryGetLineCost` from the current cell through each waypoint) and, if one is blocked, rebuilds toward the same goal with the same `allowPartial`; with no route left the path clears and `Tick` returns zero. Changes that open a shorter route are left to the owner's own repaths. `CanReach` = goal walkable + same region as the nearest walkable cell to the start. `MobPathAgent2D` calls it with `Time.fixedDeltaTime` and the motor's speed.

Straight-line shortcut (`UseStraightLineShortcut`): when the Bresenham line from the start cell to the goal cell is traversable and its cost is no more than the octile heuristic (so no route can be cheaper), the follower skips A* and uses the exact goal as its only waypoint. It is off for mobs: on random grids about 9% of the queries where it applies gave different waypoints than A* plus smoothing (smoothing stops at the first intermediate cell it cannot see, and A* breaks ties between equal-cost routes differently from the line), so turning it on would change mob paths. It is meant for player clicks.

Costs: 10 per straight step by default, diagonals x1.4.

## World

- A world's chunk scenes (see Worlds and streaming below) each hold a `Grid` at the world's origin with ground layers (plain data tilemaps, only those of the materials the chunk and its neighbours' borders use, in the shared draw order of `TerrainStyle.LayerOrder`: grass first, then dry grass, meadow, tall grass, snow, dirt, sand, mud, ice, the path layers (gravel, plaza, stone), then shallows, water, the ocean and lava over them, and planks over everything, each with a `NavigationTerrainSource2D` whose priority is its draw order) drawn by a `TerrainBlendRenderer` on the `Grid` (see Blended terrain below), optionally a `CollisionTilemap` on the `Obstacles` layer (a DualGrid walls data tilemap from `Walls.prefab`; paint it with the Terrain Brush, which is the Dual Grid Brush there, with the Dual Grid Brush itself, or through `AreaTerrain`), `NavigationGrid2D` (with the `CollisionTilemap` as its blocking tilemap), optionally a `RaisedTilemap` of raised ground and ramps (`RaisedGroundTile`s, [TerrainTypes.md](TerrainTypes.md)'s Phase 10) that the terrain renderer draws as plateaus with cliffs and the navigation reads for their cliff, an `AreaTerrain` for runtime edits, [MobSpawnPoint](../Assets/Scripts/World/MobSpawnPoint.cs)s (mob prefab, gizmo, `PersistentId`) and a `ChunkLifetimeScope`. A world's entries are data on its `WorldDefinition`, not scene objects. A standalone grid (a Scene Generator scratch scene) is set up the same way without a `WorldChunk`.
- [MobSpawning](../Assets/Scripts/World/MobSpawning.cs): static, what `ChunkMobSpawner` spawns with: `HasAuthority` (offline or host) and `IsClient`, `CollectNetworkPrefabs` (the distinct networked mob prefabs of a set of spawn points), `Instantiate` (one spawn point's mob through a resolver, named after its prefab, moved into a scene; null with an error without a prefab), `NetworkSpawn` (in a session) and `Despawn` (network-despawned in a session, destroyed offline, nothing for a mob already gone).
- [EntrySlots](../Assets/Scripts/World/EntrySlots.cs): static, where players arriving at one place of a world stand so they do not stand on each other: slot 0 on the place, then alternately right and left, `GameplaySettings.EntrySlotSpacing` (0.6) apart (`SlotPosition`); `FreeSlot` gives the first slot no other registered player of that world stands within half a spacing of, whatever the players' order in the registry. `WorldStreaming.EnterAsync` and `ArriveAsync` (the session's start, trips) and `PlayerSpawner.SpawnRemotePlayer` (a joining player) place players with it.
- Project layers include `Obstacles` (collision tilemaps; `MobConfig.obstacleLayerMask` uses it for line of sight), `Player` and `Enemy`. Separation only senses colliders on the mob's own layer. `Player` vs `Player` collisions are off in the 2D physics matrix, so players walk through each other; everything else collides.
- Blended terrain ([Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain), shaders in [Assets/Shaders](../Assets/Shaders), spec and phases in [TerrainBlending.md](TerrainBlending.md)): one shader draws a grid's ground layers at 32 px per unit, in one of two looks: crisp (designed rounded edges with rims, outlines, shadows and tufts) or soft (shapes blended from the cells' weights and the materials' heights), switched by a developer toggle on the renderer. Every chunk's ground is drawn this way, each chunk listing only the layers it and its neighbours' borders use, a subsequence of the style's layer order (Phase 2 of [TerrainTypes.md](TerrainTypes.md)), with `TerrainStyle.asset`: Testlands' and the Sandbox's chunks draw grass, meadow, tall grass, dirt, sand, gravel, stone and water among them, the SandboxInterior's dirt and plaza, and the stress world's its `GrassTilemap` only, all saved in the crisp look. Water and the shallows are animated (their glints, sparkles and the swell of their shore rims) in the shader, from each machine's own clock, and so are snow (sparkles only), ice (glints and sparkles) and the ocean (its wave crests travelling towards the shore, sparkles and the swell of its surf); no world lists snow, ice or the ocean yet (the generated areas that did went in WorldStreaming.md's Phase 9; their generators still lay them, TerrainTypes.md's Phases 7 and 8). Raised ground, plateaus with a cliff face, a ledge and ramps, is drawn over the layers from the grid's raised tilemap; the Sandbox and Testlands have a plateau each (Phase 10). The ground layers are plain data tilemaps (no `TilemapRenderer`) holding their material's plain tile (`Tile_Grass` and so on), written by the scene generator, the Terrain Brush, `AreaTerrain` and `StressTestMap`; the dual-grid ground (modules, rule tiles, 64 px tilesets and render tilemaps) went in Phase 4. The Terrain Brush, the Tile Palette's default, previews a stroke on these layers through the renderer (see Editor tools).

  | Type / asset | Role |
  |---|---|
  | [TerrainBlendRenderer](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainBlendRenderer.cs) | `[ExecuteAlways]` on a chunk's (or a standalone) `Grid`. Lists its ground layers bottom first (data tilemap and `TerrainMaterial` asset, at most 32 (`TerrainControlMap.MaxLayers`), drawn in the shader's slower wide variant past 16; several layers may draw the same material; the order is the draw order and also the navigation priority: each layer's navigation source, `NavigationSourceOf` (the `NavigationTerrainSource2D` on its tilemap, when `FindLayerDrawing` finds this renderer and layer for the tilemap, that is the nearest renderer above it listing it, at its first listing), must have its index as priority, `NavigationPriorityOf`; `HasNavigationPrioritiesInStep` tells, its inspector keeps them in step, and a mismatch is logged as an error at each build while the ground is still drawn, since the drawing never reads the priority), its `TerrainStyle`, the chunk size (16), the sorting layer and order (`Terrain`, 0) and its `mode` (`TerrainBlendRenderer.BlendMode`: `Crisp` or `Soft`, serialized, a developer toggle that is not saved per machine; chunks are saved as `Crisp`). When enabled it builds a `TerrainControlMap` (narrow, `R16`; wide, `RGBA32`, when it lists more than `TerrainControlMap.NarrowLayers` (16) layers, or whenever the `internal` seam `SetAlwaysWide(true)` asks, so tests and checks can compare the wide path with the narrow one on the same layers; `IsWide` tells which; see [TerrainLayers32.md](TerrainLayers32.md)), an albedo, a cell field and a height `Texture2DArray` (a slice per layer, sized to the largest material, filled on the GPU with `Graphics.CopyTexture`; a material without a cell field or height leaves its slice empty), the lookup texture of the style and the layers' materials, a `TerrainCoastMap` for its first layer whose material has waves (built again whenever the control map uploads, with the bridge layers' bits as a `uint`; a chunk with a baked coast map uses that instead, see below), a runtime material of the style's shader with the `_TERRAIN_SOFT` keyword set from the mode and `_TERRAIN_WIDE` when built wide (both in `ApplyMode`), and a `MeshRenderer` quad per chunk of a fixed 16-cell grid over the control map's bounds (a world chunk's: over its own cells only), each a child object flagged `HideAndDontSave`, so nothing is saved into the scene and the Scene view never picks the ground; chunks the bounds no longer reach (after an undo shrinks them) are destroyed. The arrays keep no copy in memory (uploaded empty and made unreadable before the copies). Draws nothing where no layer is filled. Subscribes to `Tilemap.tilemapPositionsChanged` (and, in the editor, `Undo.undoRedoPerformed`, since an undo restores tilemaps without reporting the cells) in `OnEnable`: a change patches the control map at once, adds chunks when its bounds grow, and subscribes once to `RenderPipelineManager.beginContextRendering`, which uploads the texture and unsubscribes, so nothing runs per frame while no cell changes. The `Mode` property switches the keyword on the runtime material at once, in edit and play mode, rebuilding nothing and allocating nothing (the keyword is looked up on every switch, since one kept across a reimport of the shader stops working); set from code it marks nothing changed.
