Inspector changes to it switch the keyword too and rebuild it after the editor's current update (`EditorApplication.delayCall`) only when something the build reads changed since the last build (its style, its layers' tilemaps and materials, the chunk size, the sorting), so flipping the mode in the inspector rebuilds nothing; changes to its style or to one of its layers' materials (their editor-only `Validated` events), and to one of its layers' navigation sources (which call its `HandleNavigationSourceChanged`), always rebuild it; it follows its style and materials even when it could not build, so fixing one rebuilds it. The chunks are a `TerrainChunkSet`, the build's checks `TerrainBlendChecks` and the editor's rebuild decisions `TerrainBlendRebuilds` (rows below); the renderer keeps the layers and their navigation rules, the build, following tile changes and undo, previews and the seams. A chunk of a seamless world (WorldStreaming.md's Phase 2; its `WorldChunk` sets this up) is drawn through `internal` `SetChunk(cells, skirtWidth, coastMap)` and `SetSkirt(sources)`, neither saved: its control map covers a fixed area, `ChunkControlArea(cells, skirt)` (its cells and the skirt around them plus the margin, 74 x 74 cells for a 64-cell chunk and a 4-cell skirt), its quads only its own cells, and it also reads `TerrainSkirtSource`s, its loaded neighbours' ground layers and raised ground in a strip along each border, each ground source counted for its first layer of the same material. A material a skirt source shows (with a tile in its strip) that none of its layers draws adds a built layer at its place in the style's order (`BuiltMaterials`; its own layer list is untouched), and a tile landing in such a source's strip, or raised ground in a raised source's while it draws none, rebuilds it; a skirt that changes nothing else only reads the control map and elevation map again. The skirt never touches its tilemaps, so navigation never reads it. With a wave layer and a baked coast map of the control map's size (`IsCoastBaked`, `CoastTexture`) it draws that map and builds no `TerrainCoastMap`; a map of another size is warned about and one is built instead. It builds only once its own `OnEnable` ran (another component enabled before it in the same load, a `WorldChunk`, only stores its settings), so a scene load or domain reload never builds it twice or leaks chunk quads. `OnDisable` unsubscribes and destroys the chunks, meshes, material and textures. A brush's hover preview goes through `internal` `IsDrawing(tilemap)` (built, and built with that tilemap among its layers), `SetPreview(tilemap, cells, filled)` and `ClearPreview()`. They set or clear the control map's preview bits without touching the tilemaps, navigation or anything saved, add chunks when filled preview cells grow the bounds, and schedule the upload before the next render like a tile change (one helper, `FollowControlMapChange`, does this bookkeeping for tile changes, undos and previews). `SetPreview` replaces any earlier preview and finds the layer among the tilemaps the renderer was built from, returning false and changing nothing for a tilemap it is not drawing (one added in the inspector before the rebuild). A preview lasts until cleared or rebuilt. The bounds a preview grew stay while previews replace each other, and `ClearPreview` shrinks them and the chunks back by reading the layers again. Moving a preview inside the bounds allocates nothing. Logs an error for each problem that stops it (no style or shader, no layers or more than 32, a graphics device that cannot sample the format of the control map it builds, `R16_UNorm` or, wide, `R8G8B8A8_UNorm` (`TerrainControlMap.IsFormatSupported(wide)`, the message naming `TerrainControlMap.FormatOf(wide)`), a wide build for a shader without `_TERRAIN_WIDE`, a layer without a tilemap, a tilemap listed twice, a layer without a material, a material without an albedo or without the cell field its cell mode needs, a texture (the height included) that is not repeat x 32 px RGBA32 with sRGB off, an unknown sorting layer; a missing height is not an error, the soft look moves that material's edges by nothing), and warns when the mode is soft and the shader has no `_TERRAIN_SOFT` keyword, when it is built wide and the shader (reimported since) has no `_TERRAIN_WIDE`, and when a layer's tilemap has an enabled `TilemapRenderer` (it would draw its tiles' sprites, the palette swatches, beside the ground), except when completely unconfigured outside play mode; a material several layers draw is checked, and reported, once. `LayerIndexOf(tilemap)` gives the layer drawing a tilemap. Animated materials move in the shader from the frame's time (URP's `_TimeParameters`), with nothing done per frame here; `SetFixedTime(seconds)` draws them at a fixed time instead, through `_TerrainInfo`'s last two values, for repeatable checks, and null goes back to the frame's time (not saved). `internal` seams for tests and the probe: `ControlMap`, `IsWide`, `SetAlwaysWide` (rebuilds when it changes; not saved), `RuntimeMaterial`, the arrays (`AlbedoArray`, `FieldArray`, `HeightArray`), `Lut`, `Chunks`, `FlushPendingChanges`, `SetFixedTime`, `AnimationFramesPerSecond` (the fastest animated material it built from, 0 for none), `SetDebugView` (topmost visible layer as a pure colour, `TB_DEBUG_COLOURS` in the HLSL: each channel off, at a quarter, half, three quarters or full with at least one full, so the 32 colours differ whatever the lights' intensity; layers 0 to 6 light red, green and blue fully by the bits of the layer plus one, layers 7 to 12 one channel full and another at half, layers 13 to 15 two full and the third at half, and in the wide variant layers 16 to 21 and 22 to 27 are layers 7 to 12 with the second channel at a quarter and at three quarters, 28 to 30 one channel full and the others at half, 31 blue full and the others at three quarters, so the first 16 keep their colours), `IsFlushScheduled`, `HandledChangeCount`, `RenderCallbackCount`, and in the editor `HandledUndoCount`, `IsRebuildScheduled` and `RunScheduledRebuild`. Its optional raised tilemap (`raisedTilemap`, `RaisedTilemap`, `Configure(style, raised, layers)`), whose cells are raised ground or ramps, is read into a `TerrainElevationMap` (`_TerrainRaised`; seam `ElevationMap`) at each build, again when the control map's bounds change and after an undo, and when one of its tiles changes, before the next render as for a layer's; with one, the lookup texture holds the style's cliff settings and the runtime material has the `_TERRAIN_RAISED` keyword, set with the look's keyword (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 10 notes). A change of it in the inspector rebuilds. |
  | [TerrainChunkSet](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainChunkSet.cs) | Plain C# (`internal`), the renderer's chunk quads: `Fit(bounds, empty, material, sortingLayerId, sortingOrder)` destroys the chunks the bounds no longer reach (all of them when empty), creates those it needs on a fixed grid of `ChunkSize` cells (child objects of the renderer, `HideAndDontSave`, a `MeshRenderer` with shadows, probes and occlusion off, named after the renderer and on its layer, meshes `DontSave`) and fits each quad to the bounds; `Clear` destroys them all; `Renderers` lists them. The renderer makes one at each build with its chunk size and calls `Fit` when the control map's bounds move. |
  | [TerrainBlendChecks](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainBlendChecks.cs) | Static (`internal`): `IsUsable(renderer, grid, sortingLayerName, wide)`, the checks a renderer runs before it builds a narrow or wide control map, with the errors and warnings listed under `TerrainBlendRenderer` above, each logged against the renderer or the material or texture at fault. |
  | [TerrainBlendRebuilds](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainBlendRebuilds.cs) | Editor only (`#if UNITY_EDITOR`, plain C#): when an inspector change rebuilds a renderer. `Remember` what a build read (style, layers' tilemaps and materials, chunk size, sorting) and `Changed` against it; `Follow` the style's and the layers' materials' `Validated` events (once per material) and `StopFollowing`; `Schedule` the rebuild on `EditorApplication.delayCall`, once however often asked, and `RunScheduled` (the renderer's `RunScheduledRebuild`), which rebuilds through the renderer unless it was destroyed or disabled since. |
  | [TerrainControlMap](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainControlMap.cs) | Plain C# (`IDisposable`). A point-filtered texture with a texel per cell over the layers' filled cells plus a margin of one empty cell (so edge bands stay on the chunks), bit i for layer i, built narrow or wide by an explicit choice (the constructor's `wide`, `IsWide`): narrow an `R16` (`R16_UNorm`) texel, up to `NarrowLayers` (16), which the shader reads as `round(r * 65535)`; wide an `RGBA32` (`R8G8B8A8_UNorm`, linear) texel, up to `WideLayers` (32), its four bytes the bits from red up, which the shader's `_TERRAIN_WIDE` variant reads as `r | g << 8 | b << 16 | a << 24`, each channel `round(c * 255)`; more layers than its format holds throw. `MaxLayers` (32) is the most a renderer draws, the wide variant's cap. Cells, `GetCell`, `GetShownCell` and the preview bits are `uint` either way, and `Apply` writes each texel's bytes lowest first. `FormatOf(wide)` gives the format and `IsFormatSupported(wide)` tells whether the device can sample it (the format and its layout are checked against Unity's, D3D's and Vulkan's docs in TerrainLayers32.md's Decisions). `Rebuild` reads every layer (tight bounds, whatever the tilemaps' own bounds; z = 0 only); `Refresh(layer, positions)` reads changed cells (ignoring z other than 0), growing the bounds first (by the cells needed plus 8 more on each growing side) when a filled cell lands outside them or in the margin; changed cells wait for `Apply`, which writes them into the texture's data and uploads once (`UploadCount`), allocating nothing inside the bounds. Preview bits (`SetPreview`, `ClearPreview`, `HasPreview`; the Terrain Brush's hover, through the renderer) show in the texture without touching the real bits (`GetCell` vs `GetShownCell`); a filled preview cell outside the bounds or in the margin grows them like a filled tile. A layer may also read `Source`s (tilemap, layer, cells; `SetSources`, `Sources`, `Reads`), extra tilemaps counted only inside their cells, so a cell is filled for a layer when its own tilemap or a source covering it holds a tile; `Refresh(tilemap, positions)` reads changed cells for every layer that has the tilemap as its own or a source. Built over a fixed area (the constructor's `fixedArea`, `HasArea`, `Area`; a world chunk's), the bounds are that area while any cell in it is filled and nothing outside it is read. |
  | [TerrainCoastMap](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainCoastMap.cs) | Plain C# (`IDisposable`). How far each spot of a renderer's area lies from the coast of its layer with waves: a bilinear `R16` texture, `TexelsPerCell` (8) texels per cell side over the control map's bounds, holding the signed distance in pixels (positive at sea) as (distance - `MinDistance`) / `DistanceRange`; 1 x 1 without a layer with waves. A cell is the sea's where that layer or one above it that is no bridge is filled, or where it is empty, so the map's edge is no coast. `Build(controlMap, waveLayer, bridges)` (`bridges` a `uint`, a bit per bridge layer, so a narrow or wide map's layers all count) takes chamfer distances both ways, blurs them three times over 3 texels and, blending in from 8 px out over 50 px, over 8 texels, so waves following it bend gently near the coast and forget its steps out at sea; it works them out again only when a cell changed sides since the last build (about 70 ms for the Coast in the editor's debug code). `BuildFromCells(sea, width, height)` builds it from a sea flag per cell of a window instead, and `EncodedAt(x, y)` reads a texel, clamped at the edge: the editor's coast bake (`WorldCoastBake`). The blur's running sums are doubles, so a texel does not depend on where its line starts and a window of the world gives the texels a map of the whole world gives. See TerrainTypes.md's Phase 8 notes. |
  | [TerrainElevationMap](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainElevationMap.cs) | Plain C# (`IDisposable`). Which cells of a renderer's area are raised ground: a point-filtered `R8` texel per cell over the control map's bounds holding what the cell of the raised tilemap is (`RaisedGround.KindOf`: 0 level, 1 raised, 2 a ramp), 1 x 1 and level without a raised tilemap or with an empty control map. `Build(controlMap, raised)` reads the tilemap again, and `Build(controlMap, raised, skirt)` also the raised `TerrainSkirtSource`s where the cell is level on its own tilemap (`HasRaised`); `GetCell` reads the last build. About 3 KiB for the Coast. |
  | [TerrainSkirtSource](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainSkirtSource.cs) | Readonly struct: one tilemap of a neighbouring chunk a renderer also reads, only in `Cells` (the strip along the border): a ground layer drawing `Material`, or raised ground (`Raised(tilemap, cells)`, `IsRaised`); `Contains`, `HasAnyTile`. |
| [RaisedGround](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/RaisedGround.cs) | Static: the rules of raised ground the renderer, navigation and the scene generator share. `KindOf(tile)` (`Level` without a tile, `Ramp` for a `RaisedGroundTile` that is a ramp, else `Raised`), `KindAt(raised, cell)`, `CanCross(from, to, step)` (a step between level ground and raised ground, ramps included, is refused, except between a ramp and the level cell right below it; navigation bakes it into cliff sides), `RowsCovered(faceHeight)` and `RowsReached(faceHeight)` (how many rows of cells a face that many cells tall covers whole, and reaches into at all), `IsOnFace(raised, cell, rows)` and its twin over a layout's cell sets (a raised cell at most `rows` cells above level ground, raised all the way down, whose lowest raised cell, the face's foot, is no ramp: above a ramp the slope rises instead), `OffTheFace(raised, point, faceHeight)` (a point on the cliff face moved straight up to the face's top, `faceHeight` cells above its foot); `IsOnFace` and `OffTheFace` also take any `IKinds` (a raised tilemap's is `TilemapKinds`), through which navigation reads raised ground across chunk borders; and `IsCliff` over a layout's cell sets: the plateau's rim, a raised cell that is no ramp with level ground among its 8 neighbours, which the generator keeps mobs and props off and counts as blocked when it checks what the spawn reaches. |
| [RaisedGroundTile](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/RaisedGroundTile.cs) | A `Tile` for a raised tilemap: raised ground, or a ramp (`IsRamp`), raised ground that slopes down to the ground below along its bottom edge and can be walked up there. Its sprite is a palette swatch only. `internal static Create(isRamp)` for tests. |
| [TerrainMaterial](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainMaterial.cs) | ScriptableObject, one per ground material, read-only at runtime; its asset name is its name. Albedo, optional cell field and height textures, repeat in cells, outer and inner corner radii, cell mode (`TerrainMaterial.CellMode`: none / whole chips / cut stones) with base colour, keep margin (whole chips: how far clear of the rim all of a kept chip must lie; cut stones: how far inside the rim a kept stone's centre must lie), cut gap and cut bevel, the soft look's height scale (how many pixels the height texture moves its edges either way, and each whole stone's centre; at most `MaxHeightScale`, 2.5 px, which the inspector's range and the `HeightScale` getter hold to, and `OnValidate` warns about an edited asset holding more; see TerrainBlending.md's Phase 2 notes for the bound), its animation (frames per second, 0 for a still material, `IsAnimated` above it; glint colour, period and duty; sparkle colour, period and duty; rim swell in pixels and swell period; swash, up to `MaxSwash` (12 px), how far past its edge it washes up what it meets as each of its waves arrives; see TerrainBlending.md's Phase 5 notes), its waves, under a header of their own (spacing in pixels, 0 for none, up to `MaxWaveSpacing`; period; width; angle in degrees; the distance from the coast within which they run whole; stretch, how much of each shows further out; wave, crest and trough colours; the swash's foam, foam shade, film and film back colours and how wet it leaves the shore; `HasWaves` when animated with a spacing; see TerrainTypes.md's Phase 8 notes), its sinking (sink depth in pixels, 0 for a material level with the ground, up to `MaxSinkDepth`; bank shadow), its bank colour (the face it shows where a sunken material cuts into it), its sway, under a header of its own in the inspector (amplitude up to `MaxSwayAmplitude`, period, gust length in cells; `Sways` when animated with an amplitude; `WholeClumps`, off by default: each weed takes its gust at the cell of its foot and only its own pixels lean, the ground inside its box staying still), its placement (`Bridge`: it lies on what is painted under it, so the layers under it count its cells only where they are painted there instead of going on beneath it; `TurnsToRuns`: its textures turn a quarter in cells where its layer runs further left and right than up and down; see TerrainTypes.md's Phase 5 notes), and a default `TerrainStyle.EdgeSettings`. The textures, repeat, cell mode and base colour, and an animated recipe's glint and sparkle colours, are written by its terrain material recipe (see Editor tools). `internal static Create` builds one in memory for tests. In the editor its `OnValidate` and `NotifyChanged` (which the recipe writer calls after rewriting the textures) raise `Validated` for the renderers drawing it. |
  | [TerrainStyle](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainStyle.cs) | ScriptableObject, read-only at runtime: the shader; shared edge noise (px), pair margin (1.5 px) and pair blend (1.5 px); the layer order (`LayerOrder`, `LayerOrderIndexOf`): every ground material, bottom first, the project-wide draw order each grid's layers follow as a subsequence, so a pair is always drawn the same way up (TerrainTypes.md's Decisions; the scene generator's writer and Convert Ground enforce it, the renderer draws whatever it lists); and pair overrides, each an upper `TerrainMaterial` over a lower one or over nothing (no lower material) with its `EdgeSettings`. `EdgeSettings`: rim width and rim `Gradient` (colours from the edge inwards, alpha how much of the interior it covers), outline colour and whether it is drawn, shadow colour, alpha and width, tuft height; for the soft look a band (width up to 4 px and colour) that replaces the outline and the rim, and whole stones (over a material with cut stones, each stone drawn whole or left out, decided at its centre moved by its own height). `GetEdge(upper, lower)` (upper required) returns the override for that pair of material assets or the upper material's default edge; overrides are keyed by asset, so another material of the same name and settings does not match. An override whose lower material is missing (`PairOverride.HasMissingLower`: set, but its asset deleted or the object destroyed, which Unity's `==` calls null) never applies, so it cannot pass for one over nothing. `internal static Create` builds one in memory for tests, with or without a layer order. In the editor its `OnValidate` warns about overrides without an upper material or with a missing lower one and about empty or repeated entries in the layer order, and raises `Validated` for the renderers using it. Its Raised ground settings are raised ground's look, the same for every material, whose bank colour the face takes: the cliff's height (inside the raised cells), fringe, outer and inner radius, band rows, band shade, depth shade, pebble chance and light, foot and outline shades, rim light and ledge width, the ramp's shade, and the shadow's alpha and height (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 10 notes). |
  | [TerrainBlendLut](../Assets/Scripts/World/Terrain/TerrainBlendLut.cs) | Static: the style and the layers' materials as shader data for both looks, an RGBA float texture read with `Load`, 37 texels wide (`Width`). Its layout has one name per column and row type, in C# constants and in the `#define`s of [TerrainBlendLut.hlsl](../Assets/Shaders/Terrain/TerrainBlendLut.hlsl) (`TB_` and the constant's name in capitals with underscores: `LayerBankColourColumn` is `TB_LAYER_BANK_COLOUR_COLUMN`), which `TerrainBlendLutTests` checks agree; the shader reads no column by number. Row `SharedRow` (0) the shared settings at `SharedColumn` (edge noise, pair margin, pair blend, and the low 16 bits of the bridge layers, `BridgeBits`, a `uint`: bit i for a layer i drawing a bridge material) and at `SharedHighBridgesColumn` the high 16 bits in its first value (empty for 16 layers or fewer, so a narrow renderer's lookup texture is as before; each half is a whole number a float holds exactly, and the shader reads the high half only with `_TERRAIN_WIDE`); a row per layer (`LayerRow`, the shader's `TBLayerRow`): `LayerShapeColumn` (radii, repeat in pixels, cell mode), `LayerChipsColumn` (keep margin, cut gap, cut bevel, 1 when the material has cut stones and one of the layer's pairs shows them whole), `LayerBaseColumn` (base colour), `LayerHeightColumn` (height scale, 0 without a height texture; 1 when the material turns to its runs), for an animated material `LayerAnimationColumn` (frames per second, glint period, glint duty, rim swell), `LayerGlintColumn` (glint colour, swell period), `LayerSparkleColumn` (sparkle colour, sparkle period) and `LayerSparkleTimingColumn` (sparkle duty, swash), zero for a still one; `LayerBankColumn` (sink depth, bank shadow), zero for a level one; `LayerBankColourColumn` its bank colour; `LayerSwayColumn` (sway amplitude, sway period, gust length, 1 when its clumps lean whole) for a swaying one, zero otherwise; for a material with waves `LayerWavesColumn` (spacing, period, width, tangent of the angle), `LayerWaveColourColumn` (wave colour, whole within), `LayerWaveCrestColumn` (crest colour, stretch), `LayerWaveTroughColumn` (trough colour), `LayerWashFoamColumn` (foam colour, wet), `LayerWashFoamShadeColumn`, `LayerWashFilmColumn` and `LayerWashFilmBackColumn` (their colours), zero otherwise; a row per pair of an upper layer over a lower one or over nothing (`PairRow`; the shader's `TBFirstPairRow` is a layer's pair over nothing, the others following): `PairShadowColumn` (shadow colour, alpha), `PairOutlineColumn` (outline colour, drawn), `PairWidthsColumn` (shadow width, rim width, tuft height, whole stones, only over cut stones), `PairFillColumn` (the rim's outer colour; alpha 0 when the edge is seamless), `PairBandColumn` (the soft band's colour, its width), then from `PairRampColumn` the rim sampled every `RampStep` (half a pixel), `RampTexels` (32) texels. For layers drawn over raised ground (`BuildTexels(…, raised)`, `Create(…, raised)`), row 0 also holds the style's cliff settings at `SharedCliffColumn` (height, fringe, outer and inner radius), `SharedCliffFaceColumn` (band rows, band shade, depth shade, pebble chance), `SharedCliffEdgeColumn` (foot shade, outline shade, rim light, pebble light), `SharedCliffShadowColumn` (shadow alpha and height, ramp shade) and `SharedCliffRimColumn` (ledge width); they are empty otherwise, and the shader then draws no cliffs. |
  | [TerrainBlend.shadergraph](../Assets/Shaders/Terrain/TerrainBlend.shadergraph), [TerrainBlend.hlsl](../Assets/Shaders/Terrain/TerrainBlend.hlsl) | Sprite Lit graph (`TopDownRPG/Terrain/TerrainBlend`, Disable Color Tint on) whose file-mode Custom Function calls `TerrainBlend_float` with the world position, the control map, the lookup texture, the three arrays, `_ControlRect` (world origin and size of the control map) and `_TerrainInfo` (the layer count the renderer was built with, debug view). Its keyword "Terrain Soft" (`_TERRAIN_SOFT`) is a local, fragment-only `multi_compile`, so builds keep both looks although only code ever enables it; so is "Terrain Raised" (`_TERRAIN_RAISED`), which the renderer enables when it has a raised tilemap, so a grid without raised ground runs a variant without its code (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 10 notes), and so is "Terrain Wide" (`_TERRAIN_WIDE`), which the renderer enables when it builds a wide control map: under it `TBCell` decodes the RGBA32 texel, `TerrainBlend_float` adds the bridge bits' high half from `TB_SHARED_HIGH_BRIDGES_COLUMN`, and `TBNearBridgeEdge` builds its mask of the bridges from two layers up as `2u << min(m + 1, 31)`, never shifting by 32 or more (without it, `1u << min(m + 2, 31)` as before). `TB_MAX_LAYERS` is 32 with it and 16 without it, and so are the debug colours, so without the keyword every variant compiles to the same code as before the keyword and grids of 16 layers or fewer draw exactly as they did. In the wide variant the per-pixel arrays, every layer's distance `d` and the pair weights `w`, are static variables every function reads in place, where the narrow variant passes them as parameters, which FXC copies into each function; the macros `TB_D_PARAM`, `TB_D_OUT_PARAM`, `TB_W_PARAM`, `TB_W_OUT_PARAM`, `TB_D_ARG` and `TB_W_ARG` of `TerrainBlendInputs.hlsl` name those parameters and arguments, expanding to nothing in the wide variant. The wide variant draws the 16-layer stress scene about twice as long as the narrow one in crisp and 1.35 times in soft, and 32 layers 2.1 and 1.65 times (TerrainLayers32.md's Phase 2 and 3 notes). The HLSL is split by concern (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 11): `TerrainBlend.hlsl`, the file the graph calls, holds `TerrainBlend_float` (the composite) and includes, in order, `TerrainBlendInputs.hlsl` (the lookup texture's named columns from `TerrainBlendLut.hlsl`, the row functions `TBLayerRow` and `TBFirstPairRow`, the other defines and the shared structs `TBInputs`, `TBCorner`, `TBEdge`, `TBCentre`; `TBLut`, `TBShape`, `TBToOutput`), `TerrainBlendShape.hlsl` (control map cells, masks and bridges, `TBLayersAt`), `TerrainBlendLook.hlsl`, which includes one look by the keyword: `TerrainBlendCrisp.hlsl` or `TerrainBlendSoft.hlsl`, each defining the same look functions (`TBDistanceTerms`, `TBLayerDistance`, `TBOutward`, `TBEdgeDirection` for banks and tufts, `TBAddPairEdge` and `TBRampTexel` for a pair's part of the edge and the rim, `TBAddCentrePair`, `TBCentreOutward` and `TBChipNearest` for chips and stones, `TBOpaqueInside`; crisp also `TBGradient`, soft also `TBCellHeights`, `TBSoftDistance` and `TBDepthAt`), `TerrainBlendCoverage.hlsl` (every layer's distance, `TBVisible`, the bridge checks), `TerrainBlendRuns.hlsl` (turning to runs), `TerrainBlendEdges.hlsl` (pair weights, edge settings, rims, `TBSeamlessBelow`, tufts), `TerrainBlendCells.hlsl` (`TBCentreAt`: what a chip or stone sees at its centre), `TerrainBlendAnimation.hlsl` (animation time, glints and sparkles, rim swell), `TerrainBlendSway.hlsl` (sway and the clump border check), `TerrainBlendBanks.hlsl` (sunken banks), `TerrainBlendInterior.hlsl` (`TBInterior`; `TBLayerInterior`, which works the interior out once per layer for the edge pixel and the inside; `TBSeamlessEdge`, `TBInside`, which draws a layer's waves over its inside), `TerrainBlendWaves.hlsl` (`TBCoastDistance`, which samples the coast map, `_TerrainCoast`, a texture the runtime material sets and the include declares, the graph listing it as the hidden property "Terrain Coast" (`_TerrainCoast`) with its HLSL declaration overridden to "Do Not Declare", like "Terrain Raised" (`_TerrainRaised`): a texture a material sets that its shader's Properties do not list is not bound per material by the SRP Batcher, so with several chunk renderers every chunk read one material's elevation and coast maps (WorldStreaming.md's Phase 2 notes); `TBWaves`, a layer's waves; `TBWash`, its swash past its edge, which each look draws there, over the pair's shadow in crisp and where it covers at least half a pixel in soft), `TerrainBlendLayer.hlsl`, which includes `TerrainBlendLayerCrisp.hlsl` or `TerrainBlendLayerSoft.hlsl` (each look's `TBLayer`) by the keyword, and `TerrainBlendDebug.hlsl` (the debug colours); each includes what it uses, and only the two selector files name the look's keyword (`_TERRAIN_WIDE` is named where it changes the code: `TerrainBlendShape.hlsl`, `TerrainBlend.hlsl` and `TerrainBlendCoverage.hlsl`). The HLSL (`TB_MAX_LAYERS` 16, or 32 with `_TERRAIN_WIDE`) snaps to the 32 px grid, reads the four cells around the nearest corner (each a 16-bit texel read as `round(r * 65535)`, or with `_TERRAIN_WIDE` an RGBA32 texel read byte by byte), computes the signed distance of each layer up to the highest one filled in those four cells (`TBLayersAt`; the layers above cover none of them, so a grid pays per pixel only for the layers painted there) with cumulative masks, in which a cell counts for a layer when it or a layer above it is filled, except a bridge layer above it (`TBCounted`, from row 0's bridge bits), which lies on what is painted under it (crisp: the rounded shape distance pushed by the shared edge noise; soft: the bilinear weight of the four cells, less a bump on diagonal pairs, plus the heights of the cells' top materials mixed by the same weights, as a distance whose 0.5 is where the weight passes one half), chooses which lower layers show (a lower layer only where its edge lies the pair margin beyond the next layer up); where a bridge ends on the edge of a layer painted under it, which the next layer up does not then hide, that layer draws its edge pixel and its inside but neither its shadow nor its tufts (`TBBridgesAt`, `TBNearBridgeEdge`), blends each edge's pair settings over the pair blend width, and composites bottom up: shadow, outline, rim gradient over the interior, chips and stones decided at their centre with the pair found there (a whole chip kept only where its reach, read from the cell field and stretched by the edge noise's steepest slope, keeps all of it clear of the rim; stones left out or cut along the rim and bevelled to the light), and attached tufts on straight edges. An animated layer (its layer row's frames per second above 0) lights its glint and sparkle pixels, those whose height texel's blue channel holds a glint phase (1 to 127) or a sparkle phase (128 to 255), with its glint or sparkle colour for that one's duty fraction of each of its periods (half strength over its first and last quarter), and reads its rims up to its rim swell further in, swelling in and out over its swell period at a phase that drifts along the edge with the edge noise's pattern; its time is `_TimeParameters.x` (or `_TerrainInfo.w` when `_TerrainInfo.z` is set), stepped to its frames per second. A swaying layer (sway amplitude above 0) reads its albedo from a texel moved left by its sway (`TBSwayTexel`: a 0-to-1 gust rolling over the cells picks a pose, the tips' lean in whole pixels up to the amplitude, and a pixel leans by the pose times the square of its weight in the height's blue channel, rounded, at the frames per second), and its blue channel then holds no glints; where its clumps lean whole (its sway row's last value) the gust is taken at the cell of the clump's foot (the pixel's row less its box row from the height's green channel), and a leaning pixel that would show a texel the cell field holds unchanged (the ground) shows the field under itself instead, so only the clump's own pixels move. A pixel of a clump's box (the height's green channel above 0: its row from the row under the clump's base, plus one) shows the cell field's colour, the ground under the clumps, unless the clump's foot and its top (the field's alpha: the box's rows above its base) both lie on the layer where it shows (`TBOpenOn`: inside the layer past its edge pixel, with no layer above covering it), so a clump rooted under a path or beyond its layer's edge leaves no tip above it and one growing up under a path is not cut by it. A seamless pair (its rim's outer colour with alpha 0) draws the edge pixel with the layer's own interior under the rim's first texel (`TBSeamlessEdge`), so a seamless pair with a rim (deep water over shallows) starts its rim at the edge. A sunken layer (`TBInside`) draws its bank inside its edge, the sink depth times how much the edge faces up the screen (the bare shape's direction in crisp, so the bank keeps its height along a straight edge) and how much of it lies over another layer along a pair that is not seamless (`TBSeamlessBelow`), so none over nothing or over a layer of the same surface (shallows), in the bank colours of the layers it meets blended by their pair weights (`TBBankFace`, leaving out seamless pairs), its lowest pixel darker, from the edge pixel in (`TBEdgeColour`, in place of the outline or the rim's outer colour); the rim starts below the bank, and the bank's shadow darkens the layer over as many pixels again. In soft every pixel is opaque or clear: the edge pixel is opaque, no shadow is drawn, a pair's band replaces its outline and rim, whole chips are kept by the depth where they come nearest the edge, and over a pair with whole stones (where the stone's centre lies 10 px inside the lower layer) each stone is drawn whole or left clear, inside or outside the shape. A layer that turns to its runs (`TBTurned`) reads its albedo, cell field and height turned a quarter (`TBSampled`: the texture's x up the world, its top to the left) in a cell where its layer runs further left and right than up and down, counting up to `TB_RUN_REACH` (3) cells each way with a loop over control map reads, ties left as they are, and turns the offsets it reads back into the world (`TBUnturn`). Colours are treated as sRGB and converted for the target. After the layer loop, with `_TERRAIN_RAISED` and when row 0's cliff height is above 0, `TerrainBlendRaised.hlsl` draws raised ground over the composite (`TBRaisedOver`, `TBRaised`): it reads `_TerrainRaised` (the elevation map, a texture the runtime material sets and the include declares; the graph lists it as the hidden property "Terrain Raised" with "Do Not Declare", see below), the raised ground's distance at the pixel's corner patch with each look's `TBRaisedDistance` (crisp the rounded shape with the cliff's radii and no edge noise, soft the cells' bilinear weight), and how many rows a pixel lies above the cliff's foot by halving the cliff's height (`TBRowsAboveFoot`); within the cliff's height of the foot it draws the cliff face (`TBCliffFace`: the ground hanging over it, banded earth in the bank colour of the composite's top layer, a dark foot) or, where the foot lies in a ramp's cell, the ramp's slope over the face's whole height, up through the raised cells above the ramp (`TBRampFace`), elsewhere on raised ground the outline and the ledge along the sides and back (`TBCliffRim`), and below the foot the shadow (`TBCliffShadow`, scaled by each look's `TBCliffShadowScale`, none in soft). The debug view does not draw it. |
  | [Assets/Shaders/Include](../Assets/Shaders/Include) | Shared helpers: `PixelSnap.hlsl` (`SnapToArtPixel`, `WrapPixel`), `PeriodicNoise.hlsl` (`PeriodicEdgeNoise`: three waves repeating every 128 px, slope under 0.33 per pixel at amplitude 1), `RoundedCorners.hlsl` (`CornerShapeDistance`: the exact distance to the filled cells around a corner, outer corners rounded, inner ones filleted). |
  | [TerrainStyle.asset](../Assets/Settings/Terrain/TerrainStyle.asset) | The terrain shader and the shared settings (edge noise 1 px, pair margin and pair blend 1.5 px). Its layer order: `Grass`, `DryGrass`, `Weeds`, `Meadow`, `TallGrass`, `Snow`, `Dirt`, `Sand`, `Mud`, `Ice`, `Gravel`, `Plaza`, `Stone`, `Shallows`, `Water`, `Ocean`, `Lava`, `Planks`. Pair overrides: stone over gravel: in crisp a 0.75 px rim of mortar (98, 88, 76), no outline, a neutral shadow (46, 40, 32) at 0.28 over a pixel, so the cobbles look set into the gravel; in soft whole stones. Plaza over gravel and stone over plaza: the same edge, so the street looks laid into the square. Water over sand: water's shore rim without its outline, and a wet sand shadow (150, 118, 76) at 0.5 over 2.5 px. Dry grass over grass: seamless, no outline, rim or shadow, with 3 px tufts in straw (164, 145, 98). Sand, mud, gravel and stone over dirt: their own default edges with an earth shadow (66, 46, 28) instead of a green one. See TerrainTypes.md's Phase 3 notes. Shallows over sand: their shore with the wet sand shadow (150, 118, 76) at 0.45 over 2 px. Water over shallows: seamless, no outline or shadow, a 4 px rim of two opaque steps, (56, 126, 172) then (48, 114, 166), so the lake steps down from the shallows into deep water (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 4 notes). The layer order ends with `Planks`, after the water. Planks over water and over shallows: their outline with a shadow cast on the water, (12, 30, 48) at 0.5 over 2.5 px (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 5 notes). Meadow over grass: seamless, with no outline, rim, shadow or tufts, since the meadow's ground is the grass's; meadow over dry grass: seamless with 3 px green tufts (52, 128, 50) (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 6 notes). Weeds over grass and the meadow over weeds: seamless the same way, all three sharing the grass's ground; weeds over dry grass: seamless with the same green tufts (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 12 notes). Dirt, gravel, stone and ice over snow: their own edges with a soft blue shade (150, 172, 210) at 0.35 over 3.5 px instead of their shadow, so the snow looks banked against them, dirt's without its straw tufts and with a 1.5 px rim of wet earth (118, 96, 72) under an outline (92, 72, 50); water over ice takes its default edge (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 7 notes). The ocean comes after the water: ocean over sand draws its surf without an outline and a wet sand shadow (150, 118, 76) at 0.5 over 3 px; ocean over nothing (the map's edge, where the sea goes on) draws no outline, rim or shadow (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 8 notes). Lava comes after the ocean, with no overrides: its default edge reads the same over every ground it meets (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 9 notes). Raised ground: a 64 px cliff, two cells (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 14), with a fringe of up to 3 px, radii 14 and 11 px, bands of 3 rows every other one at 0.82, darkening by 0.3 to a foot at 0.6, pebbles at 1.25 on 1.2 % of the face, an outline at 0.55, a rim light of 0.2, a 3 px ledge, a ramp shade of 0.18, and a shadow of 0.4 over 4 px (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 10 notes). |
  | [Assets/Settings/Terrain/Materials](../Assets/Settings/Terrain/Materials) | `Grass`, `DryGrass`, `Weeds`, `Meadow`, `TallGrass`, `Snow`, `Dirt`, `Sand`, `Mud`, `Ice`, `Gravel`, `Plaza`, `Stone`, `Shallows`, `Water`, `Ocean`, `Lava` and `Planks`. Dry grass is grass's edge in straw colours (rim (192, 175, 128) to (154, 133, 89), outline (95, 79, 51)) and level with it. Dirt: a 3 px rim from straw (190, 168, 92) fading into the earth, an outline (112, 94, 50), a green shadow (34, 70, 30) at 0.3 and 4.5 px tufts, so it meets grass with a fringe of dry blades, kept in soft (no band); a height scale of 2 px; bank colour (100, 76, 52). The plaza has stone's settings (radii, cut stones, keep margin, cut gap and bevel, height scale, edge). See TerrainTypes.md's Phase 3 notes. The grass is still (not animated, no clumps, no cell field); weeds and dry grass sway (8 frames per second, 2 px, 2.5 s, gusts 12 cells long): the clumps of weeds and stalks their recipes draw lean in the wind. Weeds are the grass's edge, radii, height scale and bank colour with the clumps (merged into the grass in TerrainTypes.md's Phase 3, separated again in Phase 12). Sand and mud have gravel's kind of edge in their own colours (a 1.5 px rim fading in, an outline, a green shadow, a 1.5 px band in soft) and a height scale of 2 px; water an outline, an 8 px shore rim in fixed steps from foam to shallows and a faint shadow, no soft band, a height scale of 1.5 px, and its animation (8 frames per second; glints in the strokes' highlight (140, 196, 228) every 3 s for 35 % of it; sparkles (226, 246, 242) every 4 s for a fifth of it; a 1.5 px rim swell over 4.5 s), and it is sunken 4.5 px under a bank with a 0.35 shadow; bank colours: grass (96, 74, 50), the dirt under it, sand (178, 146, 96), mud (82, 60, 40), gravel (118, 94, 64), stone (86, 76, 64), shallows (140, 118, 82); see TerrainBlending.md's Phase 5 notes. The shallows are lighter water over a sandy bed with the water's radii: a soft shore (no outline, a pixel of foam (214, 236, 232), two of pale water (156, 206, 214) at half cover, a faint wet shadow (24, 44, 30) at 0.25), a height scale of 1.5 px, animated like the water (8 frames per second; glints (168, 218, 236) every 2.5 s for 35 % of it; sparkles (232, 250, 246) every 4 s for a fifth of it; a 1 px rim swell over 3.5 s) and sunken 1.5 px under a bank with a 0.25 shadow; see TerrainTypes.md's Phase 4 notes. Grass, gravel and stone have today's pair rules as their default edges: grass over nothing (outline, the light fringe bands as a fixed-mode rim gradient 9 px deep, a green fringe shadow, 4.5 px tufts), gravel over grass (darker dirt rim fading over 1.75 px, outline, green shadow; whole chips kept only where all of them lies clear of the rim), stone over grass (earthy rim fading into mortar over 1.5 px, outline, green shadow; stones left out only where their centre lies more than 0.5 px into the rim, the rest cut along it). Base colours (35, 114, 40), (146, 122, 88) and (98, 88, 76). Soft: height scales 2.5, 2 and 1.5 px; grass over nothing keeps its outline and fringe, gravel draws a 1.5 px band of dirt (126, 104, 76), stone a 1.5 px band of mortar (98, 88, 76). The planks are boards cut along the rim (cut stones, keep margin -0.5 px, no cut gap, bevel 18), with 2 px corner radii, level (no sink), a bridge that turns to its runs; their edge: no rim, an outline (50, 35, 24) and an earthy shadow (28, 20, 12) at 0.35 over 1.5 px, no soft band; a height scale of 0, so their edges stay straight in the soft look; see TerrainTypes.md's Phase 5 notes. The meadow is the grass with flowers: the grass's edge, radii, height scale and bank colour, still (no animation, since Phase 12), so its flowers stand still. Tall grass sways in the same wind 3 px (`MaxSwayAmplitude`), its clumps leaning whole, and draws an edge of its own over every material and nothing: a 2 px rim from (104, 162, 58) into its blades, an outline (26, 62, 18), a shadow (22, 54, 18) at 0.35 over 1.5 px and 5 px tufts, a fringe of blades, kept in soft (no band); see TerrainTypes.md's Phase 6 notes. Snow has the water's radii, a height scale of 2 px and sparkles only (8 frames per second; white sparkles every 4.5 s for a quarter of it), level, bank colour (182, 198, 222); its edge over everything: no outline, a 2.5 px rim fading from (196, 212, 236) and a white frost shadow (240, 245, 252) at 0.35 over 1.5 px. Ice has the water's radii, a height scale of 1.5 px, glints (244, 252, 255) every 3.5 s for 30 % of it and white sparkles every 5 s for a fifth of it, level, bank colour (136, 178, 208); its edge over everything: an outline (84, 128, 166), a 2 px bright bevel from (220, 238, 250) and a shadow (36, 56, 78) at 0.3 over a pixel; see TerrainTypes.md's Phase 7 notes. The ocean has the water's radii and height scale and its recipe's dark tones with no glints or sparkles lit (both duties 0); its waves (8 frames per second; 48 px apart, rolling in every 3.2 s at 8.5 degrees, 10 px wide in (44, 94, 150) with a crest (140, 184, 218) and a trough (14, 40, 80), whole within 40 px of the coast, in stretches further out at a stretch of 0.05), its swash up to 10 px (foam (240, 244, 248) and its shade (214, 226, 238), the film (150, 192, 222) and its back (112, 166, 204), the shore 15 % darker as it dries), no rim swell, sunk 6 px (`MaxSinkDepth`) under a bank with a 0.4 shadow, bank colour (140, 118, 82); its edge over grass and every other material without an override: an outline (36, 62, 64), an 8 px pale band in fixed steps ((126, 180, 212), (100, 158, 200), (74, 132, 182), (50, 104, 160), 2 px each) and a faint shadow (18, 40, 30) at 0.25 over a pixel, no soft band; see TerrainTypes.md's Phase 8 notes. Lava has the water's radii and a height scale of 1.5 px; its bubbles glint (250, 160, 60) and their rims sparkle (130, 26, 18), both every 4 s for a fifth of it, so each bubble swells, opens into a ring and goes out (8 frames per second); a 1 px rim swell over 3 s; sunk 1.5 px under a bank with a 0.35 shadow, bank colour (70, 34, 28); its edge over every material and nothing: an outline (48, 20, 18), a 4.5 px molten rim in fixed steps (yellow (255, 214, 110) to 2 px, orange (240, 132, 40) to 3 px, then (220, 70, 26) at 0.6 to 4.5 px, the first pixel under the outline) and a warm glow (150, 52, 24) at 0.4 over 4 px on what it meets, no soft band; see TerrainTypes.md's Phase 9 notes. |
  | [Assets/Textures/Terrain](../Assets/Textures/Terrain) | A folder per material, named after it: `Grass/`, `DryGrass/`, `Weeds/`, `Meadow/`, `TallGrass/`, `Snow/`, `Dirt/`, `Sand/`, `Mud/`, `Ice/`, `Shallows/`, `Water/`, `Ocean/` and `Lava/` (albedo and height each), `Gravel/`, `Plaza/`, `Stone/` and `Planks/` (albedo, height and cell field each; dry grass's, the weeds', the meadow's and tall grass's field is the ground under their clumps; the grass has none). 128 px textures (4 cells; grass's, dry grass's, the weeds', the meadow's and tall grass's 256 px, 8 cells; water's, the shallows' and the planks' 256 px, 8 cells; mud's, sand's, snow's, ice's, the ocean's and lava's 512 px, 16 cells), written by their recipes in `Assets/Settings/Terrain/Recipes` through `TerrainMaterialWriter` and imported by `TerrainTextureDefaults`. The renderer copies the heights into its height array for the soft look, which reads R to move the edges and G to move whole stones. The cell field's contract, which the shader reads, per texel: R and G the offset in whole pixels (x right, y up) to the centre pixel of the texel's chip or stone (its pixel nearest its seed, which points at itself), plus 128; B 128 plus the amount added to the material's base colour to give the base under the chip or stone (dirt or mortar), which is exactly what the albedo shows wherever no chip or stone covers it; A times 16, at least 1, for whole chips the chip's reach (the distance from its centre pixel to its farthest pixel, its shadow included, the same over the whole chip) and for cut stones the texel's distance to its stone's cell edge; A 0 marks a texel of no chip. The planks point every pixel straight across its board, at the board's middle row in its own column, since a board can be longer than a dock is wide (its centre pixel then points at itself as the contract asks). Height: R a smooth height whose slope stays under 0.2 per pixel (the recipes keep it under 0.1, as `materials.py` did; the soft look's height scale bound of 2.5 px holds for slopes up to 0.1, see TerrainBlending.md's Phase 2 notes), G one random value per chip or stone (0.5 where there is none, and for grass), B an animated material's glint or sparkle (0 for neither, 1 plus 127 times a glint's phase, or 128 plus 128 times a sparkle's; only the water highlights, ocean waves and lava bubbles recipes write any, snow's only sparkles). |
  | [Assets/Tiles/Ground](../Assets/Tiles/Ground) | `Tile_Grass`, `Tile_DryGrass`, `Tile_Weeds`, `Tile_Meadow`, `Tile_TallGrass`, `Tile_Snow`, `Tile_Dirt`, `Tile_Sand`, `Tile_Mud`, `Tile_Ice`, `Tile_Gravel`, `Tile_Plaza`, `Tile_Stone`, `Tile_Shallows`, `Tile_Water`, `Tile_Ocean`, `Tile_Lava` and `Tile_Planks`: the plain `Tile` of each ground material, without a collider, held by the palette of the same name in `Assets/Tiles/Palettes` (`Grass_Palette` and so on) (`Walls_Palette` holds the walls' data tile). Each tile's sprite is its material's swatch in [Assets/Sprites/Terrain](../Assets/Sprites/Terrain) (`grass_swatch.png` and so on: the albedo's bottom-left 32 px cell, a single 32 PPU sprite), seen only in the Tile Palette, since ground layers are data tilemaps without a `TilemapRenderer`. Written by `TerrainTileWriter` (see Editor tools). The ground layers hold them: the scene generator, the brushes, `StressTestMap` and `AreaTerrain` (as the ground layers' fill tile) write them. `Assets/Tiles/Raised` holds `Tile_Raised` and `Tile_Ramp`, the `RaisedGroundTile`s of raised ground and of a ramp (swatches `raised_swatch.png` and `ramp_swatch.png`, generated), held by `Raised_Palette`; the scene generator writes them on a grid's `RaisedTilemap`. |
- Dual-grid walls ([Assets/Scripts/World/DualGrid](../Assets/Scripts/World/DualGrid), editor tools in [Assets/Editor/DualGrid](../Assets/Editor/DualGrid)): a `DualGridTilemapModule` on the painted data tilemap keeps a half-cell-offset render tilemap of `DualGridRuleTile`s in sync, each render cell picking its sprite from the four data cells around its corner. Since Phase 4 of TerrainBlending.md only the walls use it (`walls_DualGridRuleTile`, whose 32 px sheet `WallSheetRecipe` generates; see Editor tools). Adopted from skner's DualGrid 2.1.0 (MIT, license kept beside the code). On a chunk of a seamless world its `WorldChunk` gives the module its cells and its loaded neighbours' wall modules (`internal SetChunk(cells, neighbours)`, `HasChunk`, `ChunkCells`): its rules read a neighbour's data cells past its border (`DataTileForRules`, which `DualGridRuleTile` reads through the module), it draws the render tiles on its own cells, and those along its upper and right border only while the chunk owning them is not loaded (`OwnsRenderTile`), refreshes its border when a neighbour comes or goes, and follows a neighbour's data changes along it (in edit mode `DualGridTilemapPersistentListener` forwards them, `ReadsNeighbourData`). So a wall across a border shows the same sprites as on one grid, each drawn once. Full description, authoring steps and editor behaviour in [DualGrid.md](DualGrid.md).
- Runtime terrain: [AreaTerrain](../Assets/Scripts/World/AreaTerrain.cs) on a world chunk's `Grid` lists the tilemaps gameplay may change (a dual-grid data tilemap, painted through its module, or a plain tilemap with a fill tile) and its sync prefab (`AssignSyncPrefabIfMissing`, editor only, for the authoring tools). `TrySetCells(layer, cells, filled)` works only with authority, writes the tiles in bulk and records a [TerrainEdit](../Assets/Scripts/World/TerrainEdit.cs) per cell (layer index, cell, filled; `INetworkSerializable`), raising `EditRecorded(index)`; `ApplyReplicated(edits)` applies the host's edits on a client without recording them; `RestoreEdits(edits)` applies persisted edits and records them as just made (skipping, with a warning, layers it does not have). [NetworkAreaTerrain](../Assets/Scripts/World/NetworkAreaTerrain.cs) (on [AreaTerrainSync.prefab](../Assets/Prefabs/Network/AreaTerrainSync.prefab)) replicates a chunk's; `ChunkTerrainSync` (see Worlds and streaming) spawns it. Every chunk of the worlds lists its `GrassTilemap` (filled with `Tile_Grass`) and its walls when it has them (`WorldAuthoring.SetUpTerrainEdits`, which `CreateChunk`, `AddWalls` and the brush's walls copy run). A layer's index is its place in `AreaTerrain`'s own list, not in the terrain renderer, so it does not depend on which ground layers a grid draws; every machine loads the same scene and so agrees on it, a new layer is appended after those listed, and the scene generator's writer and Convert Ground never remove a tilemap `AreaTerrain` lists. A chunk's edits persist in its world's `WorldState`: `ChunkTerrainSync` restores them as the chunk loads, before its sync object spawns, and records each new one. Nothing in gameplay edits terrain yet; in play mode the editor menu `Tools/TopDownRPG/Dev/Toggle Terrain Cell At View Centre` (`AreaTerrainDevMenu`) makes edits by hand (see [Multiplayer.md](Multiplayer.md#checking-runtime-terrain-edits)).
- Props: none for now. The borrowed prop art (four sheets in `Assets/Sprites/Props` sliced into 65 sprites, and a prefab for each in `Assets/Prefabs/Props/<Trees|Bushes|Rocks|Plants|Statues|Town|Buildings>`) was removed, and the generators place no props until the game has its own. What stays is the layout every prop follows, in two base prefabs, the placement tool and the snapping (see Editor tools), and the generators' prop steps. A prop is laid out like the player and mob prefabs: the root stands at the prop's base, and a `Visuals` child half a unit above it holds the `SpriteRenderer` (on the `Entities` sorting layer, its sprite pivoted at the middle of its base, half a unit up, at 32 PPU like the characters) and the `YPositionSorter`. That is where the player's `Visuals` sits above its feet, so props and the player sort against each other by where they stand; a tall sprite is not sorted by its centre, which would draw a tree over a player standing behind it. Every prop is a variant of one of two bases:
  - [Prop.prefab](../Assets/Prefabs/Props/Prop.prefab): the root on `Default` with nothing on it, for walk-through props (grass, flowers, mushrooms).
  - [BlockingProp.prefab](../Assets/Prefabs/Props/BlockingProp.prefab): the root on the `Obstacles` layer with a solid `BoxCollider2D` standing on it and lying under the sprite. Each variant sets its size. It is the prop's equivalent of the feet collider on a unit's root. The `Visuals` child stays on `Default`, because the camera culls `Obstacles`; this is the same split as the collision tilemap and its render tilemap. Bodies collide with the footprint, mobs cannot see through it (their line of sight uses `Obstacles`), and the chunk's `NavigationGrid2D` blocks every cell it overlaps, so paths go around it. Place props with the prop placement tool (see Editor tools), which snaps each footprint onto exactly the cells it needs; Snap Selected Props To Grid fixes props moved by hand.

  A variant shifts `Visuals` sideways so the sprite's centre sits on the root, rounded to whole pixels, and sizes its footprint, in sprite pixels, as the bounding box of the solid ground-contact part of the art, even when that is off centre: a tree's root flare up to where the trunk narrows, a statue's plinth, a stool's legs, the lower body of a rock or bush, a building's walls. When slicing a prop from a sheet, check its rect for strips of the next tile along the edges.

  Props are static scenery placed in chunk scenes, identical on every machine, so nothing about them is networked.
- Adding a world: with the World Editor (`Tools/TopDownRPG/World/World Editor`, see Editor tools); the steps are in [WorldStreaming.md](WorldStreaming.md#authoring-a-world).

### Worlds and streaming

Seamless worlds, specified in [WorldStreaming.md](WorldStreaming.md) (Phases 1 to 8 so far: worlds and chunks streaming offline, terrain and walls drawn across chunk borders, navigation across chunks, authoring (the World Editor, the Terrain Brush across chunk borders, ownership validation and persistent ids), mobs per chunk, world exits with trips between worlds offline, multiplayer: each player's world replicated, trips by RPC, per-chunk readiness and release, home-chunk routing and per-chunk terrain sync, persistence: world states, mob respawns, terrain edits and players' places saved and continued, see Save data; and death and respawn: waystones discovered per player, a dead player sent to the nearest one on a trip within its world, nothing restarting, and the area path gone, every session in worlds). A world is a root scene and up to 16 x 16 chunk scenes of 64 x 64 cells, at a fixed origin in one global layout; chunk scenes hold their content at world positions (each chunk's `Grid` sits at the world's origin, its tilemaps in cells counted from it). Code in `Assets/Scripts/Core/Worlds` and `Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming`. The worlds (Scenes, above): Testlands, the starting world, the Sandbox and its interior, the dev stress world, all painted by hand, and the Overworld, generated in the editor ([WorldGeneration.md](WorldGeneration.md)) into chunk scenes that stream, draw, navigate, spawn and save as the painted ones do.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [WorldDefinition](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Worlds/WorldDefinition.cs) | ScriptableObject (Core): `WorldId` (its asset GUID, set by the editor), the root scene's `SceneDefinition`, `Origin` (world units of its cell (0, 0)), `SizeInChunks` (at most `MaxChunksPerSide`, 16), `Chunks` (coordinates and `SceneDefinition` each; a missing chunk has no scene and loads nothing), `Entries` (name, position from the origin, waystone; the first is `DefaultEntry`). `TryGetChunkScene`, `HasChunk`, `HasChunkAt(worldPosition)`, `FindEntry` (the default, with a warning, for an unknown name), `EntryPosition(entryName)` (that entry's world position, or the world's middle without entries), `OwnsScene(path)`, `ToWorld`/`ToLocal`, `LocalBounds`. Editor-only `Configure`, `SetChunk`, `SetEntries`; `internal static Create` builds one with transient scenes for tests. |
| [ChunkCoords](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Worlds/ChunkCoords.cs) | Static (Core): `Size` (64), `ChunkOf(cell)` (rounding down, below zero too), `CellOf(position)`, `Bounds(chunk)`, `Around(cell, extents)`, `AddOverlapping`, `Overlaps`, `SquaredDistance(cell, chunk)`. |
| [IWorldSession](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Worlds/IWorldSession.cs) | Core interface `GameFlow` enters and leaves worlds through: `IsActive`, `EnterAsync(world, position)`, `PrepareAsync(world, position)` (loads a world around a place for this machine's simulation without moving anyone: the host before spawning a joining player there), `JoinAsync()` (a joined client's start, returning the world the host put its player in, or null when the session ended first), `ArriveAsync(world, entryName)` (the end of a local player's trip), `LeaveAsync()`. |
| [ChunkStreamer](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/ChunkStreamer.cs) | Plain C#, the decisions. Interests ([StreamInterest](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/StreamInterest.cs): a world and a world position) come in with the game time (`SetInterests`); `TryNext` gives one [StreamAction](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/StreamAction.cs) at a time (`LoadWorld`, `LoadChunk`, `UnloadChunk`, `UnloadWorld`) and `Complete(success)` records how it ended (a failed world or chunk is not tried again). Order: a world a player is in; the missing chunk nearest a player within the view plus the load margin; a loaded chunk no player keeps within the view plus the margin plus the unload hysteresis (only in worlds someone is in); then, for a world nobody has been in for the empty-world grace, its chunks and itself. A chunk its `IChunkHolds` (the host's session) says a client holds never unloads, so neither does its world, until it is released or `ReleaseAll` (the session ending) lets everything go. `ReleaseAll` lets everything go (`Resume` streams again); `IsSettled(interest)`, `IsWorldLoaded`, `IsChunkLoaded`, `IsBusy`, `IsEmpty`; static `ExtentsOf(viewCells, margin)`, the cells a rectangle reaches around a player's cell. |
| [WorldStreaming](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/WorldStreaming.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`) and `IWorldSession`. Each frame, while a session runs, it gives the streamer (made with the session as its `IChunkHolds`) the players it streams for (every registered player in a world with `IGameAuthority`, so the host streams for every player in every world; else the local one; their world from `PlayerWorlds`) and runs its next action: a world's root scene loaded additively under the Gameplay scope with the `WorldDefinition` enqueued, a chunk under its world's scope with a `ChunkIdentity` enqueued, recorded in `Worlds`; unloads remove them from `Worlds` first. A failure is logged and completes the action as failed. The client's half of the handshake (`IChunkHandshake`, nothing offline or on the host): a loaded chunk is reported ready with the local player's trip count; when that count changes, every loaded chunk of the player's new world is reported again (a chunk kept from an earlier visit is up, and its report from before the trip may have been ignored); and an unload first releases the chunk and waits for the host's acknowledgement. `EnterAsync` waits for the local player (up to 600 frames), puts every player this machine simulates in the world and teleports it to a free slot (`EntrySlots`) at the start position (an entry's or a saved place), snaps the camera, and waits until `IsSettled` there; `PrepareAsync` streams a place as an interest of its own (`preparations`) until `IsSettled` there, then drops it (at once when no session streams); `JoinAsync` waits (up to 60 s of real time, or until the session ends) for the host to spawn the local player in a world, snaps the camera and waits until `IsSettled` around the player; `LeaveAsync` releases everything, forgets the trips' arrivals, waits until the streamer is empty and takes the players out of their worlds. It also streams each held trip arrival (`TripArrivals.AddInterests`) as an interest of its own, so a trip's target world and the chunks around its entry load while the traveller's screen fades out. `ArriveAsync`, the end of the local player's trip, teleports the local player (already in the target world, put there by the trip's authority) to a free slot at the entry, snaps the camera and waits until `IsSettled` there; the world it left is untouched and waits out the empty-world grace like any world nobody is in. |
| [Worlds](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/Worlds.cs), [LoadedWorld](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/LoadedWorld.cs) | Gameplay-scope registry of the loaded worlds: each `LoadedWorld` has its definition, root scene, scope, `WorldLighting`, `Navigation` (the `WorldNavigation` its scope registers, resolved by `WorldStreaming` as the root scene loads) and the scenes of its loaded chunks. `TryGet`, events `WorldLoaded`, `WorldUnloading`, `ChunkLoaded`, `ChunkUnloading`. |
| [PlayerWorlds](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/PlayerWorlds.cs) | Gameplay-scope record of the world each registered player is in, with its trip count and its latest trip's entry (`Get`, `TripOf`, `EntryOf`, `Changed` when the world or the trip count changes): `Set` (no trip; taking a player out of every world forgets its trips), `Travel(player, world, entry)` (an approved trip: one more, even back into the same world), `Apply(player, world, trip, entry)` (a client following the host, through `PlayerWorldSync`); forgets a player that leaves the registry. |
| [ChunkIdentity](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/ChunkIdentity.cs) | Which chunk a chunk scene is (world, coordinates), registered into its scope. |
| [WorldLighting](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/WorldLighting.cs) | Marker on the object of a world's root scene that holds its global lights (`SetShown`). In play mode it hides its object in `Awake` (execution order -1000, before the lights under it are enabled), since URP logs an error whenever a second global light is enabled, as when a trip's target world loads beside the one on screen; `LocalWorldPresentation` shows the world on screen. |
| [LocalWorldPresentation](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/LocalWorldPresentation.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`, with the `WorldStreamingSettings`): shows the lighting of the presented world and hides every other loaded world's, as a world loads (at once, so two global lights never draw together) and whenever the local player, its world or its cell change. The presented world (`PresentedWorld`) is the local player's world once the player stands on that world's cells: a trip puts the player in its target world before moving it there, so the world being left stays lit and drawn behind the fade out until the player arrives. URP warns about two global lights on a sorting layer and lights everything with one of them, whatever world it is in (WorldStreaming.md's Phase 0). Only the local world draws: it records each loaded chunk's `WorldChunk` (`ChunkLoaded`, `ChunkUnloading`) and enables a chunk's terrain once the local player's interest rectangle (`LoadExtents`) overlaps it, keeping it until it leaves the rectangle widened by the hysteresis (`KeepExtents`), and disables every chunk of another world (`ShouldDraw`), so the host builds no ground for chunks it loads for other players. Each frame it only compares the local player's cell with the last one. |
| [WorldChunk](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/WorldChunk.cs) | `[ExecuteAlways]`, `[DefaultExecutionOrder(-100)]` on a chunk scene's `Grid`: its `World`, `Coords` (`Cells`), the `CoastMap` baked for it and `CoastSource`, what that map was baked from (`WorldCoastBake.SourceOf`; serialized; `Configure`). When enabled it makes the grid's terrain renderer a chunk with a `SkirtCells` (4) skirt and its wall modules chunk modules, then links with each of its eight neighbours of the same world whose scene is open (`SceneManager.GetSceneByPath` of the world's chunk scene, so at runtime and in the editor alike): each gives the other its ground layers and raised ground in the strip along their border and its wall modules of the same rule tile. Disabled or unloaded, it unlinks and its neighbours draw their own edges again. `Link`, `Unlink` (`internal`), `Neighbours`, `RefreshLinks` (after an editor tool changes its layers or walls). In play mode its terrain starts disabled (`IsDrawn`, `SetDrawn`) until `LocalWorldPresentation` shows it. |
| [WorldStreamingSettings](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/WorldStreamingSettings.cs) | ScriptableObject: `ViewCells` (20 x 12), `LoadMargin` (12), `UnloadHysteresis` (8), `EmptyWorldGraceSeconds` (10); `LoadExtents` and `KeepExtents` (`ChunkStreamer.ExtentsOf`: 22 x 18 and 30 x 26 cells each side), `CreateStreamer`. The margins come from WorldStreaming.md's Phase 2 load times. `KeepsHomeChunkLoaded(config, out problem)` is the leash rule: a mob's `LeashCells` plus its `DetectionRadius` under the load extents' smaller side (18), so any player it can detect has its home chunk in its interest rectangle, and plus its `LoseTargetDistance` under the keep extents' smaller side (26), so that chunk never unloads under it while it chases. `MobLeashConstraintTests` checks every `MobConfig` asset against the project's settings (the Weasel's 8 + 6 and 8 + 8). |
| [ChunkMobSpawner](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/ChunkMobSpawner.cs) | Chunk-scope entry point (`IPostStartable`, so it runs after `ChunkNavigation.Start` added the chunk's page). Offline or on the host, `PostStart` spawns one mob per spawn point of the chunk through the chunk's scope (`MobSpawning`) into the chunk's scene, gives it the chunk's area as its home (`HomeArea`, `MobController.SetHome`) and network-spawns it in a session as an object of its chunk (`Spawn(instance, homeChunk)`: visible to the clients holding the chunk; `Mobs` lists them). It despawns them when `Worlds.ChunkUnloading` names its chunk, before the scene unloads, and anything left when its scope is disposed. A mob stays in its home chunk's scene its whole life, wherever it walks, so it goes with that chunk. In a session `RegisterNetworkPrefabs` (the scope's build callback) registers each distinct mob prefab for its chunk with `ChunkNetworkPrefabs`, on a client so the host's mobs arrive in this chunk's scope, on the host so its spawns carry their home chunk, and `Dispose` unregisters them. On a client it spawns nothing. Persistence, offline and on the host: it follows each mob's `Health.Died` and records the death in its chunk's record of the world's `WorldState` by the spawn point's `PersistentId`, at `SessionSave.Time` (a spawn point without an id warns, and its mob comes back at every load); `ShouldSpawn` (private) skips a spawn point whose mob `StaysDead` by its `MobConfig` (`Respawns`, `RespawnDelaySeconds`). |
| [ChunkNetworkPrefabs](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/ChunkNetworkPrefabs.cs) | Gameplay-scope registry of the network prefabs the loaded chunks of this machine's worlds register in a session (`Register(prefab, chunk, resolver, scene)`, `Unregister`, `IsRegistered`). Netcode keeps one handler per prefab while several chunks list the same mob, so it installs one routing handler per prefab through `INetworkObjectSpawner` when the first chunk registers it and removes it when the last one unregisters. The handler is a `NetworkPrefabInstanceHandlerWithData<NetworkChunkId>`: the host's spawns carry their home chunk as instantiation data, and a client's copy is created through the `InjectingNetworkPrefabHandler` of that chunk, wherever the object stands (a mob that walked into a neighbour still lands in its home chunk's scene); a copy without one, or whose chunk is not registered (with a warning), goes to the registered chunk holding its spawn position, or the nearest. The host registers too, since Netcode sets instantiation data only for a prefab with such a handler; it never instantiates through it. `Dispose` removes every handler. |
| [ChunkTerrainSync](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/ChunkTerrainSync.cs) | Chunk-scope entry point for a chunk with an `AreaTerrain`, per-chunk terrain sync. In a session `RegisterNetworkPrefab` (the scope's build callback) registers the terrain's sync prefab for the chunk with `ChunkNetworkPrefabs`, so a client's copy is injected with this chunk's terrain. Offline and on the host `Start` restores the chunk's edits from its record in the world's `WorldState` (`AreaTerrain.RestoreEdits`, so they are the terrain's own and replicate) and records every later edit there (`EditRecorded`, until disposed). On the host it then instantiates the sync prefab through the chunk's scope into the chunk's scene and spawns it as an object of the chunk (`Spawned`), and despawns it when `Worlds.ChunkUnloading` names the chunk or the scope is disposed. On a client it restores and records nothing. |
| [WorldPlayers](../Assets/Scripts/World/Streaming/WorldPlayers.cs) | The players in one world, an `IPlayerRegistry` (its `World`): the Gameplay scope's registry narrowed by `PlayerWorlds`, following players into and out of the world (`PlayerAdded`, `PlayerRemoved`) without allocating per frame. The world's scope registers it as its `IPlayerRegistry`, so the chunks' mobs consider only the players in their world. |
| [PersistentId](../Assets/Scripts/World/PersistentId.cs) | A stable id for a placed object whose state persists (`Id`, `HasId`): a GUID string the editor assigns once and keeps unique within the world (`PersistentIds`, `PersistentIdWatcher`). Every `MobSpawnPoint` and `WorldExit` in a chunk scene has one (the Sandbox's twelve spawn points got theirs in Phase 5). A trip request names its exit by it (`WorldExits.TryFind`), and the world state keys killed mobs by it. |
| [WorldExit](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/WorldExit.cs) | A marker in a chunk scene (`[RequireComponent(typeof(PersistentId))]`, a cyan gizmo): `TargetWorld`, `TargetEntry` (empty for the default entry), `SizeInCells`, `Id` (its persistent id), `CellsIn(world)` (its lower-left cell holds its position). Placed under an `Exits` root by `WorldAuthoring.AddExit`; validated as owned by its chunk. |
| [WorldExits](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/WorldExits.cs) | Gameplay-scope registry of the exits in the loaded chunks of every loaded world: it reads a chunk's exits (`SceneQuery`) on `Worlds.ChunkLoaded` and forgets them on `ChunkUnloading`. `TryFind(world, id)`, `TryFindAt(world, cell)`, `Add` (tests), `Count`. |
| [TripRequest](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/TripRequest.cs), [TripApproval](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/TripApproval.cs) | Plain data: a player, an exit id and, for a client's request, the position its own machine has for the player (`HasPosition`); a player, its target world, the entry's name and its position. |
| [ITripAuthority](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/ITripAuthority.cs), [LocalTripAuthority](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/LocalTripAuthority.cs) | Where a player's machine sends trip requests (`Request`) and hears back which trips go (`Approved`). `LocalTripAuthority` is the checks, offline and on the host: `TryApprove` checks that the exit is loaded in the player's world, that the player stands within `ReachCells` (1) of its cells (at the request's position when it has one, else its body's), that it leads to a world `GameScenes` lists, and that the world has the entry (an unknown name falls back to the default, with `FindEntry`'s warning), and refuses a player whose trip is under way; then it holds the arrival in `TripArrivals` (so the target loads), puts the player in the target world for one more trip (`PlayerWorlds.Travel`) and gives the approval with the resolved entry, through `Send(player, world, entry)` (internal), which does that last part without any check for a trip the authority starts itself (a respawn). Its own `Request` (tests) logs a refusal with the reason. |
| [NetworkTripAuthority](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/NetworkTripAuthority.cs), [ITripLink](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/ITripLink.cs) | The Gameplay scope's `ITripAuthority`. Offline and on the host `Request` goes to `Approve`: `LocalTripAuthority.TryApprove`, then `Approved` (a refusal is a warning); a client's player hears of its trip through its networked world instead. On a client (`SendsToHost`: in a session, not hosting) `Request` goes through the player's `ITripLink` (`PlayerWorldSync`; `LinkFinder`, internal, swaps the lookup in tests) with the position this machine has for the player, a warning without one; `Deliver(approval)`, from `PlayerWorldSync`, holds the arrival in `TripArrivals` and raises `Approved`. `Send(player, world, entry)` (offline or the host): a trip no request asked for, a dead player's to its waystone (`PlayerRespawner`), through `LocalTripAuthority.Send`, then `Approved`; a client's player hears of it through its networked world, as of any trip. |
| [TripArrivals](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/TripArrivals.cs) | Gameplay-scope record of the trips under way: per player, the target world and entry position (`Hold`, `IsHeld`, `TryGet`, `Release`, `Clear`). `AddInterests(interests, playerWorlds)` adds each to the streamed interests and drops it once the player stands within two cells of the entry in that world (`HasArrived`), or is gone; allocates nothing once warm. |
| [WaystoneRules](../Assets/Scripts/Core/Worlds/WaystoneRules.cs) | Static (Core), the waystone rules over a player's discoveries (a list of `GameSaveData.Waystone`): `IsDiscovered` (the world's default entry always, a waystone entry once the list names it in that world, a plain entry never), `Contains`, `TryFindNearest(world, position, discovered)` (the discovered entry nearest in a straight line, the first listed on a tie; false without entries), `TryFindDiscovery(world, position, reach, discovered)` (the first undiscovered waystone, in list order, whose cell lies within `reach` cells of the position's along each axis). |
| [WaystoneDiscovery](../Assets/Scripts/World/Waystones/WaystoneDiscovery.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`), offline and on the host only (it has every player's position): each frame it compares every living player's cell in its world with the last one, allocating nothing once warm, and on a new cell asks `WaystoneRules.TryFindDiscovery` with `GameplaySettings.WaystoneReachCells` (2). A discovery is recorded in the `SessionSave` under the player's id (`PlayerIds`), raised on `WaystoneNotices`, and sent to the player's machine through its `IWaystoneLink` (`PlayerWorldSync`; `LinkFinder`, internal, swaps the lookup in tests). |
| [WaystoneNotices](../Assets/Scripts/World/Waystones/WaystoneNotices.cs), [IWaystoneLink](../Assets/Scripts/World/Waystones/IWaystoneLink.cs) | Gameplay-scope news of discoveries on this machine (`Discovered(player, entryName)`, `Raise`): every player's offline and on the host, its own player's on a client (raised by `PlayerWorldSync` from the host's RPC); and how the host tells a client's machine (`SendWaystoneDiscovered`). |
| [PlayerIds](../Assets/Scripts/World/Persistence/PlayerIds.cs) | Gameplay-scope record of each registered player's player id (`IdOf`): its owner's on the host while it is a spawned network object, this install's otherwise (`IPlayerIdSource`), learned as it registers, while its owner is still known, and kept until another player registers after it leaves, so it stays readable while the registry announces the removal. `GameSaveRecorder`, `WaystoneDiscovery` and `PlayerRespawner` key a player's place and waystones by it. |
| [ExitTriggerGuard](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/ExitTriggerGuard.cs) | Plain re-trigger protection: `Step(onExit)` fires once when the player steps onto an exit from a cell that is none; it starts unarmed and `Disarm` (after a trip) waits for the player to stand on no exit again. |
| [LocalTripTraveller](../Assets/Scripts/World/Trips/LocalTripTraveller.cs) | Gameplay-scope entry point (`IStartable`, `ITickable`), the local player's side of trips. Each frame outside transitions it compares the local player's cell with the last one; on a new cell it asks `WorldExits.TryFindAt` and the guard, and requests a trip through `ITripAuthority` when the guard fires (on a client, an RPC to the host). On an approval for the local player it waits for any running transition, stops the player (`ClearOrders`) and runs `GameFlow.TravelAsync`; a session that ended meanwhile releases the arrival instead. `IsTravelling`. |

Online (Phase 7) every machine knows each player's world, a client streams only its own player's world and the host every player's, and a client sees a chunk's mobs and terrain edits only while it holds that chunk (see Networking). A player walking into a world exit travels to another world (Phase 6): the trip is a local transition (`GameFlow.TravelAsync`) with gameplay input off and game time running, and the world left behind stays loaded for the empty-world grace (10 s), so a trip straight back reloads nothing. A player in a world paths on its world's `WorldNavigation` (Phase 3): each loaded chunk's page joined to its loaded neighbours', so steps, costs, the cliff and regions run across borders and a path ends at the edge of the loaded chunks. Each chunk spawns its mobs as it loads and takes them out before it unloads (Phase 4): they path on the same navigation, consider only the players in their world, patrol inside their home chunk and give up on a target or a path more than their leash (8 cells) beyond it. Ground, raised ground and walls draw across chunk borders (Phase 2): each chunk's `WorldChunk` links its renderer and walls with its loaded neighbours', and each chunk's coast map is baked from the whole world by `WorldAuthoring.BakeCoastMaps` (`WorldCoastBake`).
- Scene generation: `Tools/TopDownRPG/World/Scene Generator` fills the active scene's standalone grid (a scratch scene its New scratch scene button makes, `WorldAuthoring.CreateScratchScene`) from an `AreaGenerator` asset; it refuses a world chunk's grid, since writing layouts into worlds belongs to the world generation plan (WorldStreaming.md's Later). It repaints the terrain layers as plain data tilemaps (creating the missing ones), keeps any other layer the grid draws that is painted, at its place in the style's layer order, and removes the empty ones, sets up the grid's terrain renderer and `AreaTerrain`'s fill tiles for them, and replaces the props under `Props` and every mob spawn point, as one undo step. Its Convert Ground sets a grid's ground layers up as the generator's without repainting them, creating the ones the grid lacks and keeping or removing the others the same way (`GroundLayerMigration`). Generation runs in the editor and its result is saved in the scene.
