The six generated areas (the Clearing, the Village Forest, the Lakeside, the Snowfield, the Coast and the Lava Fields) were made this way and went with the area path in WorldStreaming.md's Phase 9; their generator assets and seeds stay, for world generation to bring them back as regions of the first world. See [SceneGeneration.md](SceneGeneration.md).

## Dev tools

### Status panel

[StatusDebugPanel](../Assets/Scripts/Dev/Statuses/StatusDebugPanel.cs) (`TopDownRPG.DevTools`, so editor and development builds only): created once per play session by `[RuntimeInitializeOnLoadMethod]` and kept in `DontDestroyOnLoad`. F2 toggles an IMGUI list of the status catalog (buffs `+`, debuffs `-`); F3 applies the selected status to the unit under the cursor (`PointerTargetPicker` rules) and F4 to the local player; a button clears the local player's statuses; the last outcome is shown. Statuses go through `StatusEffectService` without a source, so faction rules do not apply, and only with authority (offline or host; a client gets `NoAuthority`). Because the Gameplay scope cannot reference DevTools, the panel finds the `GameplayLifetimeScope` itself (cached) and resolves `StatusEffectService`, `CombatSettings`, `LocalPlayerTracker`, `PointerTargetPicker` and `Camera` from its container when used. `ApplyToSelf(definition)` and `ApplyToUnitAt(screenPoint, definition)` are public for tests.

### Stress test

Assembly `TopDownRPG.DevTools`, the dev-only stress world `Assets/Dev/Worlds/StressTest` (see Scenes), mob variant `Weasel_StressTest.prefab` using `Mob_StressTest` (zero attack damage), in `Assets/Dev/StressTest`.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [StressTestMap](../Assets/Scripts/Dev/StressTest/StressTestMap.cs) | `[DefaultExecutionOrder(-1000)]`; in `Awake` generates a seeded map of `width` x `height` cells (128 x 128, the whole stress world) from its grid's cell (0, 0), walled along its outer ring and inside in an Open, Scattered or Corridors layout, its middle kept clear: it fills the plain ground tilemap (`groundTilemap`, the chunk's `GrassTilemap`, a layer of its `TerrainBlendRenderer`) with the ground tile (`groundTile`, `Tile_Grass`) in one batch and paints the walls through the collision tilemap's module (`DualGridTilemapModule.SetDataTiles`). On a chunk's grid (with a `WorldChunk`) it paints only that chunk's cells, every chunk laying the map out alike from the seed so walls run on across borders, and leaves the navigation page to `ChunkNavigation`, which builds it in `Start`; on a standalone grid it rebuilds the `NavigationGrid2D`. Logs an error and generates nothing when a reference is missing. |
| [StressTestSpawner](../Assets/Scripts/Dev/StressTest/StressTestSpawner.cs) | In the stress world's root scene, injected from its world scope (listed in the `WorldLifetimeScope`'s auto-inject objects). Once every chunk of the world has its page in the `WorldNavigation`, a seeded spawn of its initial mobs through the world's scope (so they path on its navigation and see its players) on reachable cells near itself and across the world, then it stops updating; `SpawnNearPlayer`, `SpawnAcrossMap`, `Mobs`, `MobCount`, `ClearMobs`. Its mobs have no home chunk, so no leash holds them. |
| [StressTestOverlay](../Assets/Scripts/Dev/StressTest/StressTestOverlay.cs) | IMGUI overlay: FPS, mob counts per state, `ProfilerRecorder` stats for FindPath/BuildPath and GC alloc per frame, spawn/clear buttons. |

Play it by opening `World_StressTest.unity` (or one of its chunks) and pressing Play: Boot From Main boots into any world asset, listed in `GameScenes` or not.

## Editor tools

Menu root: `Tools/TopDownRPG/`.

| Type | Role |
|---|---|
| [PlayModeBootstrapper](../Assets/Editor/Scenes/PlayModeBootstrapper.cs) | Boot From Main toggle (EditorPrefs), saves modified scenes before Play, records open scenes as an `EditorBootRequest`, skips Test Runner sessions. |
| [ProjectScenes](../Assets/Editor/Scenes/ProjectScenes.cs) | Constants for Main scene path, GameScenes asset path, menu root. |
| [SceneSetMenu](../Assets/Editor/Scenes/SceneSetMenu.cs) | `Open Scenes/` menu: Main; Main + Main Menu; Main + Gameplay + Starting World (its root scene). |
| [SceneBuildValidator](../Assets/Editor/Scenes/SceneBuildValidator.cs) | Pre-build check (and `Validate Build Scenes` menu): Main first in build list, every `SceneDefinition` assigned and enabled in the build, but those under `DevFolder` (`Assets/Dev/`: the stress world's, played from the editor only). |
| [SceneDefinitionEditor.cs](../Assets/Editor/Scenes/SceneDefinitionEditor.cs) | Inspector with a `SceneAsset` picker + build-list warning; also contains `SceneDefinitionPathSync` (`AssetPostprocessor` refreshing paths when scenes move). |
| [PlayerSlashPrefabBootstrap](../Assets/Editor/Combat/PlayerSlashPrefabBootstrap.cs) | On editor load, (re)creates `SwordSlash.prefab` if missing/invalid and links it into `Sword.asset`; menu `Rebuild Player Slash Prefab`. |
| [VContainerDiagnosticsBuildGuard](../Assets/Editor/VContainerDiagnosticsBuildGuard.cs) | Turns off VContainer diagnostics for non-development builds and restores it afterwards. |
| [PixelArtTextureDefaults](../Assets/Editor/PixelArtTextureDefaults.cs) | `AssetPostprocessor` for `Assets/Sprites/`: sprite, 32 PPU, point filter, no mips, uncompressed RGBA32; menu `Tools/Textures/Apply Pixel Art Defaults To Sprites`. |
| [PixelArtEditorSnapSettings](../Assets/Editor/PixelArtEditorSnapSettings.cs) | Sets editor move snap to 1/32; menu `Tools/Pixel Art/Apply Move Snap (1/32)`. |
| [TerrainBlendRendererEditor](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainBlendRendererEditor.cs) | Inspector of `TerrainBlendRenderer`: its settings, with each layer's navigation priority kept equal to its index, the draw order. Whenever the inspector changes the renderer (reordering, adding or removing layers), it sets the priority of each layer's navigation source (`TerrainBlendRenderer.NavigationSourceOf`) in the same undo step (`SyncNavigationPriorities`); when they disagree anyway (`HasNavigationPrioritiesInStep`), an error box and Set Navigation Priorities From Draw Order fix them. |
| [NavigationTerrainSource2DEditor](../Assets/Editor/Navigation/NavigationTerrainSource2DEditor.cs) | Inspector of `NavigationTerrainSource2D`: its settings, with the priority read-only and a note when a `TerrainBlendRenderer` draws its tilemap as a ground layer (`TerrainBlendRenderer.FindLayerDrawing`), since that layer's place sets it. |
| [TerrainMaterialRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainMaterialRecipe.cs) and its kinds | Editor-only ScriptableObjects that generate a `TerrainMaterial`'s textures, like `AreaGenerator` generates an area. The abstract recipe holds the material it fills, a seed, the repeat in cells (32 px each, up to 16) and the waves of the smooth height (normalised into 0-1, and scaled down when the red channel's slope could reach 0.1 per pixel); `Generate(seed)` draws every random value from one `System.Random` seeded with it and returns a [TerrainTextures](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainTextures.cs): plain pixel arrays, rows from the bottom (y up), with the base colour and cell mode, touching no asset. Waves count whole periods across the texture, so everything repeats with it. Kinds (each a material's look; see TerrainBlending.md's Phase 1b and Phase 5 notes): [SandRipplesRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/SandRipplesRecipe.cs) (sand colour, tint waves and grit; ripples, whole across the texture along x and y, bent by wobble waves and shown where cover waves rise above a level, each a shaded trough under a lit crest of a band width; specks on a jittered grid, light or dark; clumps, none by default: low bumps on another jittered grid, each kept at a chance, an ellipse of a height between two values and a stretch times as wide, its middle in the middle colour and its outer half lit where it faces the light and shaded where it faces away, kept apart; sparkles, none by default: pixels off the clumps' shade on a third grid, each with a sparkle phase, the sparkle colour handed to the material; the Snow material is this recipe with both), [MudPuddlesRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/MudPuddlesRecipe.cs) (mud colour, tint waves and grit; puddles where puddle waves (many short ones, set for 16 cells) rise above a level, in wetter mud above a lower level, both levels lowered and the mud darkened by cluster waves scaled by a cluster amount and shade, their pool, bank shade on the top-left edge, wet rim on the bottom-right one and two-pixel reflections; ridges on a jittered grid, each kept at a chance with a length between the shortest and longest, a lit row over a shaded row a pixel to its right; lumps two pixels square on another, lit from the top left; ridges and lumps off the puddles and never touching another mark; cracks, none by default, on a third jittered grid, each kept at a chance with a length between the fewest and most pixels, each pixel a step from the last that turns an eighth of a turn left or right at a chance, in a crack colour with a lit lip on the pixel below and right of each crack pixel, also off the puddles and clear of other marks; the Dirt material is this recipe with no puddles or wet patches), [WeedsRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/WeedsRecipe.cs) (the grass recipe's textures repeated over its own repeat, or the grass colour with grit without one; `Recipe_Grass`, `Recipe_DryGrass`, `Recipe_Meadow` and `Recipe_TallGrass` are this kind: weed clumps over `Recipe_GrassGround`, dry stalks over `Recipe_DryGrassGround`, weeds and flowers over `Recipe_GrassGround` and tall blades over `Recipe_TallGrassGround`, grass recipes of hatched blades that write no material; weed spots on a jittered grid kept at a chance plus density waves, each a clump of blades with feet, heights, curves and seed heads, in stem, two leaf colours and tip over a shadow; flowers, none by default (their random draws are made only with a flower chance): at the flower chance plus the density waves times the flower drift a kept spot is a flower cluster instead, stems between the fewest and most, of a stem height, spread and curve, each topped by one flower head glyph ('o' petal, 'c' heart) in one petal and heart colour, picked by slow colour waves whose middle half is cut into even bands (or at the mix chance any colour), so clusters of one colour gather in drifts, a cluster's sway weight reaching its top at its lowest head so every head leans whole; every pixel of a weed's box, sway margin included, given its row's weight in the height's blue channel and its row from the shadow row under the base, plus one, in the green, boxes inside one cell column and a pixel apart; the cell field is the ground without the weeds, its alpha a box's height in rows above its base, 255 outside the boxes), [WaterHighlightsRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/WaterHighlightsRecipe.cs) (pond and lake water, and the ice made with it: the water, darker and lighter tones, cut from tone waves at the darker and lighter shares; for shallow water, none by default, a bed tone where bed waves are highest, at the bed share, under the strokes, and pebbles on another jittered grid, each kept at a chance, on plain water off the strokes and sparkles; the Shallows material is this recipe with both; stroke spots per world cell on a jittered grid, each kept at a chance plus density waves scaled by a density amount, with a length between the shortest and longest, a glint phase, and at a chance a companion half as long a row above, shifted a third of its length to one side and glinting a twentieth of a period after it; a stroke left out when it would touch another, bowed up a pixel in its middle from a length on (off by default), in the resting colour, its glint phases falling by the spread from its middle to its ends; sparkle spots off the strokes at a chance, each with a sparkle phase; a slant, 0 by default, that has each stroke climb that many rows per pixel, rounded (1 for the Ice material's diagonal glare); shines, none by default: shine spots on another jittered grid, each kept at a chance with a half-width between two values, a squat ellipse the shine squash times as tall in the edge colour round a core in the shine colour, the pixels within half of it from a point a quarter of the way towards the light whose four neighbours are in the shine, left out where it would lie on a stroke or beside one, on a sparkle or on or beside another shine, as the Ice material's are; the highlight and sparkle colours handed to the material as its glint and sparkle colours; with no strokes it writes only sparkle phases, as snow's), [OceanWavesRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/OceanWavesRecipe.cs) (the Ocean material's: dark water in three flat tones cut from a tone field at shares, as the water highlights recipe's; crests in families, each with its own phase offset, a sideways bend from smooth noise along x and a smooth mask, a pixel on the crests of the family whose mask is highest and above the crest level (below the crest level plus the fade, only at a chance that grows with the mask, so a crest's ends break up), its crest's middle pushed towards the shore by up to the arch as its mask rises, carrying a glint phase that grows by one period over the crest spacing up the texture (the texture's height over `crestsAcross`), so the band lit for the glint duty moves down the texture by the spacing each glint period, towards a shore to the south, as one field wherever the material lies; a few sparkles off the crests; the crests' and the sparkles' colours handed to the material), [LavaBubblesRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/LavaBubblesRecipe.cs) (the Lava material's: a surface in a red and a darker red where a slow tint noise is highest, at the darker share; hot patches where a patch noise roughened by a finer one is highest, at the hot share, with hotter cores at the core share, each connected patch (wrapping) in its own orange, its hue and brightness moved by up to the hue and value ranges; dark specks where a speck noise is highest, on the cooler half of the surface only; the noises are tileable value noise with no direction; bubbles per cell, each a disc of a radius between two values placed a pixel clear of the others, its pixels' glint phases falling behind by the swell delay per pixel from its middle, so it swells from a point and opens into a ring as its middle goes out first, the outermost pixel of a bubble of two pixels' radius or more a sparkle phase on the same timing, its dark rim; the bubble and rim colours handed to the material as its glint and sparkle colours, which needs the material's sparkle period and duty to match its glint's), [PlankBoardsRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/PlankBoardsRecipe.cs) (planks: rows of boards along x, boards per cell (a gap row under each), joists per cell (nail heads), butt joints on the cell borders at lengths of whole cells between the shortest and longest, each kept off the two rows below's where a redraw allows; a palette colour and tint per board; a bevel lighter along the top row and left end and darker along the bottom row and right end; grain streaks per board per cell, dark at a chance, else light; knots at a chance; a nail on the middle row over each joist at a chance; the cell field points each pixel straight across its board at its middle row in the same column, alpha its distance to the board's edge, the gap colour the base; the Planks material), [GrassTuftsRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/GrassTuftsRecipe.cs) (grass colour, tint waves and grit; tuft spots on a jittered grid, their chance plus density waves, blade and tip colours and the tuft glyphs, top row drawn highest; colour patches, none by default: slow waves that shift the ground, blades and tips together towards a low and a high colour by up to an amount, so the hue varies as well as the lightness, as the DryGrass material's straw does), [GravelChipsRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/GravelChipsRecipe.cs) (dirt colour, tint waves and grit; chip spots, chance and density waves, the light, grey and dark chip colours with the dark chance, colour jitter, radius and stretch ranges, chip grit, bevel and where it starts, the one-pixel shadow; overlapping chips give a pixel to the chip it lies deepest in), [StoneCobblesRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/StoneCobblesRecipe.cs) (mortar colour, width and grit; stones per cell and jitter, a seed offset in pixels (0 by default; with no jitter, half a pixel puts the cell edges on pixel centres for one-pixel mortar, as the Plaza material's square slabs have), the palette, bevel and its width, the rounded look, the shaded side; each pixel's nearest seed and exact distance to its stone's cell edge, found by searching the seed grid ring by ring around the pixel until no farther ring can hold a nearer seed or edge, so a pixel's cost does not grow with the number of stones: about 30 ms at 128 px, 110 ms at 256 px). A chip's or stone's centre pixel is its pixel nearest its seed. Shared maths in [TerrainArt](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainArt.cs): periodic noise, tileable value noise (`PeriodicValueNoise`, a lattice read with a cubic B-spline), the value below which a share of a field lies (`Level`), jittered seeds, the per-pixel hash, shading with halves rounded up, wrapping, light from the top left as in the shader, waves' total amplitude, the height texture's pixels (with optional glint and sparkle phases, `GlintByte` and `SparkleByte`), and cracks (`CrackSteps`, `CrackDirections` drawing a crack's length and turns, `CrackPixels` its groove and its lip below and right), the mud recipe's. Ported from `tools/terrain-art/materials.py`, which is gone: same parameters and look, other random draws. |
| [TerrainMaterialWriter](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainMaterialWriter.cs) | Static. `Generate(recipe)` writes the recipe's textures for its seed into the material's folder under `Assets/Textures/Terrain`, at `TargetPaths`: for each texture the PNG the material already uses there (so a renamed material keeps its files and GUIDs), else `<material name in lower case>_albedo.png`, `_height.png` and, with chips, stones or clumps, `_field.png` (`PathsIn`) in `FolderOf(material)`: the folder of the material's textures already there, so they stay together and a renamed material keeps its folder, else a new folder named after the material, which `EnsureFolder` creates (the tile writer uses it too). It refuses, with an error and without writing, a path that is another material's texture (`MaterialUsingTexture`, which searches every `TerrainMaterial` asset) or one file for two textures. `WriteFiles` encodes every PNG before touching a file and writes all of them or none: when one write fails, the files already written get their previous bytes back (new ones are deleted) and the error is rethrown. Then the files are reimported so `TerrainTextureDefaults` applies, and `Apply` points the material at them and writes the base colour, repeat and cell mode they were made for through its serialized data, without undo, and calls its `NotifyChanged`, so the renderers drawing it rebuild even when nothing on it changed; the material is saved. Last, `TerrainTileWriter.RefreshSwatch` rewrites the swatch of the material's ground tile when it has one. `EncodePng` gives the bytes; the same seed writes byte-identical files. `Generate(recipe, textures)` writes textures already generated for the recipe's seed (the inspector's preview) the same way. |
| [TerrainTileWriter](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainTileWriter.cs) | Static. `MaterialOf(tile)` is the ground material whose tile a tile is (`TilePath` read backwards), or null. `Write(material)` writes a ground material's swatch, `SwatchPixels` (the bottom-left 32 px cell of its albedo, decoded from the albedo PNG), to `SwatchPath` (`Assets/Sprites/Terrain/<material name in lower case>_swatch.png`, imported by `PixelArtTextureDefaults` and made a single sprite). Then it creates the material's plain `Tile` at `TilePath` (`Assets/Tiles/Ground/Tile_<material name>.asset`), or points the existing one at the swatch, without a collider. It refuses, with an error, a material outside `Assets/Settings/Terrain/Materials` (tiles and swatches are named after materials, whose names are unique only there), a material without a square albedo PNG of at least a cell, and a path holding anything but a plain `Tile` (a subclass included). `RefreshSwatch(material, textures)` rewrites the swatch of a material in that folder that has a plain tile and points the tile at it again (the material writer's Generate calls it). A swatch whose file already holds the same bytes is neither rewritten nor reimported. Menu `Tools/TopDownRPG/World/Terrain/Write Ground Tiles` writes the tile of every material in the folder. The tile is found by the material's name, so a renamed material gets a new tile. |
| [TerrainBrush](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainBrush.cs) | The Tile Palette's default brush, `[CustomGridBrush(true, true, true, "Terrain Brush")]`, which also hides Unity's default brush. A `DualGridBrush`: on a tilemap with a `DualGridTilemapModule` (the walls) it writes the module's data tile into every cell of a stroke (flood fill included), and on a plain tilemap (the ground layers) the palette's tile, as the grid brush does. What it adds is its editor's preview on ground layers and, on a chunk of a world, strokes across chunk borders: its `BoxFill`, `BoxErase` (so paint and erase, which the grid brush runs through them) and `FloodFill` hand a target under a `WorldChunk` to `WorldBrushStroke`, and leave any other target (a standalone area) to the Dual Grid Brush as before. The Dual Grid Brush stays in the brush list. |
| [TerrainBrushEditor](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainBrushEditor.cs) | Editor of `TerrainBrush`, a `DualGridBrushEditor`. A ground layer here is a target tilemap that the renderer `TerrainBlendRenderer.FindLayerDrawing` finds is drawing right now (`IsDrawing`). On one, `PaintPreview`, `ErasePreview` and `BoxFillPreview` replace the last preview with the stroke's cells, filled or empty, through the renderer's `SetPreview`, never touching the tilemap. On a ground layer of a chunk the preview shows on the renderer of each chunk it covers, the target's and its linked neighbours' (`ShowOnChunk`), on their layer of the same material, each given the cells within its own cells and skirt so the preview blends across the border; a chunk that lacks the layer shows none until the stroke adds it. A paint with the Paint tool or a box shows empty while shift is held, since the click then erases (`PaintPreview(…, erasing)`, `BoxPreview`). Pressing or releasing shift redoes a hovered paint or box preview, which Unity's refresh would not (`FollowShift`). Only the cells the click writes are shown: z = 0, and on a plain layer when filling only the cells whose brush cell holds a tile. A flood fill shows no preview there. The preview clears on commit (`RegisterUndo`), on stroke end (`FollowStroke`: the mouse released), pointer leave, tool deactivation, undo or redo, brush or paint target change (`GridPaintingState` events), scene closing, before an assembly reload, and when the editor is disabled. During `OnPaintSceneGUI` a clear waits for its end and is dropped when a new preview replaced the old one first, so a hover refresh uploads once and never shrinks a grown map in between. A clear that happens, or a preview that added chunks, has the Scene view repaint. A hover update allocates nothing. On every other tilemap, including the layers of a renderer that is off or has not built, it is the Dual Grid Brush's editor. In the Tile Palette (`OnPaintInspectorGUI`) it warns when a picked tile is a ground material's (`TerrainTileWriter.MaterialOf`) that the target layer is not drawn with (`PaletteProblem`): it names the area's layer for that material, or, when the area has none (`MissingMaterial`), says so and shows a button, "Add the <material> layer", which adds it (`AddMissingLayer`: `GroundLayerMigration.AddLayer`, one undo step) and makes it the paint target, since each area lists only the layers it uses and Unity's palette list cannot be filtered (TerrainLayers32.md's Phase 4). See TerrainBlending.md's Phase 3 notes and TerrainTypes.md's Phase 2 notes. |
| [TerrainMaterialRecipeEditor](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainMaterialRecipeEditor.cs) | Inspector of every recipe: its settings; previews of the albedo, the cell field (each chip or stone in its own hue, brighter where its alpha is larger, its centre pixel white, texels of no chip dark) and the height's red channel (glint pixels in a hue by their phase, sparkle pixels white), regenerated after every change and after an undo or redo that changed the recipe (its serialized settings are compared, so undos elsewhere cost nothing); a line with the size, base colour and cell mode; and Generate, disabled without a material, which runs `TerrainMaterialWriter.Generate` once the inspector is drawn and names the files it writes. Beside it Auto Generate (`AutoGenerate`, an editor preference, off by default; turning it on generates once): the textures are written `AutoGenerateDelay` (0.4 s) after the last change or undo, each change pushing it back, from the preview when it matches the current settings (else rebuilt first), so the ground in the open scenes follows the settings while they are tuned; one still waiting when the inspector closes is written right after. `ScheduleAutoGenerate`, `IsAutoGenerateScheduled` and `FollowAutoGenerate(now)` are internal hooks. |
| [TerrainSceneViewAnimation](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainSceneViewAnimation.cs) | `[InitializeOnLoad]`: outside play mode, while an enabled `TerrainBlendRenderer` draws an animated material, repaints every Scene view at twice the fastest one's frames per second (`FramesPerSecond(renderers)`, from their `AnimationFramesPerSecond`), whichever window has focus, so water keeps moving there; otherwise it looks twice a second. Menu `Tools/TopDownRPG/World/Terrain/Animate In Scene View` turns it off and on (an editor preference, on by default). Unity throttles editor updates while it is in the background, which slows it there. |
| [TilePaletteFollower](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TilePaletteFollower.cs) | `[InitializeOnLoad]`: when the Tile Palette's paint target changes (`GridPaintingState.scenePaintTargetChanged`), selects the palette holding a tile the target paints: a dual-grid layer's data tile, then the tile of the material a `TerrainBlendRenderer` draws the layer with (`TerrainTileWriter.TilePath`, so an empty layer finds its palette), then the tiles painted on it; with none it leaves the palette alone. `PaletteFor(target, palettes)` is the choice; it works with any set of layers an area lists. Tools/TopDownRPG/World/Terrain/Match Palette To Tilemap turns it off (an editor preference, on by default). |
| [TerrainTextureDefaults](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainTextureDefaults.cs) | `AssetPostprocessor` for `Assets/Textures/Terrain/` and its material folders (the files `TerrainMaterialWriter` writes): default texture type, sRGB off, point filtering, repeat wrapping, no mipmaps or NPOT scaling, uncompressed RGBA32, so `TerrainBlendRenderer` can copy the textures into its arrays as they are. |
| [TerrainBlendProbe](../Assets/Editor/World/Terrain/TerrainBlendProbe.cs) | Editor checks for a `TerrainBlendRenderer`, ported from the Phase 0 spike's probe. `Render` draws a world rectangle through a temporary camera (`RenderPipeline.SubmitRenderRequest`) at whole screen pixels per art pixel, with shaders compiled synchronously (`ShaderUtil.allowAsyncCompilation` off) and props, or every renderer in the open scenes but the chunks (other areas' chunks included, found with `Resources.FindObjectsOfTypeAll`), hidden through `Renderer.forceRenderingOff`; it clears the dirty mark that leaves on each prop, or the next save would write every prop's `YPositionSorter` order into the scene as an override. `Capture` writes a PNG; `CheckBlobs` renders the debug view over the control map, where its grid places it, at a fixed time, and lists per layer (named after its material) detached blobs under a size and holes, telling up to 32 layers apart by `DebugClass` (each channel read as the nearest quarter of the brightest one and looked up in `DebugColours`, the HLSL's `TB_DEBUG_COLOURS` in quarters; a colour that is no layer's counts as "unknown colour"), or says that nothing is built or that nothing was drawn yet (menu `Tools/TopDownRPG/World/Terrain/Check Blobs In Open Areas`, which checks every renderer in both looks); `MeasureShimmer` and `MeasureCost` repeat Phase 0's measurements; `MeasureRebuildAndModeSwitch` times a full rebuild and a switch of the look. `BuildStressScene(layers)` builds the scene those measurements share into the active scene, so every session measures the same ground ([TerrainLayers32.md](TerrainLayers32.md)'s Decisions), and returns its renderer: a white Global Light 2D at 0.85, as in the areas, and a grid at the origin whose renderer draws that many layers of `TerrainStyle.asset` in the crisp look, grass over 72 x 48 cells and above it the style's later materials in turn, starting over after the last, each painted as four discs of radius 2 to 5 cells inside the grass at spots drawn from a `System.Random` seeded with the layer's index, so a scene of more layers is one of fewer with more on top (16 layers reach the ocean; planks, a bridge, fall at layer 17); the caller opens an empty scene first and saves nothing. `InMode(renderer, mode, check)` runs any of them in a given look and puts the renderer's mode back afterwards, through the `Mode` property, so checks leave no scene diff. Chunks created or rebuilt in the current editor update are not drawn until the next one, so render in a later call after (re)building. |
| [NavigationGridEditModeRefresh](../Assets/Editor/Navigation/NavigationGridEditModeRefresh.cs) | `[InitializeOnLoad]`: outside Play Mode, forwards tile changes to every built `NavigationGrid2D` (`HandleTilemapPositionsChanged`), so painting updates the walkable-cell gizmo and `MovementBlockerGizmo` cell by cell (a raised cell on a chunk's border reaches the page across it when both are in one map). The grid also rebuilds before its next gizmo draw after an inspector change to it or one of its terrain sources (`OnValidate` -> `MarkGizmoGridStale`). |
| [MovementBlockerGizmo](../Assets/Editor/Navigation/MovementBlockerGizmo.cs) | Static `[DrawGizmo]` drawer for every enabled `NavigationGrid2D`, selected or not, in edit and play mode (the Scene view, and the Game view with gizmos on): what keeps players off each cell of the grid's walkable bounds (a chunk's cells on a chunk's grid, a page per chunk), under the movement profile of the project's `PlayerControlSettings` (the first found; mobs use the same profile today). Blocked cells are filled by `NavigationGrid2D.Blocker`: terrain the profile cannot walk (water, ocean, lava) magenta, walls red, blocking props orange, cells the cliff face of raised ground covers whole yellow, no ground grey; a white line runs along every side where a walkable cell meets a blocked one, the cell beyond the bounds read from the grid's navigation (nothing outside an area or in a chunk not loaded, the neighbouring chunk's page when it is, so no line runs along a border the ground crosses: in play mode through the world's navigation, outside it through `EditModeWorldNavigation`), and a yellow one along the cliff of raised ground (`GetCliffSides`), which blocks steps rather than cells. Cells outside the bounds are not drawn (nothing there is walkable). `BlockerMap` caches each grid's fill as runs of neighbouring cells along a row with the same blocker (`BlockedRun`) plus the edge and cliff lines (`LineList`s, `Edges` and `Cliffs`), rebuilt when the grid's `Version` or the profile (or its `Version`) changes: on the Coast 62 runs, 220 edge lines and 40 cliff lines, built in about 4 ms. Toggle: `Tools/TopDownRPG/Gizmos/Movement Blockers` (EditorPrefs, on by default). |
| [PropGridSnapping](../Assets/Editor/World/PropGridSnapping.cs) | Static rule for where a prop's root goes on a `GridLayout`. A blocking prop's footprint (the solid `BoxCollider2D` on its root, `TryGetFootprint`) is centred on the fewest whole cells that hold it (`CellsSpanned`, allowing for `NavigationGrid2D`'s edge inset), on the run nearest to where it is now, and stands on the bottom edge of the cell its bottom falls in; snapping a snapped position changes nothing. A walk-through prop rounds to the 1/32 pixel grid. `BlockedCells` gives the cells the navigation grid will block, with the same inset rule. `RootOnCells` places a footprint on the cells starting at a given bottom-left cell (what `SnapBlocking` does once it has picked them), and `CellsCovered` gives how many columns and rows a snapped footprint blocks; the scene generator uses both. |
| [PropPlacementWindow](../Assets/Editor/World/PropPlacementWindow.cs) | `Tools/TopDownRPG/Props/Prop Placement`: a palette of the prefabs in `Assets/Prefabs/Props/<category>` (none for now: only the two base prefabs remain) with sprite thumbnails, a search field and a category filter. While one is picked, the Scene view keeps its clicks: a translucent preview follows the pointer at the snapped position, the cells it would block are drawn green, or red when a collider on the grid's obstacle layers (another prop, a wall) is already there, and a left click places it under the active scene's `Props` root object (created when missing) as one undo step. A red spot refuses the click. Escape or Stop ends placing. It targets the scene holding a `NavigationGrid2D` (the active scene first), and has that grid's gizmos rebuild after each change. `Tools/TopDownRPG/Props/Snap Selected Props To Grid`, also a button in the window, snaps the selected prop instances (or the props a selected child belongs to) with undo. |
| [Generation/](../Assets/Editor/World/Generation) | Scene generation: `AreaGenerator` (abstract `ScriptableObject` with a seed, its terrain layers (only those its areas use, a subsequence of the style's layer order), `Generate(seed)`, the `PropScatter` base of scatter layers and the `WeedPatches` settings of `AreaLayoutBuilder.LayWeeds`, the weed patches the Clearing, the Village Forest, the Lakeside and the Coast lay last on their plain grass, along the band of trees and in the open; the project's generator assets set no props for now, so their scatter layers, campfires, seats and village furniture are empty and they lay no props, campsites, buildings or square), `ClearingGenerator` (with a campsite: a campfire and seats on a patch of dirt), `VillageForestGenerator` (a village, a clearing and a forest in one area, with a plaza square, dirt along the trails near the village, tall grass across the forest's edge and flower meadows in the clearing; `GenerateWithRegions` also returns each grass cell's region), `LakesideGenerator` (most terrain types around a lake: water, a beach, a marsh, mud and dry grass patches, small shallow ponds, a road, a trail to the beach, a dock from its end into the lake, tall grass along the trees and the marsh and flower meadows), `SnowfieldGenerator` (a field of snow, its ground, ringed by pines: a frozen lake of ice with a hole of open water, a road, a trail that stops short of the ice and a campsite on trodden dirt), `CoastGenerator` (a beach along the northern edge giving onto the ocean out to the map's edge, the ocean meeting only the beach, a road, a trail onto the beach, a campsite on trodden dirt and a plateau of raised grass with a ramp), `LavaFieldsGenerator` (scorched earth, its ground, crossed by a lava river with pools beside it that keep a cell of earth from the paths, a road crossing the river on earth, an unbroken trail that stops short of the lava and a campsite), `AreaLayoutBuilder` (internal: the state and steps the generators share, from the ground's outline (`LayGround`, `Ground`, `OrderedGround`: the base layer every other one lies on, grass, the Snowfield's snow or the Lava Fields' dirt) through painting and reservations to scatter, prop removal, mobs and reachability (around a layer that cannot be walked too), each ground cell's steps from a layer (`StepsFrom`, `StepsAt`), the path cells, where a trail leaves the road at no step (`TrailStart`), wobbly patches (`PaintPatch`, `PaintPatches` with a clearance around each centre, `RemoveFragments`, `CellsNear`, `AllWithin`) and a campsite (`LayCampsite` with a `Campsite`: a campfire and seats on a patch of dirt, used by the Clearing, the Snowfield, the Coast and the Lava Fields), given the generator's ground, gravel and stone layers and any other path layers (the plaza) rather than fixed indices, with a second random stream seeded from the layout's seed, `StartDetailStream`, for terrain laid after the props and mobs so it moves none of them), `AreaLayout` (the result in cells, carrying the generator's layers, addressed by index, by layer or by material), `CellComparer` (the comparer of every set and dictionary of cells in generation and its tests: `Vector3Int`'s equality with a hash that spreads small coordinates), `AreaLayoutWriter` (writes a layout into a scene with undo, its terrain layers as plain data tilemaps drawn by the area's `TerrainBlendRenderer`), `GroundLayerMigration` (sets an area's ground layers up as a generator's without repainting them, as one undo step: the layers it lacks created empty, plain tiles in the same cells and no record of old tiles, no dual-grid parts or tilemaps under the layers, any other layer the renderer drew kept with a warning at its place in the style's layer order when painted or listed by `AreaTerrain` and removed when empty, the renderer and the navigation priorities from that order, `AreaTerrain`'s fill tiles; an area with none of the layers is refused, and a second run changes nothing; it and the writer refuse to run in play mode; `AddLayer(grid, material)` adds one material's layer, its tilemap `TilemapName` (the material's name and Tilemap), its tile and its terrain type `TerrainTypePath` (`Assets/Settings/AI/Terrain_<material>.asset`), and `AddLayer(grid, layer)` a generator's layer, at its material's place in the style's order through `AreaGroundSetup.InsertLayer`, keeping every layer the renderer draws, empty ones included, as one undo step (or, with `ownUndoStep` false, in the current undo group, so a brush stroke adding layers to several chunks stays one step), refused with an error during play, without a renderer and style, for a material the order does not list or the renderer draws, and past `TerrainControlMap.MaxLayers`), `AreaGroundSetup` (internal: the set-up steps the writer and the migration share: `Problem`, the check that a generator's layers can be set up, a subsequence of the style's layer order among them, and `SetUpLayers`, which merges the generator's layers with the ones kept, removes the empty ones and sets the children's order, the navigation priorities and the renderer, recorded in an order an undo can pass through) and `SceneGeneratorWindow` (`Tools/TopDownRPG/World/Scene Generator`: the generator's inspector, a live map of the layout in the materials' base colours, Random Seed, and Generate and Convert Ground, both disabled in play mode and, with the reason shown, while `Problem` finds one or the active scene's grid is a world chunk's; New scratch scene, `WorldAuthoring.CreateScratchScene`). See [SceneGeneration.md](SceneGeneration.md). Raised ground: `AreaGenerator`'s raised and ramp tiles (`RaisedTile`, `RampTile`; none for a generator that lays none), `AreaLayout.Raised` and `Ramps`, `AreaLayoutBuilder.LayPlateau` with a `Plateau` (half size, ramp width, face rows: the rows its cliff face reaches into, whose props it clears, the Coast's from its style's cliff height; a mesa of blocks three cells thick everywhere, its ramp in a straight stretch of its bottom with cliff on either side; the Coast's), and the writer putting them on the grid's `RaisedTilemap` and Convert Ground keeping it, both through `AreaGroundSetup.SetUpRaised` (the tilemap created when missing and set on the terrain renderer and the navigation grid). World generation ([WorldGeneration.md](WorldGeneration.md), Phases 1 to 3: composing in memory, writing into chunk scenes and the World Generator window): `WorldGenerator` (`ScriptableObject`, create menu `TopDownRPG/World/World Generator`, editor-only data: the `WorldDefinition` it composes, a world seed, the terrain style, the movement profile, the world's own materials (`GroundMaterials`), the sea (depth, wobble, wavelengths, beach widths, corner radius, the ease beside a sea region), the `RegionPlacement`s (key, generator, seed, cell) and gaps, the plaza's radius, the `RoadLink`s (from a port to a port, to the road leaving a port, or to the nearest road; stone or gravel; width) and the roads' lead-in, spline spacing, margin, avoidance and wander, the gates' distance, the wilds' `Patches` and mobs per chunk, a `ChunkRecord` per chunk (written hash, locked, allowed wide, wilds seed) and the names of the entries the writer last set (`WrittenEntries`); `Compose()`; internal `Create` and `Set*`, `SetWrittenHash` among them, for tests and tools), `WorldComposition` (the result in world-local cells: a bit per material of the style's layer order, the owner (a region, the wilds or the sea) and the raised ground of every cell; the regions with their outlines, ports (`PortSide`) and gates, the roads, the mobs with derived ids, the entries, the waystones first, the terrain style, the base ground materials (`Grounds`: the wilds' grass and each region's ground), each chunk's material list with its neighbours' 4-cell strips and content hash, the warnings, those among them that stop a write (`BlockingWarnings`: layer lists past 16, reachability) and each step's time), `WorldComposer` (internal: the steps in order on dense arrays: regions moved cell for cell, the land with its rounded, wobbling coast and beach held at a sea region's own rows, the wilds' grass, the roads as A* routes pulled straight, wandering, through a spline, the waystones' plaza discs and the gates, the wilds' patches and mobs per chunk on its own stream, chunk lists and hashes, and the checks: placement from one pass outwards from every outline, layer lists past 16, reachability by the movement profile and `RaisedGround.CanCross`, ids and links) `GeneratedIds` (`For(worldId, scope, kind, ordinal)`: the first 32 hex digits of a SHA-256, the format of `PersistentIds.NewId`), `ChunkContent` (internal: what a chunk holds in its written hash's terms, a bit per material for each of its cells, its raised ground and the spawn points under its generated root, read from a composition, `Of`, or from a chunk's grid, `Read`; `Hash`, the one hash the composition's content hashes and the writer's records share, which ignores empty layers, the walls and every other object) and `WorldGenerationWriter` (`WriteChunk(grid, composition, coords)` writes one open chunk, its grid inactive meanwhile and its scene active: its ground layers become its list's materials plus those its `AreaTerrain` lists, in the style's order, set up through `AreaGroundSetup.SetUpLayers`, each cleared and filled in one batch in exactly the composed cells, a layer neither listed nor kept removed, its raised tilemap written or removed, its base ground layers appended to its `AreaTerrain`, which is only appended to, and the spawn points under its grid's `GeneratedMobSpawns` made its mobs, reused in order, each with its prefab and derived `PersistentId`, every object outside that root kept; `Write(generator, composition, chunks, force, despiteWarnings)` creates the missing chunks (`WorldAuthoring.CreateChunk`), never opens a locked chunk, skips one whose composed hash equals its record without opening it, refuses one whose scene no longer hashes to its record (an empty chunk's hash when it has none) unless forced, and opens, writes, saves, records and closes the others one at a time, then sets the generated entries first in the world's list and the hand-made ones after, returning a `ChunkWrite` per chunk (outcome, time, line); it refuses play mode, a composition with blocking warnings unless asked, a world that is no asset and creating chunks over an untitled scene with unsaved changes; `ContentHash(grid, coords, layerOrder)` reads a chunk scene's hash; `RunWorldSteps(world)` runs Sync layer lists, Bake changed coasts, Repair ids and Validate after a write), `WorldGeneratorSession` (internal: what the World Generator window works on, kept out of its GUI so tests and tools drive every step with no dialog: one generator's composition, composed again `RecomposeDelay` (0.4 s) after the last change (`NoteChange`, `FollowRecompose`) to what it is made from (`SettingsKey`: the generator's serialized settings without its chunk records and written entries, the records' wide allowances and wilds seeds, the world's id, size and chunks, the region generators' settings), so a lock, a write's hashes and an undo elsewhere recompose nothing; each chunk's `ChunkState` (unchanged, changed, locked, missing) from its record and composed hash alone, never opening a scene; `Plan(scope, selection)`, a `WritePlan` per button (`Scope`: the changed and missing chunks, the selected ones forced, or every chunk forced, never a locked one): the chunks it covers (`Targets`, handed to the writer so each gets its line) and writes, those it creates, the warnings, its `Question`, and the problems that stop it whatever the answer (play mode, no world, a failed composition, a world that is no asset, the world's chunk scenes open with unsaved changes, chunks to create over an unsaved untitled scene); `Write(plan, confirm)`, which asks through the callback it is given (an `Answer`: cancel, write, write anyway, the last needed past `BlockingWarnings`), writes through `WorldGenerationWriter.Write`, runs `RunWorldSteps` when any chunk was written and returns the report, a line per chunk and per step with its time; `SetLocked` and `SetAllowWide` on the records through the generator's serialized object, a record made in row order where missing, one undo step each), `WorldGeneratorPreview` (internal, static: `Pixels(composition, generator)` and `Draw` into a point-filtered texture, one pixel per cell: the top material's base colour, a shade darker for each earlier material of that colour, the world's weeds and meadow tinted; raised ground darker, ramps lighter; chunk borders darker; locked chunks hatched; outlines lighter, ports magenta (orange unlinked); mobs red (the wilds' pink); waystones cyan and gates yellow crosses; each chunk's layer count by its top-left corner, `CountOrigin`, black, red past 16 and blue when allowed wide) and `WorldGeneratorWindow` (`Tools/TopDownRPG/World/World Generator`, a thin window over the session: the asset picker, Recompose and the world; the preview zoomed to fit or by whole pixels a cell, the selected chunks framed, the cell under the pointer described and a click selecting its chunk; the warnings, those that stop a write first; the chunk grid, north up, each button tinted by its state with its coordinates, layer count (red past 16 unless allowed wide), L, + and W; Locked and Allowed wide for the selection, mixed when they differ; Write changed chunks, Write selected chunks and Write every chunk, each run after the GUI pass and asking with `EditorUtility.DisplayDialog`, Write anyway past warnings that stop it; the last write's report; the generator's inspector. Its only standing work is following changes to the generator, its world and its region generators (inspector edits, undo, `ObjectChangeEvents`, gaining focus) and one editor update hook while a recompose waits; disabled in play mode). |
| [Walls/](../Assets/Editor/World/Walls) | The walls' 32 px sheet, generated like the terrain materials: [WallSheetRecipe](../Assets/Editor/World/Walls/WallSheetRecipe.cs) (editor-only ScriptableObject: the sheet texture, the walls rule tile, a seed, the piece size, outer and inner corner radii, outline width, face height, block size and colours; `Generate(seed)` returns the sheet's pixels without touching an asset, each piece's quadrants read from the rule tile's tiling rules by `PieceMasks`, the shape from the exact signed distance to them like the shader's `CornerShapeDistance`; `CanGenerate` tells whether the settings can make a sheet), [WallSheetWriter](../Assets/Editor/World/Walls/WallSheetWriter.cs) (`Generate(recipe)` logs and refuses settings or rules that cannot make a sheet, then writes the PNG over the recipe's sheet when its bytes changed, sets the import settings and slices it into `walls_0` to `walls_15` at `PieceRect` through the sprite editor's data provider, so existing slices keep their sprite IDs and the rule tile and palette references survive; an unchanged sheet is not reimported) and [WallSheetRecipeEditor](../Assets/Editor/World/Walls/WallSheetRecipeEditor.cs) (its inspector: the settings, a preview of the sheet regenerated on every change and undo and when the rule tile's rules change, and Generate). See [DualGrid.md](DualGrid.md#walls) and TerrainBlending.md's Phase 4 notes. |
| [CollisionTileGizmo](../Assets/Editor/World/CollisionTileGizmo.cs) | Static `[DrawGizmo]` drawer for every enabled `TilemapCollider2D`, selected or not, in edit and play mode (the Scene view, and the Game view with gizmos on): a translucent red fill and an outline on each cell whose tile has a collider (`CollectCollisionCells`: the filled cells of the tilemap's bounds whose collider type is not `None`), drawn whole even for a `Sprite` collider. Shows the walls' `CollisionTilemap`, which has no renderer and sits on the culled `Obstacles` layer. Reuses its buffers across repaints. Toggle: `Tools/TopDownRPG/Gizmos/Collision Tiles` (EditorPrefs, on by default). |
| [WorldAuthoring](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/WorldAuthoring.cs) | Static, the steps the World Editor runs. `NameProblem(name)`: letters and digits, starting with a letter, and no folder yet. `CreateWorld(name, origin, sizeInChunks, parentFolder)`: `Assets/Worlds/<name>/` (or another parent folder: the stress world's is `Assets/Dev/Worlds`) with the root scene `World_<name>.unity` (a `WorldLifetimeScope`, and `Lighting` with `WorldLighting` and a white Global Light 2D at 0.85), its `SceneDefinition` beside it, and the `WorldDefinition` `<name>.asset`. `CreateChunk(world, coords)`: `Chunks/World_<name>_<x>_<y>.unity` with a `ChunkLifetimeScope` and a `Grid` at the world's origin whose `TerrainBlendRenderer` (the project's style) draws an empty grass layer, its `SceneDefinition`, and its entry in the world. Every scene goes into the build list, but a dev world's (under `SceneBuildValidator.DevFolder`). The scenes are made beside the open ones, or in their place when an untitled scene is open. `CreateChunk` is also how the world generation writer makes a chunk the world lacks, and it also puts a `WorldChunk` on the grid and sets up its navigation (`SetUpNavigation(grid)`: a `NavigationGrid2D` reading the ground layers under the grid, blocked by the walls and the `Obstacles` layer, reading the raised tilemap the renderer draws; `AddWalls` adds the walls to it). `Paint(world, material, cells)` paints world-local cells into the open chunk scenes that own them, adding the material's layer where missing (`GroundLayerMigration.AddLayer`) and to each open neighbour whose skirt the cells reach; `PaintWalls(world, cells, filled)` through each chunk's wall module, adding the walls (`AddWalls`: an unlinked copy of `Walls.prefab`, `WallsPrefabPath`, an empty `CollisionTilemap`) where missing; `PaintRaised(world, cells, tile)` into each chunk's raised tilemap (`AreaGroundSetup.SetUpRaised` where missing). `AddRaised(grid)` sets up a chunk's raised tilemap on its own, as one undo step. Whole-world steps, opening the chunk scenes that are not open and saving the ones they change: `MarkChunks(world)` gives every chunk its `WorldChunk` (how the Sandbox's got theirs), `SetUpNavigation(world)` sets up every chunk's navigation (how both test worlds' chunks got theirs), `SyncLayerLists(world)` adds to each chunk a layer for every material within its skirt it lacks, in the style's order, and `BakeCoastMaps(world)` bakes each chunk's coast map (`WorldCoastBake`) into a 16-bit PNG beside its scene (`CoastMapPath`: `World_<name>_<x>_<y>_Coast.png`, imported linear, uncompressed `R16`, bilinear, clamped, unreadable), sets it on the chunk's `WorldChunk`, and removes the map of a chunk that shows no waves, returning a line per chunk with its time; with `onlyChanged` it bakes only the chunks whose `WorldChunk.CoastSource` differs from what they would be baked from now (`IsCoastStale`), and `StaleCoastMaps(world)` lists them without baking (opening the chunk scenes and closing them unchanged). Opening and closing: `OpenChunk(world, coords, withNeighbours)` opens a chunk's scene and its eight neighbours' beside the open scenes (closing an untitled scene without changes; opening beside the others keeps loaded assets alive) and makes it active, `OpenRoot`, `OpenChunkCoords`, `OpenScenesExcept` and `CloseChunks(world, keep, root)` (the caller asks to save first; the last scene open stays), `OpenChunkScenes`/`CloseScenes` for whole-world steps. `ListInGameScenes(world)`. World exits and entries: `AddExit(world, cell, target, entryName, size)` places a `WorldExit` with a new `PersistentId` on a world cell of the open chunk owning it, under that scene's `Exits` root (`ExitsRootName`, made when missing), as one undo step; `ConfigureExit` sets where an exit leads and its size through its serialized object; `OpenExits(world)` lists the exits of the open chunks; `SetEntry(world, name, position, waystone)` replaces or appends an entry and saves the world. `CreateScratchScene()`: after offering to save the open scenes, a new unsaved scene with a Global Light 2D and a standalone grid at the origin set up as a chunk's (an empty grass layer drawn by the terrain renderer, its navigation grid, its `AreaTerrain`, the walls) without a `WorldChunk`, for the Scene Generator. `OpenChunkGrid`, `MaterialNamed`, `ChunkSceneName`. |
| [WorldCoastBake](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/WorldCoastBake.cs) | Static: a chunk's coast map from the whole world's ground. `WorldCells` holds a bit per material per cell of the world (`Add(tilemap, material, cells)`, `BitsAt`, `MaterialsIn`, `Covered`: the filled cells and the control map's margin, as one renderer over the world would cover them). `BakeChunk(world, style, chunkMaterials, chunkCells, out width, out height)` takes the first material with waves in the style's order among the chunk's layers and the ground within its skirt (null when none), flags each cell of a window `Reach` (40) cells around the chunk, clipped to `Covered`, as sea when it is empty or holds that material or a non-bridge one above it, builds a `TerrainCoastMap` over the window and crops it to the chunk's control map (clamping past the window's edge): the texels a renderer over the whole world gives those cells, exactly. `SourceOf` hashes what a chunk's map is baked from (the material with waves, the window and which of its cells are sea), or `NoWaves`, so equal sources bake equal maps; a change of ground that is no sea, under the waves' place in the order or past the reach leaves it as it was. `ToTexture` makes the R16 texture. About 220 ms per chunk of a 2 x 2 world in the editor (20 µs per cell of the window). |
| [WorldEditorWindow](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/WorldEditorWindow.cs) | `Tools/TopDownRPG/World/World Editor`. A picker of every world in the project; New world: a name (`NameProblem`), a size in chunks and the first free origin (`WorldLayout.TryFindFreeOrigin`, or one typed in, checked against the layout as it is typed), creating every chunk and listing it in `GameScenes` when asked; the picked world's origin and size with any layout problem, and a button listing it in `GameScenes` when it is not; a grid of its chunks, north up, each closed, open or missing, the selected one created, opened with its neighbours or alone, or kept while the others close (asking to save changed scenes first), and, while open, given walls (`AddWalls`) or raised ground (`AddRaised`) when it lacks them, the root scene opened and every chunk closed; the world's entries (name, position, waystone; the first is the default) edited in place, with a warning for one on no chunk; the exits of the open chunks, each with its target world, a popup of that world's entries (or its default entry), its size and a Select button, and Add exit, which places one in the selected open chunk at the Scene view's pivot when it lies on that chunk (its middle otherwise) and selects it; and the whole-world steps: Sync layer lists, Check coasts, Bake changed coasts, Bake every coast, Repair ids and Validate, each reporting its lines under the window. Its only standing work is a flag set when a tile of an open chunk of the picked world changes (`Tilemap.tilemapTileChanged`), which shows a hint to check the coasts. |
| [WorldBrushStroke](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/WorldBrushStroke.cs) | Static, the Terrain Brush's strokes on a chunk. `TryResolve(target)` gives a tilemap under a `WorldChunk` naming a world its `Target`: the chunk and a `Kind`, `Ground` (a layer the chunk's renderer draws, with its material), `Raised` (the renderer's raised tilemap), `Walls` (any other dual-grid tilemap) or `Other`. `Apply(target, changes)` splits the stroke's `TileChangeData` by the chunk owning each cell (chunks are found among the open scenes, `OpenChunks`) and writes each part to that chunk's tilemap of the same kind: the layer drawing the material, added where missing (`GroundLayerMigration.AddLayer` in the current undo group) and added also to each open neighbour whose skirt the painted cells reach; the walls of the same rule tile (`WallsLike`), an empty copy of the target's walls where missing, joined to the chunk's navigation; the raised tilemap (`AreaGroundSetup.SetUpRaised` where missing). Each part records its tilemaps, and for walls the open neighbours' render tilemaps, in the current undo group, so the stroke is one undo step across scenes. A part of a chunk not open or outside the world, or of another chunk for an `Other` target, is skipped and named in one warning; returns how many cells it skipped. `FloodFill(target, start, fill)` fills the 4-connected cells of the kind holding the start's tile across the open chunks of the world (a chunk lacking the tilemap counts as empty), warning when it stopped at the border of a chunk that is not open, and applies them as one stroke. `LayerOf`, `WallsLike`. |
| [WorldLayout](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/WorldLayout.cs) | Static, the global layout (WorldStreaming.md's World origins): `RectOf` (a world's rectangle in units), `Gap` (the distance between two rectangles), `Problem(origin, size, others, self)` (a rectangle leaving `Extent`, 16 384 units around the origin, or within `MinimumGap`, 1024 units, of another world's, naming it), `Validate(worlds)` (every world leaving the layout and every pair too close), `TryFindFreeOrigin(size, worlds)` (the first slot of a grid `SlotSize`, 2048 units, apart, row by row from the origin's, where the world fits) and `AllWorlds()`. |
| [WorldValidation](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/WorldValidation.cs) | Static, the ownership checks, a line per problem naming the object (scene and hierarchy path). `ValidateChunk(chunk)`: every `SpawnPoint`, `MobSpawnPoint`, object with a `PersistentId` and walk-through prop (a child of the scene's `Props` root) on one of the chunk's cells; every blocking prop's footprint (`PropGridSnapping.TryGetFootprint`) blocking only its cells (`FootprintInside`, with `PropGridSnapping.BlockedCells`, the navigation's rule); every mob spawn point with a `PersistentId`, and every id set; every `WorldExit` covering only the chunk's cells and leading to a world the listed worlds hold (`GameScenes`', or a list a test passes) at an entry it has (or its default entry for an empty name). `ValidateIds(ids)`: a line per shared id naming its holders. `ValidateWorld(world, allWorlds)`: the origin against the layout, each chunk scene's `WorldChunk` naming the world and its coordinates, `ValidateChunk` for each, and ids unique across the world, opening the chunk scenes that are not open and closing them unchanged. `ValidateAll()` for every world. |
| [WorldBuildValidator](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/WorldBuildValidator.cs) | Pre-build check (`IPreprocessBuildWithReport`, after `SceneBuildValidator`) failing a build with any `WorldValidation.ValidateAll` problem, listed; also `Tools/TopDownRPG/World/Validate Worlds`, which logs each problem as an error. About 0.2 s for the three worlds. |
| [PersistentIds](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/PersistentIds.cs) | Static: `NewId` (a GUID without dashes), `SetId` (through the serialized object, with undo), `AddToSpawnPoints(roots)` (a `PersistentId` on every `MobSpawnPoint` lacking one), `MakeUnique(ids)` (an id for each without one and for each later holder of a shared one, the first keeping it), `RepairNew(fresh, existing)` (a new id for each fresh object whose id is missing or held by another, so a duplicate changes and its original keeps its own), `Duplicates`, `IsChunkScene`, `InWorld`, `Repair(world)` (the World Editor's Repair ids over every chunk scene, saving the ones it changed; it keeps the derived ids the world generation writer sets, since they are set and unique), `PathOf`. |
| [PersistentIdWatcher](../Assets/Editor/World/Worlds/PersistentIdWatcher.cs) | `[InitializeOnLoad]`: outside play mode, on `ObjectChangeEvents` for a created hierarchy (a new object, a duplicate, a paste) or a changed object structure in a chunk scene, gives its mob spawn points their `PersistentId` and repairs its ids against every loaded one (`RepairNew`), logging each new id. Changes made by scripts without undo raise no event; Repair ids covers them. The world generation writer sets each generated spawn point's derived id as it makes it, so the watcher, should it see one, keeps it. |
| [EditModeWorldNavigation](../Assets/Editor/Navigation/EditModeWorldNavigation.cs) | `[InitializeOnLoad]`: outside Play Mode, joins the `NavigationGrid2D` pages of the open chunks of each world in a `WorldNavigation` of its own (one pool, up to `NavigationPagePool.DefaultSlots` chunks), as the world's navigation joins them in play, so the movement blocker gizmo draws no line along a border the ground crosses and a cliff across a border only where the game has one. A chunk takes part when its `WorldChunk` and grid are enabled in the scene its world lists for those coordinates (chunks built in memory by tests stand alone). `Sync` runs once per editor update at most after a scene opens or the hierarchy changes; a closing scene's pages leave first, and every page leaves before Play Mode and an assembly reload (`LeaveAll`). `IsJoined(grid)`, and `NavigationOf(world)`, the joined navigation of a world's open chunks (`OverworldTests` walks it). |
| [AreaTerrainDevMenu](../Assets/Editor/World/AreaTerrainDevMenu.cs) | `Tools/TopDownRPG/Dev/Toggle Terrain Cell At View Centre`: in play mode, toggles the cell at the Scene view's pivot (or the main camera's position when no Scene view is open) on the selected tilemap, at z = 0 (`CellAt`), through its area's `AreaTerrain` (`Toggle`), so a host's edit is recorded and replicated and a client is refused. For checking runtime terrain edits by hand (see [Multiplayer.md](Multiplayer.md#checking-runtime-terrain-edits)). |
| [DualGrid/](../Assets/Editor/DualGrid) | Dual-grid tooling: inspectors for the module, rule tile and data tile, the Dual Grid Brush (listed beside the default Terrain Brush, which derives from it), the rule tile preview, automatic tiling rules, `Assets/Create/2D/Tiles/Dual Grid Rule Tile` and `GameObject/2D Object/Tilemap/Dual Grid Tilemap`, and `DualGridTilemapPersistentListener` (edit-mode render refresh). `RestrictedTransformEditor`/`RestrictedTilemapEditor` replace every `Transform` and `Tilemap` inspector to lock render tilemaps. See [DualGrid.md](DualGrid.md). |

the grass's recipe until Phase 12), `Recipe_DryGrass.asset` (`WeedsRecipe`, 20261021, 8 cells, dry stalks over `Recipe_DryGrassGround.asset`, a `GrassTuftsRecipe` that writes no material), `Recipe_Meadow.asset` (`WeedsRecipe`, 20261024, 8 cells, flowers in four colours over `Recipe_GrassGround.asset`, every kept spot a flower), `Recipe_TallGrass.asset` (`WeedsRecipe`, 20261025, 8 cells, tall blades over `Recipe_TallGrassGround.asset`, a `GrassTuftsRecipe` of dense tall blades, seed 20261026, that writes no material), `Recipe_Dirt.asset` (`MudPuddlesRecipe`, 20261019), `Recipe_Plaza.asset` (`StoneCobblesRecipe`, 20261020), `Recipe_Gravel.asset` (20261013), `Recipe_Stone.asset` (20261014), `Recipe_Sand.asset` (20261016, repeating over 16 cells), `Recipe_Mud.asset` (20261017, repeating over 16 cells), `Recipe_Water.asset` (20261018, `WaterHighlightsRecipe`, repeating over 8 cells), `Recipe_Shallows.asset` (20261022, `WaterHighlightsRecipe` with a bed and pebbles and fewer strokes, 8 cells), `Recipe_Planks.asset` (`PlankBoardsRecipe`, 20261023, 8 cells), `Recipe_Snow.asset` (`SandRipplesRecipe`, 20261027, 16 cells: smooth snow, sparse curved wind streaks, low bumps and sparkles), `Recipe_Ice.asset` (`WaterHighlightsRecipe`, 20261028, 16 cells: pale sheets, diagonal glare, sparkles and shiny spots), `Recipe_Ocean.asset` (`OceanWavesRecipe`, 20261029, 16 cells: dark blue water with wave crests travelling south), `Recipe_Lava.asset` (`LavaBubblesRecipe`, 20261030, 16 cells: red lava with orange patches each in its own tone, dark specks and bubbles) | `TerrainMaterialRecipeEditor`, `TerrainMaterialWriter` |

## Cross-system interactions

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    subgraph Main scope
        BootFlow --> GameFlow
        GameFlow --> SceneLoader
        GameFlow --> ScreenFader
        GameplayInputGate -- TransitionStarted/Finished --> GameFlow
        GameplayInputGate --> PlayerInputService
    end
    subgraph Gameplay scope
        PlayerController -- IPlayerCommandSource --> LocalPlayerCommandSource
        LocalPlayerCommandSource -- IPlayerInput --> PlayerInputService
        LocalPlayerCommandSource --> PointerTargetPicker
        PlayerController --> PlayerOrders -- IAbilityCaster --> PlayerAbilities --> AbilityService
        PlayerAbilities -- CastStarted --> PlayerNetworkSync -- CastRpc --> PlayerAbilities
        PlayerController --> PlayerMotor2D -- Of --> PlayerNavigation
        PlayerController --> PlayerWeaponController
        PlayerWeaponController --> SlashSpawner --> SwordSlashAttack
        SwordSlashAttack --> DamageService
        DamageService --> Health
        DamageService -- DamageApplied --> CombatEvents
        CombatEvents --> DamagePopupPresenter --> DamagePopupLayer
        Health -- Damaged/Died --> PlayerHudPresenter --> PlayerHudView
        PlayerWeaponController -- EquippedWeaponChanged --> PlayerHudPresenter
        Health -- Died --> PlayerDeathHandler -- TrySendToWaystone / TryRestore --> PlayerRespawner
        PlayerRespawner -- Send --> NetworkTripAuthority -- Approved --> LocalTripTraveller
        WaystoneDiscovery -- Discover --> SessionSave
        WaystoneDiscovery -- Raise --> WaystoneNotices --> WaystonePopupPresenter
        GameplayEntryPoint --> CameraFollow2D
    end
    subgraph World and chunk scopes
        ChunkMobSpawner -- resolver.Instantiate, SetHome --> MobController
        MobController -- INavigation2D --> WorldNavigation
        MobPerception2D -- IPlayerRegistry --> WorldPlayers
        MobController --> MeleeDamageDealer --> DamageService
        Health -- Died --> DestroyMobOnDeath --> EffectSpawner
    end
    MainMenuController -- StartWorldGameAsync / StartJoinedGameAsync --> GameFlow
    MainMenuController -- TryLoad, StartNew / StartFrom --> SessionSave
    MainMenuController -- TryGetStart --> PlayerStarts
    GameSaveRecorder -- WorldSessionEnding --> GameFlow
    GameSaveRecorder -- Write --> GameSave --> ISaveStore
    ChunkMobSpawner -- RecordMobDeath / StaysDead --> WorldState
    ChunkTerrainSync -- RestoreEdits / RecordTerrainEdit --> WorldState
    MainMenuController -- StartHost / JoinAsync --> NetworkSession
    NetworkSessionLifecycle -- ConnectionLost / TransitionFinished --> NetworkSession
```

Events summary:

| Event | Publisher | Subscribers |
|---|---|---|
| `GameFlow.TransitionStarted` / `TransitionFinished` | `GameFlow` | `GameplayInputGate`, `TransitionTimeFreeze` (offline only, and not for trips, `IsTravelling`), `NetworkSessionLifecycle` (finished only) |
| `GameFlow.WorldSessionEnding` | `GameFlow` (leaving a world session) | `GameSaveRecorder` |
| `Application.quitting` | Unity | `NetworkSession`, `GameSaveRecorder` |
| `ITripAuthority.Approved` | `NetworkTripAuthority` (offline or on the host once `LocalTripAuthority` checked a request, held the arrival and set the player's world, or once it sent a dead player to its waystone, `Send`; on a client when the host's trip arrives through `PlayerWorldSync`) | `LocalTripTraveller` (the local player's trip: `GameFlow.TravelAsync`) |
| `WaystoneNotices.Discovered` | `WaystoneDiscovery` (offline or the host, every player's), `PlayerWorldSync.WaystoneDiscoveredRpc` (a client, its own player's) | `WaystonePopupPresenter` (the local player's) |
| `NetworkSession.ConnectionLost` | `NetworkSession` | `NetworkSessionLifecycle` |
| `NetworkSession.ClientJoined` | `NetworkSession` (host) | `GameplayPlayers` |
| `PlayerWorlds.Changed` | `PlayerWorlds` | `WorldPlayers`, `PlayerWorldSync` (host), `WorldStreaming` (the local player's trips, for its chunk reports), `GameSaveRecorder` (a player entering a world) |
| `PlayerController.Teleported` | `PlayerController.Teleport` | `PlayerNetworkSync` (owner) |
| `MobMotor2D.AttackAnimationPlayed` | `MobMotor2D.PlayAttackAnimation` | `MobNetworkSync` (host) |
| `LocalPlayerTracker.Changed` | `PlayerBinder` | `GameplayEntryPoint`, `PlayerHudPresenter` |
| `PlayerWeaponController.Attacked` | `PlayerWeaponController.TryAttack` | `PlayerNetworkSync` (owner) |
| `Health.Damaged` | `Health` | `PlayerHudPresenter`, `NetworkHealth` (host) |
| `Health.Healed` | `Health.Heal` (via `DamageService`) | `NetworkHealth` (host) |
| `DamageReceiver.ImmuneHit` | `DamageService.ApplyDamage` (authority) | `NetworkHealth` (host) |
| `Health.Died` | `Health` | `PlayerHudPresenter`, `PlayerDeathHandler`, `DisableOnDeath`, `DestroyMobOnDeath`, `ChunkMobSpawner` (its mobs, offline or on the host) |
| `Health.Restored` | `Health.Restore` (via `PlayerRespawner`, or `NetworkHealth` on clients) | `PlayerHudPresenter`, `DisableOnDeath`, `NetworkHealth` (host) |
| `CombatEvents.DamageApplied` / `HealApplied` | `DamageService` | `DamagePopupPresenter` |
| `CombatEvents.StatusBlocked` | `StatusEffectService` (authority, or replicated on clients) | `DamagePopupPresenter` |
| `StatusEffects.Changed` | `StatusEffectSet` | `NetworkStatusEffects` (host), `StatusBarPresenter`, `StatusVisuals`, `WorldStatusIcons` |
| `StatusEffects.Blocked` | `StatusEffectService.Apply` (authority) | `NetworkStatusEffects` (host) |
| `PlayerRegistry.PlayerAdded` / `PlayerRemoved` | `PlayerRegistry` | `PlayerDeathHandler`, `PlayerIds` (added only: learning ids), `GameSaveRecorder` (removed only: a leaving player's place), `WaystoneDiscovery` (removed only) |
| `AreaTerrain.EditRecorded` | `AreaTerrain` (authority) | `NetworkAreaTerrain` (host), `ChunkTerrainSync` (offline or host, into the world state) |
| `PlayerWeaponController.EquippedWeaponChanged` | `PlayerWeaponController` | `PlayerHudPresenter`, `PlayerAbilities` |
| `PlayerAbilities.CastStarted` | `PlayerAbilities.TryCast` | `PlayerNetworkSync` (owner) |
| `PlayerAbilities.CastFailed` / `SlotsChanged` | `PlayerAbilities` (failures come from `PlayerOrders` too) | `AbilityBarPresenter` |
| `InputBindings.BindingsChanged` | `InputBindings` (a change, a reset, or a keyboard layout change) | `AbilityBarPresenter`, `OptionsMenuController` |
| `PlayerAbilities.CastEnded` | `PlayerAbilities.CompleteCast` / `CancelCast` (called by `PlayerOrders`) | none yet |
| `PlayerAbilities.CastCompleted` | `PlayerAbilities.CompleteCast` | `PlayerNetworkSync` (owner on a client) |
| `AbilityService.CastApplied` / `CastResolved` | `AbilityService.Apply` / `Resolve` (authority only) | tests |
| `MobController.onAttackRangeEntered` (UnityEvent) | `AttackRangeState` | inspector listeners (none on Weasel) |

## Tests

Tests build their own grids, tilemaps and GameObjects and destroy them in `TearDown`. When the editor is driven by a tool (UnityMCP), run them through [ProjectTestRun](../Assets/Editor/Testing/ProjectTestRun.cs) rather than UnityMCP's `run_tests`, so one call starts a run and one wait reads a short summary (see CLAUDE.md for when to run what):

- `ProjectTestRun.Start(mode, fixtures, categories, tests, slowest)`, from execute_code: `mode` is `EditMode` or `PlayMode`, and the other three are lists separated by semicolons, of test classes, categories and full test names, that narrow the run (all empty: the whole suite of that mode). It returns `started ...`, or `refused: ...` while the editor compiles, imports or plays, while a run is going, or while an open scene has unsaved changes, since it makes an empty scene active first (EditMode tests touch the active scene). It runs through the Test Framework's `TestRunnerApi`.
- When the run ends it writes `Temp/TestRunSummary.txt` (`SummaryPath`, written whole through a temporary file and deleted when a run starts): `PASSED` or `FAILED` with the filter, the counts and the run's length, the slowest test classes and tests with their times (`slowest` of each, 8 by default), and a `FAIL` line per failed test with the first line of its message; `ERROR` and the message when the run could not go. Its callbacks are registered on every domain load, so a PlayMode run writes it too.
- Waiting: a shell loop until the file exists, run in the background, which then prints it and `git status` of ProjectSettings and the scenes (a run can leave churn there).
- Checks that run for every cell or pixel (the area generation and terrain recipe tests) go through an `Expect(condition, message)` helper rather than `Assert.That`, which costs a few microseconds a call even when it passes, its message formatted and a constraint result built; `Expect` formats its message only on failure.
- Explicit tests run only when named or when their category is: the pathfinding benchmarks (`Benchmark`) and the terrain shader's compile test (`ShaderCompile`, about three and a quarter minutes, with a timeout of its own past the Test Framework's default of three minutes), run after a shader change and before merging one.
