Fakes and seams:
- [ManualClock](../Assets/Tests/Editor/ManualClock.cs): `IClock` with settable `Time` and `Advance`.
- [TestWorldGenerators](../Assets/Tests/Editor/TestWorldGenerators.cs): unsaved world generators and worlds (at (8192, 0)) with the project's style, profile, ground materials and Weasel, only read; the Overworld's Phase 0 settings (`Overworld`); chunk grids built in memory as `WorldAuthoring.CreateChunk` builds them (`NewChunk`); `Destroy` clears their undo and destroys what it made. `WorldGeneratorTests`, `WorldGenerationWriterTests`, `WorldGeneratorWindowTests` and `ChunkedGroundRenderTests` share it.
- [TestCombat](../Assets/Tests/Editor/TestCombat.cs): a shared default `CombatSettings`, `CreateDamageService(authority, events)`, `CreateStatusService(...)` (on a `ManualClock` by default) and `CreateHitService(authority, events)`.
- [TestTerrainStyle](../Assets/Tests/Editor/TestTerrainStyle.cs): a `TerrainStyle` built in memory with the project's terrain shader (only read) and given pair overrides, and `TerrainMaterial`s built in memory: plain grass, gravel (whole chips) and stone (cut stones) materials with fresh 128 px textures (albedo, a flat height and, with chips or stones, a cell field), or one of a given name and cell mode.
- [TestPropPrefabs](../Assets/Tests/Editor/TestPropPrefabs.cs): prop prefabs saved in a temporary folder (`Assets/Tests/TestPropPrefabs`, removed by `Delete`) as variants of `Prop` and `BlockingProp` without art, with footprints the sizes of the props the generators were built with (trees, bushes, rocks, plants, a campfire, stools, a building, a fountain, a fence, a lamp post, barrels and tables), and the lists of them `AreaGenerationTests` gives the generators' copies.
- [TestAreaGenerator](../Assets/Tests/Editor/TestAreaGenerator.cs): an `AreaGenerator` built in memory by `Create(style, layers)` (named TestGenerator), whose layouts fill cell (i, 0) of each layer i and nothing else, for writer cases the project's generators cannot give (such as 16 or 17 layers).
- [FakePlayerInput](../Assets/Tests/Editor/FakePlayerInput.cs): settable `IPlayerInput`. [TestPlayerControlSettings](../Assets/Tests/Editor/TestPlayerControlSettings.cs): builds a `PlayerControlSettings` (layers `Enemy`/`Player`) and assigns it to a `PlayerController`.
- [FakeInputBindingLabels](../Assets/Tests/Editor/FakeInputBindingLabels.cs): settable `IInputBindingLabels`. [FakeMenuInput](../Assets/Tests/Editor/FakeMenuInput.cs): settable `IMenuInput`. [FakeSessionStatus](../Assets/Tests/Editor/FakeSessionStatus.cs): settable `ISessionStatus`.
- `MemorySaveStore` ([EditMode](../Assets/Tests/Editor/MemorySaveStore.cs), [PlayMode](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/MemorySaveStore.cs)): in-memory `ISaveStore` with `Contents` and `WriteCount`. `GameSaveTests` builds its own `GameScenes` and `SceneDefinition`s with fake GUIDs.
- `SystemRandom` with a fixed seed stands in for `IRandom`.
- A real `PlayerRegistry` of `PlayerHandle`s over test transforms stands in for the session players.
- Mobs are driven manually: `MobController.Configure`/`Construct`, then `TickStateMachine(dt)` / `FixedTickStateMachine()`.
- `internal` hooks via `InternalsVisibleTo`: `DamageReceiver.SetProfile`, `StatusEffects.SetHold`, `StatusEffectService.Advance`/`TrackedUnitCount`, `DamagePopupLayer.Configure`/`ActiveCount`, `PlayerHudView.ConfigureReferences`, `AbilityBarView`/`AbilitySlotView.ConfigureReferences` and the slot's parts, `PointerFeedbackLayer.Configure` and its state, `WorldHealthBar.Configure`/`Refresh`/`FillFraction`/`IsShown`, `FloatingDamageText.Advance`/`Refresh`, `SwordSlashAttack.Tick`, `GameSaveRecorder.TryGetPlace`, `PlayerController.Tick`/`Orders`, `PlayerMotor2D.FixedTick`/`Follower`, `PlayerAbilities.Configure`/`RecentCastCount`/`TryGetRecentCast`, `MobMotor2D` attack/facing state, `DesiredVelocity` and `UpdateAttackAnimation`, `MobPathAgent2D.Waypoints`, `GridAStarPathfinder2D.LastExpandedCount`, `NavigationGrid2D.AreRegionsLabeled`, and in the editor assembly `TerrainBrushEditor.FollowStroke`/`FollowShift`/`PaintPreview(…, erasing)`/`BoxPreview` (outside IMGUI `Event.current` is null, so the grid brush editor's `OnPaintSceneGUI` cannot run in a test).

### PlayMode (`Assets/Tests/PlayMode`)

| File | Covers |
|---|---|
| [SceneBootTestHelper.cs](../Assets/Tests/PlayMode/SceneBootTestHelper.cs) | Helper: boots through `Main` like a build using `EditorBootRequest`, resolves services from `MainLifetimeScope` or the Gameplay scope (`ResolveFromGameplay`), finds a status in the catalog by name (`FindStatus`), timeout-guarded waits. Every boot registers a `MemorySaveStore` (the test's own, or a fresh one) into Main with `LifetimeScope.Enqueue`, and a fresh one under the `UserSettings.FileName` key, so tests never read or write the real save or settings file. |

## Known gaps and oddities

Factual observations against CLAUDE.md; nothing here has been changed.

- **Inline comments** (CLAUDE.md allows only XML doc summaries): `IClock.cs`, `SceneDefinition.cs`, the editor scene tools (`PlayModeBootstrapper`, `SceneBuildValidator`, `SceneDefinitionEditor`), `VContainerDiagnosticsBuildGuard`, and several test files.
- **`MobConfig` defaults `obstacleLayerMask`** to a hard-coded `1 << 8`. Its create-menu path is `AI/...` while other assets use `TopDownRPG/...` (same for `TerrainType2D`/`TerrainMovementProfile2D`).
- **No pooling for slashes or death effects**: `SlashSpawner` and `EffectSpawner` instantiate, and `SwordSlashAttack`/`MobDeathAnimation` destroy themselves. Only damage popups are pooled.
- **Allocating calls per attack**: `SwordSlashAttack` uses `Physics2D.OverlapBoxAll` and `GetComponentsInChildren<Collider2D>` on every slash.
- **Static mutable state**: `SceneQuery` shares a static root-object buffer. `UnityClock.Shared` is a static used as a fallback clock inside components.
- **Two classes in one file**: `SceneDefinitionEditor.cs` also defines `SceneDefinitionPathSync`.
- **A bridge's end depends on the layers listed between it and the layer under it**: `TBVisible` hides a layer under a bridge directly above it where their edges meet, while a bridge two or more layers up leaves the layer its edge pixel there (`TBNearBridgeEdge`), even when the layers between are empty. Chunks list different materials, so a plank bridge over water ending near a border where one chunk lists the ocean (between water and planks) and the other does not draws up to about 25 pixels differently on either side; `ChunkedGround` gives every grid the same list to keep its comparison to the skirt (WorldStreaming.md's Phase 2 notes).
- **Chunk loads hitch in the editor**: a Sandbox chunk loads in 3 frames, the worst about 18 to 26 ms against a 3 ms frame (the scene's activation; the ground's build is 3.5 ms of it), over a 16.7 ms budget; a player build, which loads scenes in binary, is unmeasured (WorldStreaming.md's Phase 2 notes).
- **Waystones are invisible in game**: a waystone is an entry on its `WorldDefinition`, with no art or marker in its chunk, so a player finds one by its place (Testlands' `north`) and learns of a discovery from its popup only.
- **World exits are invisible in game**: a `WorldExit` is a marker with a scene gizmo and no art (no door, no mat), so the Sandbox's exits are found by their place (the road east of the entry; the interior's bottom row).
- **A trip's arrival is not protected**: game time runs during a trip, so a mob already standing by the entry, or a status ticking, can hurt the player while it is faded out and its input is off (about half a second plus the time the chunks there take to load). A respawn's trip is protected while the player is dead, but the host restores it as soon as it stands at the waystone with the chunk there held, which can come before its machine fades in; a client's player can also show dead for a round trip after its fade in, until the host's restore arrives.
- **The save keeps one session**: there is one save file per install, and New Game (or pressing Play on a world scene in the editor) replaces it at its first save; a host's save holds every player who joined it, and a client keeps no copy of its own place in the host's world.
- **Chunk holds outlive trips**: a client keeps holding a chunk across its trips, so a respawn at a waystone in a chunk the client already held counts that chunk as ready at once; the host still waits for the player to stand at the waystone.
- **Players' places are checked only against chunks**: a saved place on a chunk that was repainted since (now water or a wall) is used as it is; the player can walk off, since paths start from the nearest walkable cell.
- **Every player is visible to every client**: players are never hidden, so a client receives the movement of players in other worlds, thousands of units away and never drawn. Fine for 2 to 4 players.
- **The walls' hover preview across a chunk border is the target chunk's**: the Terrain Brush splits a walls stroke by chunk, but its preview stays the Dual Grid Brush's on the target chunk's module, which draws past its border in the right place but picks its corner sprites there from the neighbour's walls without the preview.
- **A generated chunk keeps layers it no longer needs**: the world generation writer writes a chunk when what it holds changes, not when only a neighbour's border strip changes its list, so a material that leaves a neighbour's strip stays an empty layer of the chunk (texture memory, and a layer towards 16) until the chunk is next written; Sync layer lists only adds layers. And `CreateChunk` lists the grass layer in every chunk's `AreaTerrain`, which is only appended to, so a chunk of open sea draws an empty grass layer beside its ocean.
- **Wave crests stand on end at the Overworld's top edge**: in the open sea within about 12 cells of the world's top edge a few crests run north and south instead of along the waves (at cells x 3, 164, 184, 195, 216 and 234), lined up with the bumps of the Coast region's beach to the south: seams in the baked coast maps far from any shore. They lie 20 cells or more from every walkable cell, past the view (10 cells to either side, 5.6 up and down), so no player sees them; WorldGeneration.md's Phase 4 notes.
- **A sand nub at the Coast region's joins**: where the Coast region's beach meets the world's at either end, a cell or two of sand pokes into the sea, the step of one row the region's outermost columns leave (WorldGeneration.md's Phase 1 notes), seen from the beach in the Overworld.
- **Persistent ids are repaired only for undoable edits**: `PersistentIdWatcher` follows the editor's object change events, which scripts writing scenes without undo do not raise; the World Editor's Repair ids and Validate, and the build, catch what they miss.
- **The coast maps are baked on request**: painting the sea or its shore marks nothing; the World Editor hints that the ground changed and Check coasts lists the stale chunks, and a build does not check them.
- **A world's search limits are code defaults**: `WorldNavigation` uses `NavigationMap2D`'s `MaxSearchCells` (4096) and `NearestCellSearchRadius` (8); no asset sets them, unlike an area grid's inspector fields.
- **The status dev panel finds the Gameplay scope itself** (`FindAnyObjectByType`, cached) instead of being injected, since DevTools types cannot be registered by the Gameplay scope.
- **Unused or test-only API**: `TerrainType2D.TerrainId`; `YPositionSorter.SetSortingOrderOffset`/`SetSortingReferenceY`/`ClearSortingReferenceY`; `ScreenFader.IsOpaque`; `PlayerWeaponController.CurrentWeaponName` (tests only); `AbilityService.CastApplied`/`CastResolved` (tests only); `PlayerAbilities.CastEnded` (no listener yet); `PlayerClass.DisplayName` (nothing shows the class); `MobController.onAttackRangeEntered` has no listeners on the Weasel prefab; `AreaTerrain.TrySetCells` (tests and the editor's dev menu only: nothing in gameplay edits terrain yet); `SessionSave.PlayerCount`/`WorldCount`, `WorldState.ChunkCount` and `ChunkState.DeadMobCount` (tests only).
- **Duplicated tuning**: `nearestCellSearchRadius` exists on both `NavigationGrid2D` (used when no radius is passed, e.g. inside A*) and `MobConfig`. `MobMotor2D` speed/acceleration, `MeleeDamageDealer` damage/interval and `SwordSlashAttack.damageAmount` are serialized but overwritten at runtime by `MobConfig`/`PlayerWeapon`.
- **Magic numbers**: `MobDeathAnimation` scales the effect by a hard-coded `1.2`.
- **TMP's dynamic fallback font changes at runtime**: a glyph missing from the static `LiberationSans SDF` atlas is added to `LiberationSans SDF - Fallback`, which is package behaviour; in the editor that dirties the fallback asset. `Clear Dynamic Data On Build` empties it again on builds and when the editor closes, so the fallback is committed in that empty state. Keep UI text inside the static atlas's character set; if the fallback shows a diff with glyphs in it, find the text that used a character outside the atlas.
- **Editor asset writes on load**: `PlayerSlashPrefabBootstrap` can regenerate `SwordSlash.prefab` and rewrite `Sword.asset` from an `[InitializeOnLoad]` delay call.
- **Unit picks ignore the ability's filter**: `PointerTargetPicker` returns the closest living unit of either team, so an ally-only cast on an ally overlapping an enemy that is closer to the cursor fails with `NoTarget`.
- **Status icons are placeholders**: no status has an icon sprite, so the HUD shows two-letter abbreviations on coloured squares and the world icons are plain coloured squares; every visual is the same tinted ring.
- **A world's edge has no walls**: where a world's ground stops (Testlands' and the Sandbox's outer border), nothing physical stops a body; paths keep players and mobs inside, and the default profile's edge cost keeps routes a cell in from the edge where there is room.
- **Dual-grid leftovers from upstream**: `DualGridRuleTile`'s create-menu path is `Scriptable Objects/DualGridRuleTile` rather than `TopDownRPG/...`. `DualGridRuleTile.DataTile` logs an error and throws when the data tile sub-asset is missing.
- **Units sort by their sprite's centre, not their feet**: the player's and the Weasel's `YPositionSorter` sit on their `Visuals`, at the centre of their sprites: 0.5 above the feet for the player, 0.72 for the Weasel. Props put theirs 0.5 above their base to match the player, so a Weasel standing level with a prop's base sorts as if 0.22 further back.
- **Saved terrain edits name layers by index**: a chunk's saved edits name its `AreaTerrain` layers by index, so re-authoring a chunk must only append layers. Path followers only react to edits that block their path; an edit that opens a shorter route is picked up at the owner's next repath.
- **The camera does not snap to the pixel grid**: `CameraFollow2D.snapToPixelGrid` is off in `Main`, as are the Pixel Perfect Camera's grid and pixel snapping. The camera moves by fractions of an art pixel, so 32 px art, the blended ground included, changes shape for a frame whenever the camera crosses the half-pixel phase of the art grid. Turning the snap on (tried in terrain blending Phase 1) makes the moving player shake: the camera then jumps by whole art pixels while the interpolated player moves smoothly, so the player wobbles by up to about 2.7 screen pixels against the screen. Snapping needs the sprites snapped too, or a camera that snaps only once it settles.
- **Saving after touching a prop's renderer writes its sorting order**: `YPositionSorter` sets each prop's sorting order in edit mode without recording it, so the scene file has none, but once anything marks a prop's renderer changed (`forceRenderingOff`, a script setter), the next save writes that prop's order as a prefab override. `TerrainBlendProbe` clears the mark it leaves; other tools should too, or reopen the scene before saving.
- **The soft terrain look compiles on first use in the editor**: its shader variant compiles in the background the first time a renderer switches to soft in a session, so the ground draws nothing for a few frames. `TerrainBlendProbe` compiles variants synchronously, so its checks are unaffected.
- **A flood fill on a ground layer shows no preview**: the Terrain Brush keeps the Dual Grid Brush's flood fill, which from an empty cell outside a shape fills up to the tilemap's bounds, and previews nothing there (see TerrainBlending.md's Phase 3 notes). Undo it if it filled more than meant.
- **A ground tile is found by its material's name**: `TerrainTileWriter` names the tile and the swatch after the material, and only serves `Assets/Settings/Terrain/Materials`, so after a material is renamed, Write Ground Tiles makes a new tile and swatch, while the palette keeps the old tile and Generate no longer refreshes its swatch. Rename the tile and swatch along with the material.
- **The renderer does not check the shared layer order**: `TerrainBlendRenderer` draws its layers as listed, so layers reordered by hand in its inspector, or a material missing from `TerrainStyle.LayerOrder`, draw pairs the other way up from other areas. Only the scene generator's writer and Convert Ground enforce the order; running Convert Ground puts an area back in it.
- **A scratch scene's `AreaTerrain` lists the grass layer**: generating a layout whose ground is not grass (the Snowfield's snow, the Lava Fields' dirt) into a scratch scene keeps its empty grass layer, since `AreaTerrain` lists it, and warns, until that entry is pointed at the generator's ground layer.
- **One layer with waves per area**: a renderer builds its coast map for its first layer whose material has waves, so a second such layer would roll its waves toward the first one's coast. Each build of the coast map after the coast moves takes about 70 ms in the editor (the Coast, debug code), so painting the ocean with the brush stutters; painting anything else only compares the cells. The ocean's recipe still writes its crests' glint phases, which its material no longer lights (its glint duty is 0).
- **Water blocks only through navigation**: it has no collider, so nothing physical stops a body there, and neither do the ocean, lava and the cliff of raised ground. Bodies move only along paths today, which go around them; a knockback or a dash would need a collider on a layer that does not block line of sight.
- **Lava only blocks**: it does no damage yet; standing in or next to it burning a unit is planned in DamageAndStatusEffects.md (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 9).
- **A layer that turns to its runs picks a way per cell**: the planks turn their boards a quarter in a cell where the layer runs further left and right than up and down, counting 3 cells each way, so at a junction, in a wide platform or at the end of a boardwalk wider than 3 cells the boards may lie either way, one cell at a time, and a painter cannot choose (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 5 notes).
- **A layer beside a lower one of another look shows it under its corners**: each layer's shape counts the cells of every layer above it, since lower layers are meant to go on under upper ones (a bridge, the planks, excepted: the layers under it count its cells only where they are painted there). Where an upper layer lies beside a lower one instead (mud or a road next to dry grass, with only grass under them), the lower one shows under the upper one's rounded corners where the two meet a third material: a notch in crisp, a spike in soft. Paint the lower layer under the upper one where they meet, or keep a cell between them, as the Lakeside's dry grass does from mud and paths, and its shallow ponds from every other look (TerrainTypes.md's Phase 3 and Phase 4 notes). The map's edge counts as the third material: where bands that lie over each other run out at it, as the Coast's beach and ocean do at its side edges, the lower band shows under the upper one's rounded corner there, a notch of a pixel or two, and draws its own edge over nothing up to it (the beach's outline along the map's edge).
- **Diagonal paths step from cell to cell**: the path layers are filled cell by cell, so a road that wanders north or south climbs in cell steps. The crisp look's corner rounding turns the steps into a wavy band, but a diagonal never runs straight there (the soft look draws straight diagonals). The Clearing generator's `roadMeander` trades variety against steps.
- **No brush preview on raised ground**: the Terrain Brush paints `Tile_Raised` and `Tile_Ramp` from `Raised_Palette` on an area's `RaisedTilemap` like any plain tile, but the renderer previews only ground layers, so a stroke shows once painted. Raised ground has one level: a plateau cannot stand on another.
- **Walls leave a gap at the ground's corners**: where a wall runs along the ground's edge, as around the stress world's border, the grass's rounded outer corner (about 14 px) and the walls' fillet (6.2 px) leave a few pixels without ground at each inner corner, where the camera's background shows. The 64 px tiles left the same gap.
- **Folder naming**: `YPositionSorter` lives under `Assets/Scripts/Camera` although it handles sprite sorting; the camera follow script is in `Core/Camera`.
